    \item \textbf{Regime IV (\(\tau > \tau_{+} \approx 132.4\) yr):} Destabilising Hopf at $\tau_+$ removes the stable equilibrium; the large-amplitude cycle (already present since $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$) is the sole interior attractor.
\end{itemize}
The two folds thus sit just \emph{outside} the two Hopf points, in the order $\tau_{-}<\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}<\tau_{+}$ — the standard topology for a pair of subcritical Hopf bifurcations, each shadowed by a nearby fold of periodic orbits, rather than the single intervening fold ($\tau_-<\tau_{\text{SNPO}}<\tau_+$) a purely local reading of the eigenvalues might suggest. $\tau_-\approx6.881$ and $\tau_+\approx132.375$ follow from the characteristic equation (unchanged from a purely linear analysis, since Hopf points are intrinsically local objects), and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}\approx7.355$ and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx131.24$ follow from the continuation-based bisection above, each confirmed to five significant figures by bracketing to a window of $\pm 0.002$\,yr or better under repeated independent runs. The net monostable interval, $(\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}},\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}})\approx(7.36,131.24)$\,yr, is wider than a single-fold picture with $(\tau_-,\tau_{\text{SNPO}})\approx(6.88,109.6)$\,yr would suggest, because the apparent upper boundary in such a picture is a critical-slowing-down artifact rather than the true fold location, not because the lower boundary is more permissive (it is very slightly less permissive, since $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}>\tau_-$).

\paragraph{Which histories fall into the cycle: a finding about basin structure, not about the liquidation mechanism.}
\label{sec:basin-capture}
The liquidation mechanism of the Introduction and Section~\ref{sec:core-model-eqs} is unaffected by the correction: extraction that persistently exceeds sustainable yield still liquidates principal and still amplifies the deficit, exactly as the hen metaphor describes, in every regime discussed above. The finding here is narrower and additional: \emph{within} either bistable window, which historical trajectories are ultimately captured by the large-amplitude cycle versus settling at the quiet equilibrium. In our simulations, a resource history sitting at a \emph{large} stock with correspondingly low effort is the one captured by the sustained boom-bust cycle once the institutional delay is long enough to be inside the upper bistable window (or short enough to be inside the lower one), while a history sitting at a low, near-collapse stock instead recovers to the quiet equilibrium whenever the equilibrium itself is locally stable. The interpretation is that a large stock permits effort to grow with little resistance, and a long institutional delay then prevents that effort from down-regulating quickly enough once the deficit signal reverses, so the system overshoots into the cycle. This is an additional delay-driven phenomenon alongside the liquidation mechanism, not a replacement for it, discussed further in Sections~\ref{sec:discussion-liquidation} and~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}. Because both bistable windows identified above are narrow ($\lesssim1.1$\,yr wide at Candidate A parameters), this basin-dependent capture is a comparatively minor, edge-of-window phenomenon at these parameters; the more consequential practical distinction remains which side of the wide monostable range (Regime I versus the monostable interval, or the monostable interval versus Regime IV) a given $\tau$ falls on. As a limitation, this basin-structure finding is specific to the original ungated core's parameterisation and should be treated as provisional pending independent replication with dedicated DDE bifurcation-continuation software (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}).

\subsection{An Exact Algebraic Reduction: A Cubic Governing All Local Hopf Crossings}
\label{sec:exact-cubic}
The two Hopf points $\tau_-,\tau_+$ located above were found by numerical root-tracking of the rightmost eigenvalue of Eq.~\eqref{eq:char-eq-corrected}. This is reliable (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} cross-checks every crossing against direct simulation) but leaves one question unanswered by construction: could a numerical sweep over a bounded region of $\tau$ have missed a crossing lying between grid points, or beyond the range searched? This subsection closes that gap with a closed-form reduction of the same characteristic equation to a single real cubic, whose positive roots give \emph{every} admissible Hopf frequency, with no search or grid involved.

\paragraph{Exact factorisation of the characteristic quasi-polynomial.} Writing $d=1/\tau_m$, Eq.~\eqref{eq:char-eq-corrected}'s determinant expands, by direct symbolic computation (not asserted), to exactly
\begin{equation}
P(\lambda) - C_Z\,L(\lambda)\,e^{-\lambda\tau} = 0, \qquad
P(\lambda) = (\lambda-A_N)(\lambda+d)(\lambda-C_E), \qquad
L(\lambda) = B_E(\lambda-A_N) + A_E B_N,
\label{eq:PL-factorisation}
\end{equation}
confirmed to be an exact algebraic identity with the 3$\times$3 determinant of Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} (zero symbolic residual). For a Hopf root $\lambda=i\omega$, $\omega>0$, the equation $P(i\omega)=C_Z L(i\omega)e^{-i\omega\tau}$ requires $|P(i\omega)|=C_Z|L(i\omega)|$; writing $x=\omega^2$, both moduli reduce, again by direct expansion (not approximation), to
\begin{equation}
|P(i\omega)|^2 = (x+A_N^2)(x+d^2)(x+C_E^2), \qquad
|L(i\omega)|^2 = B_E^2\,x + (A_E B_N - A_N B_E)^2,
\label{eq:modulus-formulas}
\end{equation}
so that the Hopf modulus condition becomes the single real cubic in $x$
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\,H(x) = (x+A_N^2)(x+d^2)(x+C_E^2) - C_Z^2\bigl[B_E^2 x + (A_E B_N - A_N B_E)^2\bigr] = 0.\,}
\label{eq:hopf-cubic}
\end{equation}
Because $H$ is a genuine cubic in $x$, it has at most three real roots; only its \emph{positive} real roots correspond to genuine Hopf frequencies ($\omega=\sqrt{x}$), so \textbf{this system can have at most three distinct Hopf-frequency families for any parameter choice} — a completeness bound no finite numerical sweep can certify by itself. For each positive root $x_n$ (giving $\omega_n=\sqrt{x_n}$), the corresponding delays follow from the argument of Eq.~\eqref{eq:PL-factorisation}'s ratio:
\begin{equation}
\tau_{n,k}(\omega_n) = \frac{-\arg\!\bigl(P(i\omega_n)/[C_Z L(i\omega_n)]\bigr) + 2\pi k}{\omega_n}, \qquad k\in\mathbb Z,\ \tau_{n,k}>0.
\label{eq:hopf-tau-formula}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Verification against both published candidates.} Solving Eq.~\eqref{eq:hopf-cubic}--\eqref{eq:hopf-tau-formula} independently (symbolic cubic construction, then numeric root-finding, with no eigenvalue tracking anywhere in the pipeline) exactly reproduces the published thresholds: for Candidate A, $H(x)$ has exactly two positive roots, giving $\tau_-=6.8814$ and $\tau_+=132.3749$ (published: $6.88$, $132.4$); for Candidate B, exactly two positive roots give $\tau_-=6.2136$ and $\tau_+=76.2906$ (published: $6.214$/$6.21$, $76.29$), each computed to 10+ significant figures beyond the reported precision, so that each candidate's Hopf spectrum consists of exactly two frequency families, whose $k\ge1$ higher branches recur at $\tau_{n,k}=\tau_{n,0}+2\pi k/\omega_n$ (e.g. at $\tau\approx269.3$ and $\approx274.6$\,yr for Candidate A); these are crossings of the same two families, not new frequencies. Sweeping $\eta$ across the full literature-anchored range $[0.5,3.0]$ (Table~\ref{tab:params}, all other parameters fixed at Candidate A's values) confirms $H(x)$ has exactly 0 or 2 positive roots throughout — never 1 or 3 — consistently with (and now algebraically certifying, rather than merely numerically observing) the two-Hopf-point picture used throughout Sections~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}--\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}. This algebraically strengthens the evidentiary basis for that picture: the numerical root-tracking of Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} could not, by itself, rule out an undetected third crossing outside its search grid; the cubic can, because a cubic has at most three real roots as a matter of algebra, not of search coverage.

\paragraph{Scope of this result.} Eq.~\eqref{eq:hopf-cubic} certifies the \emph{existence and count} of local Hopf crossings exactly; it does not by itself establish which crossings are supercritical versus subcritical (that requires a first-Lyapunov-coefficient computation, e.g.\ via the Hassard--Faria normal-form reduction, not attempted here), nor does it locate the global folds $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}},\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$, which remain continuation-based results (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}) outside the reach of a purely local linearisation. It is retained as a permanent completeness check on the local Hopf structure specifically, alongside (not replacing) the root-tracking and simulation-based confirmation already used throughout this manuscript.

\subsection{A Corrected Effort-Saturation Term, and Its Effect on the Hopf Thresholds}
\label{sec:effort-saturation-fix}
The effort equation (\ref{eq:effort-core}) enforces $E\ge0$ by construction (the panic term $\delta_0 Z/(Z_{\text{ref}}+Z)$ is non-negative, and the $-\eta E^2/E_{\max}$ term vanishes at $E=0$), but does \emph{not} enforce $E\le E_{\max}$: the self-saturation term is asymptotic rather than a hard boundary, and nothing in the equation as stated prevents $E$ from exceeding $E_{\max}$ once $Z(t-\tau)$ is driven large enough by a sufficiently deep excursion. Direct simulation confirms this is not merely a theoretical edge case: inside the upper bistable window (Candidate A, $\tau=131.8$ years, the same trajectory used in Fig.~\ref{fig:timeseries} to demonstrate basin-dependent bistability), $E(t)$ is confirmed to exceed $E_{\max}=30$ by more than a factor of three during the large-amplitude cycle's peak, recurring periodically rather than as a one-off transient. This violates the stated physical constraint $E(t)\in[0,E_{\max}]$ (Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}) and is a genuine gap, not a restatement of the already-flagged SNPO or quasi-periodicity uncertainties.

We repair this the same way non-negativity is enforced for $N$ (Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected}): by making the boundary an algebraic consequence of the equation's structure rather than a numerical inequality between independently-calibrated constants. Multiplying the entire right-hand side by $(1-E/E_{\max})$,
\begin{equation}
\dot{E} = \Bigl(1-\frac{E}{E_{\max}}\Bigr)\left[\eta E \left( \frac{Z(t-\tau)}{\Delta_{\text{ref}}} - \frac{E}{E_{\max}} \right) + \delta_0 \frac{Z(t-\tau)}{Z_{\text{ref}} + Z(t-\tau)}\right],
\label{eq:effort-core-corrected}
\end{equation}
guarantees $\dot E=0$ at $E=E_{\max}$ for \emph{any} $Z(t-\tau)\ge0$, so the boundary cannot be crossed from below, exactly mirroring how $qEN\to0$ as $N\to0$ already protects the stock equation's boundary. This is a relabelling of the same self-saturation mechanism, not a new one: at $E\ll E_{\max}$, the multiplying factor is close to $1$ and Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} reduces to the original equation, so the correction acts only in the regime that was previously unprotected.

\paragraph{The interior equilibrium is unchanged; the linearisation is not.} Because $(1-E/E_{\max})>0$ away from the boundary, the equilibrium condition $\dot E=0$ still reduces, at any interior $E^*\in(0,E_{\max})$, to exactly the same bracketed condition as the original equation; the closed-form quadratic for $E^*$ (Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core}) and the values $N^*,Z^*$ are therefore \textbf{completely unchanged} by this correction, confirmed by direct symbolic substitution. The Jacobian coefficients $C_E,C_Z$ that enter the characteristic equation (\ref{eq:char-eq-corrected}), however, each acquire an exact multiplicative factor $(1-E^*/E_{\max})$ relative to their original values (confirmed symbolically): because the equilibrium condition already forces the bracketed term to vanish at $E^*$, differentiating the product rule leaves only the term where the derivative falls on the bracket, scaled by $(1-E^*/E_{\max})$ evaluated at the unperturbed equilibrium. For Candidate A, this factor is $\approx0.930$; for Candidate B, $\approx0.931$.

\paragraph{Relocated Hopf thresholds.} Re-solving the corrected characteristic equation for purely imaginary roots, using the same direct numerical root-tracking methodology as Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} (validated by first reproducing the uncorrected equation's published thresholds to residuals below $10^{-18}$), gives:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Candidate A:} $\tau_-\approx3.67$ years (from $6.881$), $\tau_+\approx150.36$ years (from $132.375$).
\item \textbf{Candidate B:} $\tau_-\approx5.5128$ years (from $6.214$), $\tau_+\approx80.4245$ years (from $76.29$), each verified by direct characteristic-equation root-tracking.
\end{itemize}
Both relocated $\tau_-$ values remain inside the empirically documented range of institutional response lags \cite{hocherman2025} (2--13 years spanning fishery reform to the Montreal Protocol), so the qualitative headline claim of Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected} --- that $\tau_-$ is the threshold whose magnitude is numerically comparable to documented governance lags, with no case-level validation yet (Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}) --- survives the correction, though its precise numerical value shifts non-uniformly between the two candidates (a $-46.7\%$ shift for Candidate A against $-11.3\%$ for Candidate B), rather than by a common factor, because the $(1-E^*/E_{\max})$ correction interacts with each candidate's distinct combination of $\eta$ and $E_{\max}$.

\paragraph{Subcriticality of the corrected core's Hopf points.} The two Hopf points of Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} are both subcritical. The linear growth rate $\sigma(\tau)=\operatorname{Re}\lambda_{\text{rightmost}}(\tau)$ of the interior equilibrium, from the exact characteristic equation of the linearisation, is linear in $\tau$ near $\tau_-$ with $d\sigma/d\tau=-9.4\times10^{-5}$\,yr$^{-2}$ (the equilibrium is stable for $\tau>\tau_-$, unstable for $\tau<\tau_-$). The small-amplitude unstable periodic branch of Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected} grows from the Hopf point as $a^2\approx\kappa(\tau-\tau_-)$ with $\kappa\approx24$ (a separate fit of the same amplitude law, using peak-to-peak amplitude, gives slope $29.8$; Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}) and fitted exponent $0.47$ (Hopf theory: $1/2$), where $a=N_{\max}-N_{\min}$ is the cycle's peak-to-peak amplitude. With the amplitude equation $da/dt=\sigma a + l_1 a^3$, an inferred first-Lyapunov-style coefficient (no Hassard--Faria normal-form computation was attempted; this is a fit-based estimate, not a computed normal-form coefficient) is $l_1=-\sigma/a^2=-(d\sigma/d\tau)/\kappa\approx+3.9\times10^{-6}$ (in the peak-to-peak amplitude convention; $+1.6\times10^{-5}$ in the half-amplitude convention). Its sign is invariant to the convention: $l_1>0$, i.e.\ consistent with subcriticality. Direct simulation confirms the defining dynamical signature: near-equilibrium histories just below $\tau_-$ ($\tau=3.5,3.55,3.6$\,yr) grow, without saturating at any intermediate amplitude, to the large-amplitude cycle (amplitude $\approx57$), so no small stable cycle exists there; the unstable small cycle exists on the stable side $\tau\in(\tau_-,\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}})$ and terminates at its own fold $\tau\approx5.587$ without meeting the stable large-amplitude branch, which persists past $\tau=5.5815$ (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}). At $\tau_+\approx150.36$, $d\sigma/d\tau=+5.5\times10^{-6}$\,yr$^{-2}$ --- destabilisation an order of magnitude slower than at $\tau_-$ (severe critical slowing down: a run of $10^6$\,yr is needed to see a near-equilibrium perturbation grow) --- and near-equilibrium histories just above $\tau_+$ likewise grow, without intermediate saturation, to the large cycle (amplitude $\approx43$ by $\tau=152$), so $\tau_+$ is also subcritical, consistent with the upper bistable window $(\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}},\tau_+)=(148.3,150.36)$ and the fold at $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx148.3$. Both Hopf points therefore conform to the subcritical-with-fold structure used throughout this paper, with the lower boundary realised as a tight pair of individually-genuine folds rather than a single SNPO collision, with the first Lyapunov coefficient quantified at $\tau_-$; these computations are verified in the accompanying code (Section~\ref{sec:data-code}).

\paragraph{Relocated fold thresholds under the corrected equation, and a qualitatively different bistability structure (Candidate A).} Because the amplitude-discontinuity fold is a genuinely global feature (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}), it cannot be relocated by root-tracking on the characteristic equation the way the Hopf points above were; we instead traced the large-amplitude periodic orbit of Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} directly via Fourier spectral collocation and natural-parameter continuation in $\tau$, and independently cross-checked every candidate threshold by direct long-horizon ($2\times10^4$--$6\times10^4$ year) perturbation tests of the resulting orbit, seeded from its own relaxed history rather than from a fixed constant initial condition (which the basin-structure finding above shows can give a false negative near a fold). The continuation also required addressing a solver artifact: an even number of collocation points admits a spurious checkerboard (Nyquist) mode that is invisible to the discretised residual (the standard even-point Fourier differentiation matrix annihilates it) and can contaminate an apparently well-converged solution; switching to an odd number of collocation points, which has no exact Nyquist frequency, eliminates this failure mode, and only orbits verified free of it (checked via the discrete Fourier transform of the solution) are reported here.

For Candidate A, this procedure locates two amplitude-discontinuity thresholds, with the lower one at $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}\gtrsim5.58$ (the stable branch persists through $\tau=5.5815$ in the collocation, residual $\sim10^{-12}$; the fixed-step persistence bracket $[5.56999,5.57001]$ at $10^6$-year horizons carries a first-order-in-step bias and is superseded, Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}) and the upper one at $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\in[148.125,148.438]$ years, together with a structural finding that recasts the bistability picture relative to the original equation. Each corrected fold sits close to its corresponding Hopf point: the lower fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}\gtrsim5.58$ lies $\approx1.9$\,yr above $\tau_-\approx3.67$, and the upper fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx148.3$ lies $\approx2$\,yr below $\tau_+\approx150.36$. Direct simulation from multiple far-from-equilibrium initial conditions (four independent seeds at each of $\tau=10,20,30,40$ years, and eight seeds at each of $\tau=64.39,\dots,140$, Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}) finds \emph{no} large-amplitude attractor anywhere in the wide interior range $(\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}},\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}})\approx(\gtrsim5.58,148.3)$ years, so this range is genuinely monostable; the large-amplitude cycle coexists with the locally stable equilibrium only in the two flanking windows $(\tau_-,\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}})\approx(3.67,\gtrsim5.58)$ and $(\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}},\tau_+)\approx(148.3,150.36)$ years. The qualitative sequence --- bistable, then monostable-safe, then bistable --- is preserved from the original equation, with both windows narrow ($\approx2$\,yr each, comparable to the original's sub-year to $1.1$-year windows); the correction relocates the windows along the $\tau$-axis (the lower fold from $7.36$ to $\gtrsim5.58$; the upper fold from $131.24$ to $148.3$) without changing their width, and the safe monostable range remains the overwhelming majority of the $\tau$-axis.

\paragraph{An alternative effort law with strictly inward boundaries was considered and rejected.} An alternative multiplicative form, $\dot E=\eta E(1-E/E_{\max})Z(t-\tau)/\Delta_{\text{ref}}+\delta_0(1-E/E_{\max})-\mu_EE$, offers a genuine boundary-behaviour improvement over Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected}: both boundaries point strictly inward ($\dot E|_{E=0}=\delta_0>0$, $\dot E|_{E=E_{\max}}=-\mu_EE_{\max}<0$, confirmed symbolically), rather than Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected}'s $E=E_{\max}$ boundary being only weakly invariant ($\dot E=0$ there). This alternative retains a closed-form quadratic equilibrium (same tractability class as Eq.~\eqref{eq:Estar-quadratic}), so it was evaluated as a candidate replacement. It is rejected: an extensive parameter search (a joint grid together with a 3000-point random log-uniform search over $\eta,\mu_E,E_{\max}$) finds \emph{no} combination giving both an interior equilibrium at a plausible working stock level (comparable to Candidates A/B, $N^*$ in the interior of $(0,K)$ rather than near either extreme) and a genuine Hopf bifurcation for any $\tau>0$. The root cause, confirmed symbolically: this law's baseline-panic term $\delta_0(1-E/E_{\max})$ has no dependence on $Z(t-\tau)$, so --- unlike $\delta_0Z(t-\tau)/(Z_{\text{ref}}+Z(t-\tau))$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core} and Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} --- it contributes nothing to the institutional feedback gain $C_Z$; the entire gain then comes from the $\eta$-panic term alone, $C_Z=\eta E^*(E_{\max}-E^*)/(\Delta_{\text{ref}}E_{\max})$, which is too weak at every tau-independent-stable equilibrium tested to satisfy the Hopf gain condition of Eq.~\eqref{eq:general-feedback} for any admissible frequency. Adopting this law as proposed would therefore silence the delay-induced instability this manuscript's entire argument is built on, at any comparable working equilibrium. This evaluation, together with the search and the symbolic root-cause diagnosis, is verified in the accompanying code (Section~\ref{sec:data-code}); Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} is accordingly retained as this manuscript's boundary-corrected effort law.

\paragraph{A repaired, additive variant restores the delay-dependent gain while keeping strictly inward boundaries.} The root-cause diagnosis above is constructive: it identifies exactly which structural feature to preserve. Rather than restructuring the entire bracket (as the rejected law did), adding a single term \emph{outside} the existing multiplicative gate of Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} repairs the boundary without touching the institutional gain channel:
\begin{equation}
\dot{E} = \Bigl(1-\frac{E}{E_{\max}}\Bigr)\left[\eta E \left( \frac{Z(t-\tau)}{\Delta_{\text{ref}}} - \frac{E}{E_{\max}} \right) + \delta_0 \frac{Z(t-\tau)}{Z_{\text{ref}} + Z(t-\tau)}\right] - \mu_EE,
\label{eq:effort-core-hybrid}
\end{equation}
identical to Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} except for the additive $-\mu_EE$ term. This changes the boundary at $E=E_{\max}$ from weakly invariant ($\dot E=0$ there, Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected}) to strictly inward ($\dot E=-\mu_EE_{\max}<0$, confirmed symbolically), while leaving the bracket --- and therefore the institutional gain $C_Z$'s dependence on $Z(t-\tau)$ through $\delta_0Z(t-\tau)/(Z_{\text{ref}}+Z(t-\tau))$, the exact channel the rejected law lost --- completely untouched. The equilibrium condition becomes a cubic in $E^*$ (one degree above Eq.~\eqref{eq:Estar-quadratic}, since $-\mu_EE$ breaks the bracket's own factorisation) but remains closed-form; at a small $\mu_E=10^{-4}$ it gives $E^*=2.086113$ for Candidate A and $E^*=1.804558$ for Candidate B, each within $0.005$ of the published equilibria ($2.089623$ and $1.805570$ respectively). Both the gain/phase characteristic-equation method and independent direct nonlinear DDE simulation (near-equilibrium initial conditions, no linearisation) confirm a genuine Hopf pair persists at this equilibrium for both candidates, with $\tau_-\approx3.60$ (Candidate A) and $\tau_-\approx5.51$ (Candidate B), each close to the un-hybridised law's own values; direct simulation additionally confirms $E(t)$ never exceeds $E_{\max}$ or falls below zero at any tested $\tau$, including throughout the large-amplitude cycle, and that starting exactly at $E=E_{\max}$ moves strictly inward within a single integration step. Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-hybrid} is offered as an additive, further-improved variant --- not a replacement for Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected}, which remains this manuscript's primary boundary-corrected law and the basis for every quantitative result in Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected} --- since $\mu_E$ is a new parameter with no independent empirical calibration. Repeating the fold analysis of Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} for this law (Candidate A, $\mu_E=10^{-4}$): the large-amplitude cycle persists in a narrow lower window $(\tau_-,\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}})=(3.60,5.53)$ (lower fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}\approx5.53$, versus the corrected core's $\approx5.574$), is absent across the wide middle (all test histories decay at $\tau=77$), and reappears in an upper window with $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx143.5$ (versus $\approx148.3$) and $\tau_+=150.76$, the equilibrium being locally stable throughout $\tau_-<\tau\lesssim\tau_+$ by the characteristic-equation root count --- the same qualitative two-window structure as the corrected core, with the upper window shifted downward by roughly 5--8 yr. A basin-structure caveat, applicable to the corrected core as well: immediately below the upper fold the large cycle's basin extends into the nominal monostable interval --- histories beginning with equilibrium-level deficit memory ($Z_0\approx\delta$) or above-carrying-capacity stock are captured by the large cycle at $\tau$ values (e.g., $\tau=140$) at which the lower-$Z_0$ test histories used elsewhere decay --- so the monostable interval's monostability is basin-dependent near the fold boundary, consistent with the critical-slowing-down caution of Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}.

Three scope conditions delimit this finding. First, it is established for Candidate A only: the hybrid variant's Candidate B thresholds have not been computed (for the corrected law itself, Candidate B's Hopf thresholds are reported in Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix} and Table~\ref{tab:threshold-registry} as $\tau_-\approx5.5128$~yr and $\tau_+\approx80.4245$~yr, while its fold thresholds under the corrected law remain to be located, Table~\ref{tab:threshold-registry} footnote). Second, the fold locations are established by direct perturbation-decay testing of orbits at long but finite horizons rather than by a proof; as for the original equation's folds (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}), a still-longer horizon could in principle reveal a further, undetected transient regime. Third, this analysis establishes where the large-amplitude attractor gains and loses stability, but not how: whether each transition is a genuine Saddle-Node of Periodic Orbits (an unstable limit cycle merging with and annihilating the stable one) or some other bifurcation (e.g., a period-doubling or Neimark--Sacker/torus bifurcation of the same orbit, which would leave the orbit mathematically existing but destabilised without a collision) has not been determined. Fourier spectral collocation of the effort-saturation-corrected core, with Newton/Levenberg--Marquardt correction at each continuation step, traces the small-amplitude unstable orbit outward from the Hopf point at $\tau_-$. With the collocation's $\max(0,\cdot)$ floor represented at high sharpness (essentially exact), the amplitude grows smoothly and continuously from $1.051$ at $\tau=3.700$ through $\tau=5.468$ (amplitude $\approx15.5$) to $\approx21.6$ at $\tau=5.586$, where natural-parameter continuation stalls at a genuine turning point: the branch's fold is at $\tau\approx5.587$, amplitude $\approx21.7$. The branch's $\max(0,\cdot)$ floor is inactive throughout (the softplus argument never reaches the floor; the baseline shift is $\delta=\ln 2/k$, so the floored quantity is the softplus itself, which is nonnegative identically), and with a robust corrector the branch is monotone through $\tau=5.468$. The fold at $\tau\approx5.587$ is confirmed by a shooting Floquet computation of the collocation-converged orbit: a shooting Floquet computation of the collocation-converged orbit shows a \emph{real} Floquet multiplier of $1.0514$ at $\tau=5.584$ (unstable orbit) decreasing to $0.998983$ at $\tau=5.587$ --- a real multiplier crossing $+1$, the defining signature of a fold of periodic orbits. The small-amplitude unstable orbit therefore does \emph{not} collide with the stable large-amplitude branch: careful continuation of both branches shows two nearby but distinct folds. The stable large-amplitude cycle (amplitude $29.6$ at $\tau=5.50$, $25.0$ at $\tau=5.574$) persists through $\tau=5.5815$ in the collocation continuation, converged to residual $\sim10^{-12}$ at every recorded point (Section~\ref{sec:data-code}); the earlier estimate that it terminates between $\tau=5.574$ and $\tau=5.576$, based on a $12\times10^6$-year fixed-step simulation at $\tau=5.576$ collapsing to the equilibrium, is superseded by the collocation data, since a fixed-step persistence test carries the same first-order-in-step bias quantified for the four-state core (Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}) and is additionally exposed to basin-shrinkage effects near a weakly-attracting near-fold region. The stable branch's fold therefore lies above $\tau=5.5815$. The small-amplitude unstable branch has amplitude $\approx20.5$ at the stable branch's last recorded point, not $\approx25$, so the two branches do not meet: the small orbit folds separately at $\tau\approx5.587$ (amplitude $\approx21.7$). The lower boundary of the bistable window is therefore a tight pair of two nearby, \emph{each individually genuine}, folds of periodic orbits --- the stable branch's fold above $\tau=5.5815$ and the small-unstable branch's fold at $\tau\approx5.587$ --- rather than a single fold at which stable and unstable periodic branches of one family collide. Each fold carries its own $+1$ Floquet-multiplier signature: for the stable branch, the variational collocation records a \emph{real} dominant multiplier at every point (imaginary part identically zero in \texttt{snpo\_lower\_fold\_variational.csv}, \texttt{snpo\_lower\_fold\_tip.csv}, \texttt{snpo\_lower\_fold\_push.csv}), rising monotonically from $0.2396$ at $\tau=4.0$ through $0.9473$ at $\tau=5.574$ to $0.9644$ at $\tau=5.5815$ toward $+1$ --- the standard real-multiplier signature of a fold of periodic orbits (not a complex pair); for the small branch, a real multiplier crosses $+1$ at $\tau\approx5.587$ (values $1.0514$ at $5.584$, $0.998983$ at $5.587$). The two folds belong to different periodic-orbit families (periods $\approx322.9$ and $\approx314.3$\,yr at their respective folds). A fit of the amplitude gap between the two branches against $\sqrt{\tau^*-\tau}$ yields an exponent $\approx0.46$ over the range where both exist, but because the two branches terminate at different $\tau$ values, the characteristic $\sqrt{\tau-\tau_{\text{SNPO}}}$ collision scaling of a single saddle-node of periodic orbits is not cleanly demonstrable; the classification of the lower-boundary transition accordingly remains unresolved (a pair of nearby folds, a period-doubling or torus-mediated transition, or another global bifurcation), and we retain the term ``fold''/``amplitude-discontinuity threshold'' for $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ without asserting the SNPO classification as proven. These computations are verified in the accompanying code (Section~\ref{sec:data-code}).

\paragraph{Upper fold of the corrected core.} Direct integration of Eqs.~\eqref{eq:stock-core}--\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} (fixed-step RK4 with a circular delay buffer) independently reproduces the local thresholds $\tau_-=3.666$ and $\tau_+\approx150.36$ (both $k$-independent, since $\operatorname{softplus}_k'(0)=1/2$), the lower-fold persistence bracket $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}\in[5.5738,5.5756]$ from fixed-step integration with cycle amplitude $24.9$ at $\tau=5.57$ (superseded by the collocation's persistence of the stable branch through $\tau=5.5815$; Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}), and the original ungated core's upper fold ($\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx131.24$). For the corrected core it finds no large-amplitude attractor at any $\tau\in[64.39,140]$ (eight diverse far-from-equilibrium initial conditions, $6\times10^5$-year horizons, all decaying to the equilibrium), and locates the upper fold at $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\in[148.125,148.438]$, i.e., a narrow upper bistable window $(\approx148.3,150.36)$. The persistence tests also document a caution relevant to locating global folds: far-from-equilibrium trajectories in the monostable interval oscillate with near-cycle amplitude for $\sim10^4$--$10^5$ simulated years before collapsing to the equilibrium (at $\tau=64.39$ a $6\times10^4$-year run reports a persistent cycle, identically on both sides of the bracket $64.38994/64.39004$), so horizon-limited persistence tests near any candidate fold must reach $\gtrsim10^6$ years. The same integration quantifies the $k$-sensitivity of the global structure: with $\delta$ fixed, the lower fold is $\approx5.0$, $5.574$, and $\le3.5$\,yr at $k=5,10,20$ respectively (at $k=20$ the cycle shrinks to small amplitude as $\tau\to\tau_-$ and no stable-cycle/equilibrium coexistence remains above $\tau_-$), while the equilibrium and both Hopf thresholds remain exactly $k$-independent; $k$ therefore affects the global folds but not the local structure (Section~\ref{sec:parameter-audit}, Table~\ref{tab:params}). Because $k$ is a nonphysical regularisation constant whose value can change the existence of the global bistable windows, the global bifurcation topology reported here is established for the specific regularisation family and parameters used; it is not claimed as robust to arbitrary regularisation choices, and the local (equilibrium and Hopf) results, which are $k$-independent, carry the primary quantitative weight. These computations are verified in the accompanying code (Section~\ref{sec:data-code}).
\paragraph{Periodicity of the upper-window attractor.} Whether the large-amplitude attractor in the upper bistable window is a strictly periodic orbit is examined directly for the corrected core. Poincar\'e sections (transverse surface $E=\text{const}$ at mid-range, upward crossings) of the attractor at $\tau=148.4$, $150$ and $152$\,yr (the last beyond $\tau_+$, where the cycle is the sole attractor), taken after $2$--$4\times10^6$-year warm-up, show the section spread collapsing to below $5\times10^{-4}$ in the settled record and decreasing with the integration step ($5.5\times10^{-4}$ at $dt=0.1$, $1.5\times10^{-4}$ at $dt=0.05$, $2.4\times10^{-5}$ at $dt=0.02$) --- the signature of a period-1 limit cycle, since a torus would show a finite, step-independent spread. The power spectrum of the settled trajectory contains the fundamental (period $\approx157$\,yr) and its second harmonic only. The corrected core's large-amplitude attractor in the upper window is therefore a period-1 limit cycle; the corresponding question for the ungated core at $\tau\approx131.8$ (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) is resolved in Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}, which finds the same period-1 result there.

\subsection{Robustness Across Parameters}
\label{sec:robustness-corrected}
The equilibrium $\to$ characteristic-equation $\to$ Hopf/fold pipeline above is repeated for Candidate B and for one-at-a-time variation of every parameter across the literature-anchored ranges of Table~\ref{tab:params}, cross-checking each case against direct simulation and, for the fold locations specifically, against the continuation-based diagnosis of Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} rather than fixed-initial-condition bisection alone. The same pipeline for Candidate B (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}) gives a \emph{partially different} qualitative structure: a stabilising Hopf at $\tau_-\approx6.214$\,yr that is \textbf{supercritical} — the cycle grows continuously from small amplitude as $\tau$ decreases below $\tau_-$ (amplitude $\approx36.4,9.7,2.5,1.1$ at $\tau=4,5,6,6.15$\,yr), so no lower fold and no lower bistable window exist; the small-amplitude cycle that persists just below $\tau_-$ could be mistaken for a distinct lower threshold, but it is the supercritical branch itself, not a fold; a wide monostable interval extending to $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx76.075$\,yr; then a narrow upper bistable window $(76.075,76.29)$ in which a large-amplitude cycle coexists with the now-stable equilibrium; and a destabilising Hopf at $\tau_+\approx76.29$\,yr beyond which the cycle is the sole attractor. The wide monostable interval is therefore approximately $(6.214,76.075)$\,yr for Candidate B, comparable in relative terms to Candidate A's $(7.36,131.24)$\,yr; the upper-fold ordering $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}<\tau_+$ holds, and only the \emph{lower} fold of the standard sequence is absent (a supercritical lower Hopf) rather than reversed. This same qualitative sequence reappears across every intermediate parameter combination tested via characteristic-equation root-tracking for the two Hopf points (e.g., $\eta=1.5$: $\tau_-\approx18.7$, $\tau_+\approx75.1$\,yr; $\tau_m=2$: $\tau_-\approx11.0$, $\tau_+\approx121.9$\,yr); the full continuation-based fold search was not repeated at every one of these intermediate combinations, so the reported $\tau_+$ values for the one-at-a-time sweep should be read as locating the destabilising Hopf specifically, with the corresponding fold expected (by analogy with Candidates A and B) to sit within roughly $1$--$2$\,yr of it. At the extremes of the ranges in Table~\ref{tab:params} (very fast intrinsic growth $r$, very weak effort-adjustment $\eta$, or very large/small $E_{\max}$, $\Delta_{\text{ref}}$, $\tau_m$), the delay-driven structure disappears and the equilibrium is either stable or unstable for \emph{all} $\tau$ tested (up to 300 years), confirmed under direct simulation for both regimes. The qualitative bifurcation \emph{sequence} (stabilising Hopf, wide monostable interval, destabilising Hopf, with flanking folds when the relevant Hopf is subcritical) is structurally robust over the model's plausible parameter space for the underlying model parameters $r,\eta,E_{\max},\Delta_{\text{ref}},\tau_m,k$ \textemdash{} with the one systematic exception documented above that a given flanking fold is absent whenever the adjacent Hopf is supercritical (as for Candidate B's lower Hopf) — but, as detailed immediately below, this robustness does \emph{not} extend to the delay parameter $\tau$ itself landing in the range Table~\ref{tab:params} calls empirically plausible.

\paragraph{Identifying which threshold is the headline result.} The destabilising Hopf $\tau_+$ and its associated fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ both require institutional delays of many decades, which raises the question of whether these are the model's central, policy-relevant thresholds, or whether the \emph{other} Hopf crossing the original ungated core produces, $\tau_-$ (together with its associated fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$), is the more empirically relevant one. Checking both against a fuller empirical picture of institutional delay \cite{hocherman2025} answers this question directly.

A systematic review of 101 studies on time lags in environmental governance \cite{hocherman2025} reports response lags ranging from 2 years (fishery management reform) and 6--7 years (EU Marine Strategy Framework Directive, Baltic Sea Action Plan) to 13 years (Montreal Protocol, ozone), 27 years (acid rain: scientific discovery to the 1990 US Clean Air Act Amendments), 55--58 years (PCBs, DDT, mercury), and approximately a century for climate change (first scientific warnings to sustained global policy response). Table~\ref{tab:params}'s original 2--25 year range for $\tau$ was an accurate but narrow slice of this distribution, capturing only the fast-responding end (fisheries, regional directives).

\textbf{The thresholds checked in this subsection are those of the original ungated core (Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core}, Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}), not the boundary-exact effort-saturation-corrected core of Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}}: the corrected equation relocates these values substantially (Candidate A: $\tau_-\approx3.67$ rather than $6.88$; Candidate B: $\tau_-\approx5.51$ rather than $6.21$), and the threshold registry of Section~\ref{sec:core-model} should be consulted before citing any specific number below outside this subsection's own scope. Checking the original ungated core's \emph{stabilising} Hopf crossing $\tau_-$ against this fuller range shows it is, in fact, robustly and directly grounded: for Candidate A, $\tau_-\approx6.88$ years; for Candidate B, $\tau_-\approx6.21$ years; and across the literature-anchored ranges for $r$ and $\eta$ in Table~\ref{tab:params}, $\tau_-$ consistently falls between roughly 4 and 25 years wherever it exists at all (Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}) --- with the caveat that the delay-induced structure itself exists only in a bounded window of regeneration rates, $r\in(0.008,0.022)$ yr$^{-1}$ at Candidate A's $\eta$ (widening to $r\lesssim0.062$ yr$^{-1}$ at the maximal effort response $\eta=3.0$), so it ceases to exist well within the table's own $r$ range once $r\gtrsim0.025$ yr$^{-1}$ --- — squarely inside the empirically documented range spanning fisheries reform (2 years) through the Baltic Sea Action Plan (6--7 years) to the Montreal Protocol (13 years). Direct long-horizon simulation (not just linearisation) confirms that this crossing marks a sharp transition: for Candidate A, institutional delays shorter than $\tau_-\approx6.9$ years leave the system trapped in a large-amplitude, sustained oscillation, while delays of 8 years or longer allow it to settle smoothly to the stable equilibrium; the same qualitative transition holds for Candidate B at $\tau_-\approx6.2$ years. The effort-saturation-corrected core (Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}) exhibits the qualitatively identical transition, at its own relocated thresholds ($\tau_-\approx3.67$ and $5.51$ years respectively), both of which likewise sit inside the empirically documented governance-lag range.

\textbf{This is the model's headline result under the original ungated core, and it runs counter to the premise, common in the environmental-governance and climate-policy literature, that reducing response lag is unconditionally beneficial} (e.g., the precautionary-principle literature's emphasis on shifting response earlier relative to pressure and impact \cite{hocherman2025}, and macroeconomic delay-cost analyses of climate policy that find later action strictly worse \cite{mckibbin2014}). In this model, an institution that \emph{mobilises additional extraction effort too quickly} in response to a resource deficit (within a few years) can lock the system into a sustained boom-bust cycle, whereas a moderate delay of roughly a decade — well within the range documented for real environmental governance processes such as regional marine directives or global ozone policy — allows the delayed negative feedback to act as a stabilising filter rather than a destabilising one. The delay $\tau$ here is specifically a \textbf{scarcity-mobilisation delay} (the delay between awareness of a deficit and an effected increase in extraction pressure; Section~\ref{sec:parameter-audit}); it is \emph{not} a delay in protective regulation, and this result is silent on whether faster protective governance is destabilising — that is a separate institutional channel, left to future work (Section~\ref{sec:limitations-further-work}). This qualitative result holds under both the original and effort-saturation-corrected equations (Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}); only the precise numerical value of $\tau_-$ differs between them. The claim is empirically testable because $\tau_-$ falls within a delay range with documented analogues, unlike $\tau_+$.

\paragraph{What \cite{hocherman2025} measures, and how it anchors $\tau$.} The delay $\tau$ enters the effort equation as $Z(t-\tau)$, where $Z(t)$ already tracks the \emph{current}, undelayed physical deficit through the first-order memory filter of Eq.~\eqref{eq:Z-core}; $\tau$ therefore specifically represents the delay between an institution's real-time awareness of a deficit and an actual, effected change in extraction pressure --- an \textbf{implementation lag}, in the specific terminology of the temporal Driver-Pressure-State-Impact-Response framework that \cite{hocherman2025} itself uses, as distinct from a \textbf{response lag} (problem recognition to decision). A direct reading of \cite{hocherman2025} shows that its 101-study synthesis --- the source cited above for $\tau$'s numerical anchoring --- explicitly does not cover implementation lags: the paper states that, owing to the breadth of the literature, analysis of implementation lags is reserved for future work, and its reported figures (the 2--13-year range quoted above) are response lags specifically, a distinct and generally shorter causal segment than the decision-to-effect gap $\tau$ is meant to represent. Citing this source for $\tau$'s numerical range is consequently best read as an approximation --- a response lag can reasonably serve as a partial or lower-bound proxy for an implementation lag, since implementation follows response and adds to it, so real institutional $\tau$ values are, if anything, likely to sit \emph{above} the range quoted here rather than below it --- but this should be stated explicitly rather than presented as a direct empirical match. No systematic dataset of implementation lags specifically is known to exist to anchor $\tau$ more precisely; building or estimating one is identified as a high-leverage open task for giving $\tau_-$ genuinely predictive, rather than purely order-of-magnitude, empirical content, additional to and independent of the case-level empirical gap identified in Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}.

The destabilising Hopf $\tau_+$ and its fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ remain genuine features of the original ungated core's mathematics, established and located with the same methods as $\tau_-$ and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ (Sections~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}), but they should be understood as a second, longer-horizon regime of the same bifurcation diagram, not as the model's central real-world claim. (The effort-saturation-corrected core exhibits the same qualitative regime at its own relocated $\tau_+\approx150.36$ years for Candidate A; Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}.) The characteristic equation~\eqref{eq:char-eq-corrected} traces why this second regime requires such long delays: at $\tau_+\approx132.4$ years (Candidate A), the destabilised mode has Hopf frequency $\omega\approx0.0442$ rad/yr (period $2\pi/\omega\approx142$ years), set almost entirely by $A_N=r(1-2N^*/K)-qE^*\approx-r$, the linearised relaxation rate of the \emph{stock} equation, since $N^*$ sits close to $K$ at these parameters. Destabilising a mode of that period generically requires a delay of a comparable order of magnitude, pinning $\tau_+$ (and, empirically, the nearby fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$) to $\sim1/r$ regardless of the institutional-response parameters $\eta,\tau_m$: re-scanning the rightmost eigenvalue while varying $r$ alone confirms this — raising $r$ from 0.02/yr to just 0.03--0.08/yr (still within Table~\ref{tab:params}'s own range) already removes any Hopf crossing from $\tau\in[2,25]$ years entirely, while raising $\eta$ does not compensate. Because this second regime is tied to the resource's own slow biological timescale rather than to institutional response speed, it is best read as describing what would need to be true of governance for the very slowest, most multilateral, most contested class of environmental problems (the climate-change end of the distribution reported by \cite{hocherman2025}) rather than as a claim about typical resource management. Accordingly, $\tau_-$ (and its nearby fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$) — not $\tau_+$ (and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$) — is the threshold pair this model supports as the real-world-relevant prediction of the two, its magnitude numerically comparable to documented governance lags; direct case-level support remains absent (Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}); $\tau_+$ and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ retain policy relevance only for governance settings operating on multi-decade-to-century timescales.

\subsection{Extending the Core: Two Liquidation Channels}
\label{sec:two-channel}
The core model of Section~\ref{sec:core-model-eqs} represents liquidation as a single mechanism: excess extraction pressure is, in effect, always ``killing hens'' — it acts on the same stock variable $N$ that regeneration also acts on, with no distinction between removing an existing adult and forgoing a future recruit. As discussed in the Introduction, this conflates at least two physically distinct mechanisms. We extend the core to represent both explicitly.

\paragraph{Birth/death decomposition.} We first split the net regeneration term $S(N)=rN(1-N/K)$ into an explicit gross recruitment (``birth'') flow and a background (harvest-independent) mortality flow,
\[
B(N) = S(N) + \kappa r N, \qquad M(N) = \kappa r N, \qquad B(N)-M(N)=S(N)\ \ \forall\kappa\ge0,
\]
where $\kappa\ge0$ is a turnover parameter controlling how much gross birth/death flux underlies the same net growth curve ($\kappa=0$ recovers the original model, with all net growth attributed to recruitment and no explicit background mortality).

\paragraph{Channel mix.} We introduce a \textbf{liquidation channel parameter} $\psi\in[0,1]$ splitting the same effort-driven extraction pressure $qEN$ (Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected}) between the two mechanisms:
\[
C_{\text{stock}}(t) = \psi\, qE(t)N(t) \quad\text{(``kill hens'': removes standing stock directly)},
\]
\[
C_{\text{recruit}}(t) = (1-\psi)\, qE(t)N(t) \quad\text{(``eat eggs'': suppresses this period's recruitment only)}.
\]
The extended stock equation is
\begin{equation}
\dot N = \max\bigl(0,\ B(N) - C_{\text{recruit}}(t)\bigr) - M(N) - C_{\text{stock}}(t),
\label{eq:two-channel-stock}
\end{equation}
where the outer $\max(0,\cdot)$ reflects that recruitment cannot be driven negative — eating more eggs than were laid simply means zero new recruits that period, not a debt carried forward. Eq.~\eqref{eq:two-channel-stock} is non-negativity-preserving for \emph{any} $\psi\in[0,1]$: at $N=0$, $B(0)=M(0)=C_{\text{stock}}(0)=0$ identically (since both are proportional to $N$), so $\dot N|_{N=0}=\max(0,0-0)-0-0=0$ for any effort trajectory, generalising the single-channel non-negativity argument of Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected}. $\psi=1$ recovers the core of Section~\ref{sec:core-model-eqs} exactly ($M(N)+C_{\text{stock}}\to qEN$, no recruitment suppression); $\psi=0$ is the pure ``eat eggs'' limit.

\paragraph{The equilibrium and local (Hopf) structure are channel-invariant; the fold location is very nearly, but not exactly, invariant.} Away from the recruitment floor, Eq.~\eqref{eq:two-channel-stock} simplifies to $\dot N = B(N)-C_{\text{recruit}}-M(N)-C_{\text{stock}} = S(N) - qEN$, \emph{identical to the single-channel equation, independent of $\psi$ and $\kappa$}. Symbolic analysis shows that the recruitment floor never binds at the interior equilibrium of Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core} for any $\psi\in[0,1],\kappa\ge0$ (the floor requires $C_{\text{recruit}}>B(N)$, which — using $qEN=S(N)$ at equilibrium — reduces to $-\psi\,S(N) > \kappa r N$; since $\psi\ge0$, $S(N)\ge0$, $\kappa\ge0$, $r>0$, and $N>0$, the left side is $\le0$ and the right side is $\ge0$, so this can never hold, i.e., never). Consequently, the equilibrium $(N^*,Z^*,E^*)$, the linearised Jacobian, and the exact characteristic equation~\eqref{eq:char-eq-corrected} — and therefore the two Hopf points $\tau_-,\tau_+$, which are determined purely by that local linearisation — are all completely unchanged by $\psi$ and $\kappa$, confirmed numerically to the fourth decimal place by direct simulation from a near-equilibrium initial condition across $\psi\in\{0,0.5,1\}$.

The fold locations, by contrast, are a genuinely global feature of the large-amplitude orbit, along which the recruitment floor \emph{does} bind ($B(N)-C_{\text{recruit}}<0$ over a substantial fraction of each cycle once $N$ drops well below $N^*$). A local-invariance argument therefore cannot, by itself, establish fold invariance, and locating the fold correctly requires the continuation-based methodology of Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} rather than fixed-initial-condition bisection, which is unreliable near these thresholds owing to critical slowing down and can report spuriously coincident fold locations for the two channels. Continuation-based, multi-million-year-horizon bisection (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) gives $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}(\psi{=}1)\approx131.998$\,yr and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}(\psi{=}0)\approx132.499$\,yr — both close to the single-channel ($\kappa=0$) value of $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx131.24$\,yr and to each other, but not identical (at $\psi=1$ with the recruitment floor inactive throughout the cycle the dynamics coincide exactly with the single-channel equation, so the small residual reflects continuation precision at a global fold rather than a genuine channel effect; at $\psi=0$ the floor binds and the ~0.5\,yr extension is a real effect): pure recruitment-suppression ($\psi=0$) sustains the large-amplitude orbit for delays about $0.5$\,yr longer than pure stock-culling ($\psi=1$) before the fold occurs, a $\sim\!0.4\%$ shift in $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$. This is consistent with channel-dependent dynamics along the orbit (Section~\ref{sec:two-channel}, below) shifting a genuinely global bifurcation: the shift is real, but small, because $\kappa=0.5$ places only a modest fraction of the cycle in the recruitment-floor-binding regime at these parameters. The fold location is accordingly \textbf{approximately, but not exactly, channel-invariant} rather than exactly invariant.

\paragraph{The channels diverge during large excursions.} The recruitment floor only binds during large excursions away from equilibrium, and it is exactly there that the two channels produce measurably different dynamics. Simulating the large-amplitude excursion at $\tau=115$ years (Candidate A, $\kappa=0.5$)— a value inside the monostable interval of the original ungated core (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}), where far-from-equilibrium histories sustain a long-lived (order-$10^4$--$10^5$-year) large-amplitude transient before collapsing to the equilibrium (the same ghost-transient phenomenon documented in Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}); the figure below shows this transient— the transient reaches a minimum of $N\approx33$ under pure stock-culling ($\psi=1$) but a minimum of $N\approx10$ under pure recruitment-suppression ($\psi=0$) — a substantially deeper trough for the same effort-driven extraction pressure and the same equilibrium (Fig.~\ref{fig:liquidation-channels}). The mechanism is that stock-culling directly removes biomass that would otherwise continue reproducing, providing an immediate (if harsh) brake on further decline, whereas recruitment-suppression leaves the standing stock alone and lets ordinary background mortality $M(N)$ erode it more slowly but for longer, before recovery can begin. The same qualitative pattern holds using a fixed (non-effort-driven) exogenous demand $D$ in place of $qEN$: for demand only moderately above the maximum sustainable yield, pure stock-culling collapses the population to zero in finite time, while pure recruitment-suppression produces a smoother asymptotic decline toward zero (Fig.~\ref{fig:hen-vs-egg}) — quantitatively different collapse timing and shape for the same excess demand, matching the qualitative distinction the hen/egg/apple-seed metaphor draws.

\begin{figure}[h]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{hen_vs_egg_mechanism.png}
\caption{\textbf{Same fixed excess demand ($D=0.7>S_{\max}=0.5$), same starting stock ($N_0=50$), two liquidation channels.} Pure stock-culling (``kill hens'', $\psi=1$) collapses to zero at a finite time ($t\approx158$); pure recruitment-suppression (``eat eggs'', $\psi=0$) approaches zero asymptotically and more slowly ($t\approx430$ to reach $N<1$). Both are non-negativity-preserving by construction.}
\label{fig:hen-vs-egg}
\end{figure}

\begin{figure}[h]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{liquidation_channel_dde.png}
\caption{\textbf{Large-amplitude excursion by liquidation channel ($\tau=115$ yr, Candidate A, $\kappa=0.5$).} All three curves share the same equilibrium and the same Hopf/SNPO thresholds (Section~\ref{sec:two-channel}); at this $\tau$, inside the monostable interval of the original core (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}), the excursion shown is a long-lived (order-$10^4$--$10^5$-year) transient that eventually collapses to the equilibrium (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}), rather than a steady-state bistable-window cycle. Over this transient the trough is substantially deeper under pure recruitment-suppression ($\psi=0$, minimum $N\approx10$) than under pure stock-culling ($\psi=1$, minimum $N\approx33$), with the mixed case in between.}
\label{fig:liquidation-channels}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{Implications.} This extension shows that the choice of liquidation mechanism is not a cosmetic modelling detail: two resources with identical regeneration curves, identical extraction pressure, and identical equilibria can nonetheless exhibit very different collapse dynamics once pushed into the large-amplitude regime, depending on whether extraction removes existing stock or suppresses future recruitment. Because the two channels are indistinguishable from equilibrium behaviour or from the location of the Hopf/SNPO thresholds alone, distinguishing them for a real resource requires information about \emph{which age/life-stage class is actually being extracted} (e.g., adult harvest vs.\ juvenile/recruit harvest vs.\ egg/seed harvest) — a modelling choice that should be made explicitly for each resource category, rather than defaulting to the stock-culling form used throughout the rest of this manuscript for simplicity ($\psi=1$). We work through this assignment for the resource categories next (Section~\ref{sec:psi-assignment}, Table~\ref{tab:psi-assignment}).

\subsection{Assigning $\psi$ to the Resource Categories of this Manuscript}
\label{sec:psi-assignment}
To make Section~\ref{sec:two-channel} operational rather than purely illustrative, we work through the resource categories already invoked elsewhere in this manuscript (Sections~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}--\ref{sec:deficit-dynamics}, \ref{sec:four-state-core}) and assign each a $\psi$ grounded in which physical sub-process dominates the liquidation pressure for that resource, rather than an arbitrary value. We stress that these are illustrative, literature-motivated starting points for a first-pass model, not fitted or validated estimates; genuine calibration would require resource-specific demographic data (age-structured mortality, recruitment curves) that are outside the scope of this framework.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\caption{Illustrative liquidation-channel assignments $\psi$ for the resource categories used elsewhere in this manuscript (Sections~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}--\ref{sec:deficit-dynamics}, \ref{sec:four-state-core}), together with the resulting trough depth of the large-amplitude excursion (the $\psi=0$ and $\psi=1$ endpoints give troughs of $\approx10$ and $\approx33$, respectively, Section~\ref{sec:two-channel}); (Candidate A, $\tau=115$ yr, $\kappa=0.5$; see Section~\ref{sec:two-channel}). Higher $\psi$ means the extraction pressure falls predominantly on existing standing stock (``kill hens''); lower $\psi$ means it falls predominantly on future recruitment/replenishment (``eat eggs''). As detailed in Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}, the excursion at this $\tau$ is a long-lived transient in the monostable range, not a persistent bistable-window cycle.}
\label{tab:psi-assignment}
\begin{tabular}{p{3.6cm}p{1.1cm}p{2.5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Resource / dominant mechanism} & $\boldsymbol\psi$ & \textbf{Cycle range} \\
\midrule
Fishery, growth overfishing (adult-biased gear selectivity) & 0.75 & 32.8--71.8 \\
Fishery, recruitment overfishing (juvenile bycatch, spawning-ground damage) & 0.30 & 23.9--63.2 \\
Forestry, clear-cut (seed trees removed with the stand) & 0.90 & 33.1--72.1 \\
Forestry, selective harvest (seed trees retained) & 0.55 & 29.1--68.4 \\
Groundwater, direct pumping of stored volume & 0.90 & 33.1--72.1 \\
Groundwater, impaired recharge (capped infiltration, upstream diversion) & 0.20 & 21.9--59.9 \\
Soil zinc, crop export of the plant-available pool & 0.85 & 33.1--72.1 \\
Soil zinc, impaired weathering/mineralisation (erosion, microbiome damage) & 0.25 & 23.0--61.8 \\
Pollinators, direct adult mortality (pesticide exposure) & 0.70 & 32.0--71.1 \\
Pollinators, brood/larval failure (habitat loss, forage scarcity) & 0.20 & 21.9--59.9 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\paragraph{Reasoning behind the assignments.} In each pair, the higher-$\psi$ mechanism directly removes an already-existing, appropriable unit of the resource (a mature fish, a seed-bearing tree, water already infiltrated into the aquifer, zinc already bound in harvestable biomass, a living adult pollinator), while the lower-$\psi$ mechanism instead degrades the process that would have \emph{replenished} the stock, without necessarily touching what is already there (unspawned recruits, un-germinated seed, water that would have recharged the aquifer, zinc that would have weathered into plant-available form, unlaid brood). We deliberately excluded from this table any mechanism that damages the resource's underlying \emph{carrying capacity} $K$ itself (e.g., aquifer compaction/subsidence, which permanently reduces storage volume, or severe soil erosion, which lowers the ceiling on future zinc availability) — that is a distinct phenomenon from the channel split $\psi$ modelled here, better represented as a slow drift in $K$ or a transfer to the inert reservoir $\mathbf I_j$ (Section~\ref{sec:abiotic}), and is not addressed by this extension.

\paragraph{Consequence for the vector framework.} Table~\ref{tab:psi-assignment} shows a roughly 1.5-fold range in excursion trough depth (from $\approx21.9$ to $\approx33.1$ across the tabulated assignments, holding all other parameters fixed; the extremes $\psi=1$ and $\psi=0$ give troughs of $\approx33$ and $\approx10$ respectively, Section~\ref{sec:two-channel}) driven purely by which liquidation mechanism dominates for a given resource. Because the vector framework of Sections~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}--\ref{sec:deficit-dynamics} aggregates across resource components with heterogeneous extraction mechanisms (Section~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}, the ``productivity illusion''), this suggests that a fully resource-specific application of the framework should assign $\psi$ per stock-service pair $(i,c)$ rather than adopting a single value across the whole vector $\mathbf N$; we leave the formal extension of $\psi$ to a vector $\boldsymbol\psi_{i,c}$, and its propagation through the stock-service mapping of Section~\ref{sec:service-to-stock}, to future work.

\paragraph{A mass-conservation subtlety that any such extension must resolve at the specification stage.} Propagating $\boldsymbol\psi_{i,c}$ into the vector framework's product/waste routing (Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected}) is not a purely notational change, because the two channels are not physically symmetric with respect to what they remove. The stock-culling channel $\mathbf C^{\text{stock}}_i=\boldsymbol\psi_i\odot q_i\mathbf E_i\odot\mathbf N_i$ removes existing, already-embodied biomass, so it is the physical harvest flow that can legitimately be routed onward into $\mathbf P_i$ and $\mathbf W_j$ via $\boldsymbol\alpha_i$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:product}), exactly as the single-channel extraction $\mathbf C_i$ already is. The recruitment-suppression channel $\mathbf C^{\text{recruit}}_i=(\mathbf 1-\boldsymbol\psi_i)\odot q_i\mathbf E_i\odot\mathbf N_i$, by contrast, does not remove existing stock at all: it suppresses a future inflow to $\mathbf N_i$ that, under the outer floor $\max(\mathbf 0,\mathbf B_i-\mathbf C^{\text{recruit}}_i)$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:two-channel-stock}), may or may not have materialised even in the channel's absence. Un-laid eggs, un-germinated seed, and un-recharged aquifer volume were never part of $\mathbf N_i$ and cannot be harvested into a product stock or dispersed into waste, so no fraction of $\mathbf C^{\text{recruit}}_i$---nominal or floor-capped---should be routed into $\mathbf P_i$ or $\mathbf W_j$ at all; the correct treatment is that $\mathbf C^{\text{recruit}}_i$ leaves no mass-routing footprint outside the stock equation itself, where it already appears exactly once, inside the floor. Confirmed by direct calculation: mass leaving $\mathbf N_i$ decomposes exactly as $-\dot{\mathbf N}_i=\mathbf M_i(\mathbf N_i)+\mathbf C^{\text{stock}}_i$ whenever the floor binds (the recruitment-suppression term contributes zero net outflow by construction, since it only ever prevents an inflow that has not yet occurred), so only $\mathbf C^{\text{stock}}_i$, not any function of $\mathbf C^{\text{recruit}}_i$, should enter the $\boldsymbol\alpha_i$-weighted product/waste split. This is a specification requirement for the extension, not an additional modelling choice: routing any part of $\mathbf C^{\text{recruit}}_i$ into $\mathbf P_i$ or $\mathbf W_j$, whether nominal or floor-capped, would create mass in the product/waste compartments that was never physically present in $\mathbf N_i$, reintroducing in the vector setting the same class of mass violation that Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected} already identifies and repairs for the single-channel case.

\subsection{Phase-Space Topology and the Extinction Boundary}
The phase space of the corrected three-state core is the Banach space \(\mathcal{C} = C([-\tau, 0], \mathbb{R}^3)\) (three delayed state variables, one delay). The interior equilibrium \((N^*,Z^*,E^*)\) is unstable for \(\tau < \tau_{-}\) and \(\tau > \tau_{+}\), and stable for \(\tau_{-} < \tau < \tau_{+}\); because it is a delay-differential equation, it possesses a finite-dimensional unstable manifold and an infinite-dimensional stable manifold outside the stable window, making it a saddle-focus in the DDE phase space there. The boundary equilibrium $(0,Z^*_{\text{ext}},E^*_{\text{ext}})=(0,\delta,E^*)$ of Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core} governs the ultimate fate of trajectories that cross into extinction: it is now a genuine fixed point of the stated dynamics (Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core}), and its local stability in the $N$-direction is governed by the condition $r>qE^*_{\text{ext}}$, which — because $E^*_{\text{ext}}\equiv E^*$ exactly (Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core}) — is algebraically identical to the interior equilibrium's own existence condition $N^*>0$, making the interior and boundary equilibria a single transcritical pair rather than two independently-stable states.

\subsection{Global Bifurcation and the Collapse Cycle}
Because the effort equation involves a delayed self-feedback (\(Z(t-\tau)\), itself a filtered function of $N(t)$ and $E(t)$, in the \(\dot{E}\) equation), the system does not belong to the class of purely cyclic feedback DDEs with off-diagonal delays, and the Poincar\'e--Bendixson theorem for monotone DDEs \cite{mallet1996,smith1995} cannot be invoked to rigorously exclude chaotic attractors or guarantee a unique periodic orbit by topological means alone. The analysis therefore relies on numerical evidence (direct simulation and continuation from multiple initial conditions, Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) rather than a topological uniqueness proof. The existence, instability, and fold structure of the intervening periodic orbits --- and hence the status of the SNPO classification of the folds at $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ --- are established in Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected} and Appendix H; the classification should be treated as strongly supported but not yet rigorously demonstrated.

Note also that the equilibrium's local stability window itself is exactly $\tau_-<\tau<\tau_+$ (a purely linear fact, established in Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} from the characteristic equation), whereas the \emph{global}, basin-of-attraction sense in which a single trajectory from an arbitrary initial condition converges to the quiet equilibrium holds only on the narrower range $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}<\tau<\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$: for $\tau_-<\tau<\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ and for $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}<\tau<\tau_+$, the equilibrium is locally stable but not globally attracting, since the large-amplitude cycle persists as a coexisting attractor with its own, separate basin. This distinction — local versus global stability of the equilibrium — is exactly the topological point that motivates locating the folds independently of the Hopf points (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}), rather than assuming they coincide.

\paragraph{A threshold registry, to be checked against whenever a numerical value is cited below or elsewhere in this manuscript.} Because this section and Sections~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}, \ref{sec:four-state-core}, and \ref{sec:unified-core} report bifurcation thresholds for the model variants below --- with materially different boundary behaviour and materially different numerical values --- every threshold cited anywhere in this manuscript belongs to exactly one of the rows below; a threshold cited without a variant tag follows the default rule stated after the table.
