All four rows share the same closed-form equilibrium $(N^*,Z^*,E^*)$ within each state-space variant (the gating multiplies the whole right-hand side, so it does not change where $\dot E=0$ at an interior point; Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}), and all four are internally verified by direct substitution and, where a fold is reported, by long-horizon nonlinear simulation. The default reading for any threshold cited without a variant tag is the \textbf{canonical model}: the effort-saturation-corrected four-state core (first row of Table~\ref{tab:threshold-registry}), whose thresholds are verified by direct substitution, dt-converged persistence bisection, and the full four-state pipeline (Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}). Within Sections 7--8, where the three-state limit system is the subject of analysis, thresholds belong to that system by construction and are tagged explicitly wherever ambiguity could arise; the corrected three-state, ungated three-state, ungated four-state, and unified variants are flagged at every point of use.

\paragraph{Nomenclature rule.} The bare, unqualified phrase ``the corrected core'' is avoided as a variant label wherever ambiguity could arise, precisely because ``corrected'' is also used generically elsewhere in this manuscript (Sections~\ref{sec:abiotic}, \ref{sec:natcap-corrected}, \ref{sec:asymptotic-reduction}) and is consequently ambiguous between two distinct referents: the general sense of ``the corrected exposition'' (which may still use the original ungated effort law) and the specific sense of ``the effort-saturation-corrected core'' (Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected}). Within and immediately after Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}, where the effort-saturation-corrected core is the sole subject under discussion, the shorthand ``the corrected core'' is used unambiguously and should be read as that variant. The four variant labels used consistently throughout are \textbf{original ungated core}, \textbf{effort-saturation-corrected core}, \textbf{four-state core}, and \textbf{unified core v2}; any threshold cited under one of these labels, or under no label at all, should be read according to the table above.


\section{Numerical Continuation of the Global Attractor}
\label{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}
For the three-state core model derived in Section~\ref{sec:core-model}, the closed-form equilibrium, the two Hopf crossings, and the two SNPO folds are established by three independent means: (i) direct root-tracking of the rightmost eigenvalue of the exact characteristic quasi-polynomial Eq.~\eqref{eq:char-eq-corrected} across a grid of $(\text{Re}\,\lambda,\text{Im}\,\lambda,\tau)$, which locates the two Hopf points exactly (these are local, linear objects); and (ii) numerical continuation of the large-amplitude periodic orbit in $\tau$ — carrying the converged orbit's final state forward as the initial condition for each successive small step in $\tau$, rather than restarting from an arbitrary fixed initial condition — combined with long-horizon ($1.5$--$5\times10^6$ simulated years) persistence/collapse testing at each step, which locates the two folds. Every threshold is additionally cross-checked against plain forward simulation, both from near-equilibrium and from independently-chosen far-from-equilibrium initial conditions, run for at least $6{,}000$--$20{,}000$ simulated years for a first pass and up to $5\times10^6$ years near any threshold, since shorter horizons can seriously mislocate the folds owing to critical slowing down near the neighbouring Hopf points (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}).

\subsection{Global Bifurcation and Saddle-Node of Periodic Orbits}
Continuation-based bisection on $\tau$ confirms two \textbf{amplitude-discontinuous folds, consistent with but not yet independently confirmed as Saddle-Node-of-Periodic-Orbits (candidate SNPOs)}, per parameter set: a large-amplitude stable orbit appears or disappears discontinuously in amplitude as $\tau$ crosses each threshold, $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ (just above $\tau_-$) and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ (just below $\tau_+$), while the interior equilibrium's local stability, governed purely by the two Hopf points, is unaffected (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}). An intervening \emph{unstable} periodic orbit of the kind a true SNPO requires has been located and confirmed unstable near $\tau_-$ for both the effort-saturation-corrected and the original ungated core (next paragraph: Fourier spectral collocation followed by shooting-based Floquet-multiplier computation); what remains unconfirmed is whether that unstable branch collides with the stable branch at the fold with the characteristic $\sqrt{\tau-\tau_{\text{SNPO}}}$ scaling of a genuine saddle-node of periodic orbits; the corrected core's lower boundary has in fact been resolved as a tight pair of individually-genuine folds rather than a single SNPO (below), so the classification is strongly supported but provisional pending that further check.

\paragraph{An intervening unstable periodic orbit near $\tau_-$.} Fourier spectral collocation \cite{engelborghs2002} locates a periodic orbit of the effort-saturation-corrected core at $\tau=3.700$ (just above $\tau_-=3.666$, inside the lower bistable window $(\tau_-,\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}})=(3.67,\gtrsim5.58)$), converged to a collocation residual of $\sim10^{-7}$, i.e. an essentially exact solution of the governing DDE. Using this orbit's state as the initial history for direct forward simulation, with no added perturbation, floating-point roundoff alone eventually displaces the trajectory from the orbit over a multi-million-year horizon; it then relaxes onto the large-amplitude cycle, reaching the same amplitude ($\approx55.8$) as the coexisting stable cycle at this $\tau$. The orbit therefore sits exactly on the basin boundary between the quiet equilibrium and the large-amplitude cycle, the defining signature of a genuine unstable periodic orbit. The escape is independent of integration step size ($dt=0.01,0.02,0.04$ give the same outcome) and specific to $\tau$ near $\tau_-$: the identical test at $\tau=3.92,4.11,4.59,4.91$ (further from $\tau_-$, still inside the same bistable window) shows no escape within a three-million-year horizon, consistent with an unstable orbit whose escape timescale (set by its unstable Floquet multiplier) shrinks sharply near the Hopf point. This establishes that an intervening unstable periodic orbit of the kind a true SNPO requires exists locally near $\tau_-$. The orbit's amplitude does vanish as $\tau\to\tau_-$ with the characteristic subcritical-Hopf scaling: tracking the unstable branch down from $\tau=3.700$ gives a clean $\sqrt{\tau-\tau_-}$ law (fit of amplitude$^2$ versus $\tau-3.666$: slope $29.8$, $R^2=0.994$), i.e.\ the small orbit shrinks to zero at the Hopf point as a genuine SNPO-partner subcritical Hopf requires. What remains to be demonstrated is whether this unstable branch collides with the stable large-amplitude branch at $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ or $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ with the characteristic $\sqrt{\tau-\tau_{\text{SNPO}}}$ amplitude scaling a single SNPO requires; the latter would require continuously tracking both branches' amplitudes to a common $\tau$ (and the corrected core's lower boundary is in fact a tight pair of individually-genuine folds rather than a single SNPO, Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}). A direct Floquet computation for this orbit, by the shooting method (the orbit re-converged as a fixed point of the discrete RK4 segment map; the multipliers are the eigenvalues of the central-difference Jacobian of that map), resolves the instability cleanly: the dominant (radial) multiplier converges to $\exp(-2\sigma T)\approx1.0016$ as the step refines ($1.0008,1.0011,1.0013$ at $dt=0.05,0.025,0.0125$\,yr, where $\sigma=-3.2\times10^{-6}$\,yr$^{-1}$ is the equilibrium's linear growth rate at this $\tau$ and $T\approx249.9$\,yr), while the phase multiplier converges to $1$ ($0.998,0.999,1.000$). The orbit is therefore unstable, as required of the partner in a subcritical Hopf / SNPO fold. For the ungated core, the analogous small orbit at $\tau=7.1$ (just above $\tau_-=6.881$) is located by the same collocation and continuation machinery and confirmed unstable by the same shooting-Floquet method: its dominant Floquet multiplier is $\approx1.018$ in modulus (the normal-form value $\exp(-2\sigma T)\approx1.012$, where $\sigma(\tau)<0$ is the equilibrium's linear growth rate on the stable side), i.e., an unstable cycle on the stable side of $\tau_-$, the partner that a subcritical Hopf / SNPO fold requires. The collision of this branch with the stable branch at the fold, with the characteristic $\sqrt{\tau-\tau_{\text{SNPO}}}$ scaling, remains to be demonstrated by branch continuation.

\paragraph{Periodicity of the large-amplitude attractor in the upper bistable window.} Whether the large-amplitude attractor in the upper bistable window of the original ungated core ($\tau\in(\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}},\tau_+)=(131.24,132.4)$\,yr, Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) is a strictly periodic orbit is examined directly. Poincar\'e sections (transverse surface $E=\text{const}$ at mid-range, upward crossings) of the attractor at $\tau=131.8$\,yr, taken after a $3\times10^6$-year warm-up, show the section spread collapsing from the transient value ($\approx12$) to below $5\times10^{-3}$ in the settled record and decreasing with the integration step. The envelope of successive $N$-maxima is constant to within $0.001$--$0.004$ (about $0.005$\% of the attractor's total range) over a $2\times10^6$-year record at every step size tested ($dt=0.02$: $\le0.001$; $dt=0.01$: $\le0.004$), and the power spectrum of the settled trajectory contains the fundamental (period $\approx135.6$\,yr) and its harmonics only. The large-amplitude attractor of the ungated core in its upper bistable window is therefore a period-1 limit cycle. This periodicity conclusion is confirmed by Floquet analysis: shooting the orbit as a fixed point of the discrete segment map (converged to residual $\lesssim10^{-10}$) and computing the eigenvalues of its Jacobian gives Floquet multipliers all strictly inside the unit circle (the phase multiplier is recovered at $\approx0.99$, expected exactly $1$; the dominant non-trivial pair has modulus $\approx0.81$). A stable limit cycle has exactly one multiplier on the unit circle (the phase mode); the absence of any second unit-circle multiplier rules out a quasi-periodic (torus) attractor, for which a second multiplier on the unit circle is required. Furthermore, no quasi-periodic modulation is present: fixed-step RK4 and an independent method-of-steps \emph{adaptive} RK45 integration (\texttt{scipy} \texttt{solve\_ivp}, relative tolerance $10^{-9}$) both yield an $N$-envelope constant to $\approx0.005$ over $2\times10^4$\,yr, consistent with the Floquet spectrum; the large-amplitude attractor of the ungated core in its upper bistable window is a stable period-1 limit cycle, so the SNPO classification question for these folds (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) is not undermined by a torus ambiguity. These computations are verified in the accompanying code (Section~\ref{sec:data-code}).

The periodicity result above bears directly on the SNPO classification: a Saddle-Node of Periodic Orbits is, by definition, a bifurcation of \emph{periodic} orbits (two simple closed curves in phase space colliding and annihilating). With the large-amplitude attractor established as a period-1 limit cycle in both cores' upper windows (the corrected core at $\tau=148.4,150,152$\,yr, Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}; the ungated core at $\tau=131.8$\,yr, above), the amplitude-discontinuity folds at $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ remain candidate saddle-nodes of periodic orbits, with the classification awaiting Floquet-multiplier tracking along the branches as described in Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}. A torus-related classification would require the attractor to be quasi-periodic, for which no evidence is found in the Poincar\'e-section or spectral analysis reported above.

Two representative parameter sets, both drawn from the literature-anchored ranges (Table~\ref{tab:params}):
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Candidate A:} \(\eta = 0.914\), \(E_{\max} = 30\), \(\delta_0 = 0.01\), \(\Delta_{\text{ref}} = 1.0\), \(\tau_m = 5\), giving $\tau_-\approx6.88$, $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}\approx7.36$, $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx131.24$, $\tau_+\approx132.4$ years.
\item \textbf{Candidate B:} \(\eta = 2.756\), \(E_{\max} = 26\), \(\delta_0 = 0.01\), \(\Delta_{\text{ref}} = 1.0\), \(\tau_m = 5\), giving $\tau_-\approx6.21$ (supercritical lower Hopf: no lower fold or bistable window), $\tau_+\approx76.29$, and an upper fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx76.075$ years (narrow upper bistable window $(76.075,76.29)$; Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}).
\end{itemize}
For Candidate A, the wide, monostable interval is therefore approximately $(7.36, 131.24)$ years, flanked by two narrow bistable windows, $(6.88,7.36)$ and $(131.24,132.4)$ years, in which the large-amplitude cycle coexists with the locally-stable equilibrium. We additionally verified the qualitative sequence (stabilising Hopf, nearby fold, wide monostable interval, nearby fold, destabilising Hopf) across one-at-a-time variation of every parameter in Table~\ref{tab:params}'s literature-anchored ranges (Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}) at the level of the two Hopf points; it persists throughout the interior of those ranges and disappears at their extremes, where the system is instead uniformly stable or uniformly unstable regardless of delay --- with one structural nuance: the $r$-dependence is a bounded window, the delay-induced Hopf pair existing only for $r$ between roughly $0.008$ and $0.022$ yr$^{-1}$ at Candidate A's $\eta$ (up to $\approx0.061$--$0.062$ at $\eta=3.0$), so that for $r$ above the window --- including every real fish stock ($r\gtrsim0.2$ yr$^{-1}$) --- the equilibrium is delay-independently stable.

\subsection{Signatures of Global Bistability}
Fig.~\ref{fig:bifurcation} shows the long-run attractor reached from two different initial conditions (near-equilibrium and far-from-equilibrium) as a function of $\tau$, confirming the five-regime structure of Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}: a single large-amplitude attractor for short delays ($\tau<\tau_-$), a locally-stable equilibrium that nonetheless coexists with the large cycle for far-from-equilibrium histories in a narrow window just above $\tau_-$ (up to $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$), a wide range with the quiet equilibrium as the sole attractor, a second narrow bistable window just below $\tau_+$ (from $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$), and the large-amplitude cycle as the sole attractor for $\tau>\tau_+$. Because the two bistable windows are narrow ($\lesssim1.1$\,yr wide at Candidate A parameters) relative to the horizontal scale needed to show both Hopf points and both folds on the same axis, Fig.~\ref{fig:bifurcation} necessarily under-resolves them visually; Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} gives the precise numerical brackets. Fig.~\ref{fig:timeseries} shows explicit time series at $\tau=131.8$ years (inside the upper bistable window, between $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx131.24$ and $\tau_+\approx132.4$): the near-equilibrium trajectory remains on the quiet fixed point indefinitely, while the far-from-equilibrium trajectory is captured by the large-amplitude cycle — genuine basin-dependent bistability \cite{scheffer2003}, verified over a multi-hundred-thousand-year simulation horizon (to avoid the critical-slowing-down artifact of Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) rather than inferred from linear analysis alone or from a shorter run.

\subsection{Figures}
\begin{figure}[h]

Red: long-run $N$-envelope reached from a far-from-equilibrium/continuation-seeded initial condition. The two coincide for $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}\lesssim\tau\lesssim\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ (i.e., for $7.36\,\text{yr}\lesssim\tau\lesssim131.24\,\text{yr}$; single attractor throughout the wide monostable interval) and diverge outside two narrow windows flanking $\tau_-\approx6.88$ and $\tau_+\approx132.4$ years (genuine, but narrow, bistability); see Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} for the precise thresholds, which this schematic diagram under-resolves at this horizontal scale.}

A trajectory started near the equilibrium (blue) remains on the quiet fixed point throughout the same simulation. \section{The Four-State Core: Retaining the Active Abiotic Pool, and an Empirical Audit of the Institutional-Delay Threshold}
\label{sec:four-state-core}
This section develops the \textbf{canonical model} of this manuscript: the four-state core $(N,A^{\text{act}},Z,E)$ under the effort-saturation-corrected effort law, in which the active abiotic pool is retained as a genuine dynamical state and effort is boundary-exact ($E\in[0,E_{\max}]$). The three-state core of Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected} is the frozen-$A$ limit of this model (Section~\ref{sec:asymptotic-reduction}), retained there for the exact-cubic completeness certificate, the two-channel and thermodynamic-tether extensions, and the derivation chain; the present section is self-contained, subjects the shared headline claim --- the institutional-delay threshold $\tau_-$ --- to a direct empirical audit against real-world case studies (Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}) and is additive (no quantitative result of the three-state sections is revised here); a reader interested only in the limit system's mathematics can skip this section without loss of continuity.

\subsection{Root Cause: Why the Frozen-$A$ Reduction Cannot Be Closed at Three States}
\label{sec:four-state-rootcause}
Section~\ref{sec:frozen-A} derives the core's logistic law $S(N)=rN(1-N/K)$ as the leading-order limit of the coupled regeneration function
\[
R(N,A^{\text{act}}) = rN\Bigl(1-\frac{N}{K}\Bigr)\cdot\frac{A^{\text{act}}}{A^{\text{act}}+A_0}
\]
as $A^{\text{act}}/A_0\to\infty$, and Section~\ref{sec:frozen-A} already flags (in the paragraph beginning ``The specific gap this leaves in the core's own regeneration term'') that this frozen-$A$ limit is valid only when the active abiotic pool is not itself a binding constraint. What that earlier discussion leaves open is whether $A^{\text{act}}$ can be safely eliminated as a fast auxiliary variable via the same Tikhonov argument used elsewhere in this manuscript (Section~\ref{sec:asymptotic-reduction}), or whether it must instead be retained as a genuine slow state. This subsection answers that question directly: it must be retained.

The sensitivity of regeneration to the pool is $\partial R/\partial A^{\text{act}} = rN(1-N/K)A_0/(A^{\text{act}}+A_0)^2 \to 0$ as $A^{\text{act}}/A_0\to\infty$ --- the same limit required for the frozen-$A$ law to hold in the first place. Consequently the pool's own linearised restoring rate, $\omega_A+\partial B/\partial A^{\text{act}}$ (with the donor-limited gross uptake $B=R+\kappa_A N\cdot A^{\text{act}}/(A^{\text{act}}+A_0)$, Section~\ref{sec:abiotic}), collapses to $\omega_A$ alone in exactly the regime where the reduced stock equation is valid ($\partial[\kappa_A N\cdot A^{\text{act}}/(A^{\text{act}}+A_0)]/\partial A^{\text{act}}\to0$ in the same $A^{\text{act}}/A_0\to\infty$ limit, by the identical algebraic mechanism already shown above for $\partial R/\partial A^{\text{act}}$): the frozen-$A$ functional form and a fast-recovering pool are mutually exclusive by construction, not merely in an unfavourable corner of parameter space. Direct nonlinear simulation of the augmented system at the Table~\ref{tab:params} baseline ($\kappa_A=0.05$, $\omega_A=10^{-3}\,\text{yr}^{-1}$) confirms this is not an algebraic curiosity: the linearised relaxation timescale of $A^{\text{act}}$ is $\approx1000$ years, versus $\approx56$ years for $N$ --- the abiotic pool is the \emph{slow} variable at the model's baseline, across its entire literature-anchored $\kappa_A,\omega_A$ range (Table~\ref{tab:params}).

This finding was checked directly against primary biogeochemical literature rather than accepted on the strength of the model's own internal consistency alone. Two independent lines of evidence converge: radiocarbon-based deep-ocean ventilation-age reconstructions place the modern/preindustrial global-mean deep-ocean ventilation age at roughly $900$--$2000$ years \cite{skinner2017}, with glacial-period values running higher still; and the CENTURY soil organic matter model --- a foundational, widely validated framework for terrestrial carbon cycling \cite{parton1987} --- partitions soil carbon into a fast pool with decadal turnover and a passive pool with a turnover of $400$--$2000$ years. The physical process $\omega_A$ represents here (bidirectional exchange between the fast, bioavailable active pool and the deep or geological reservoir) corresponds to this passive/inert-pool timescale specifically, and Table~\ref{tab:params}'s own baseline and range for $\omega_A$ ($10^{-3}\,\text{yr}^{-1}$, i.e.\ a $1000$-year relaxation; range $10^{-4}$--$10^{-2}\,\text{yr}^{-1}$) sit squarely within this independently documented band. The root-cause argument therefore rests on an empirically corroborated parameter regime, not an assumed or worst-case one.

\textbf{Conclusion.} The correct asymptotic target of the reduction from the general framework is not the three-state $(N,Z,E)$ core of Section~\ref{sec:core-model} but a four-state $(N,A^{\text{act}},Z,E)$ system, with $A^{\text{act}}$ retained as a genuine dynamical state rather than eliminated as fast. Every other auxiliary compartment of the general framework --- the macroeconomic block $(K,L,A_{\text{TFP}},T)$, prices/demand $(p,Q,D)$, waste and product stocks $(W,P)$, the geological reservoir $A^{\text{geo}}$, and (at baseline/high turnover rates) the detritus pool $U$ --- continues to eliminate cleanly under the same Tikhonov argument used in Section~\ref{sec:asymptotic-reduction} and Appendix G; $A^{\text{act}}$ is the single exception, isolated by the argument above rather than by exhaustive search.

\subsection{The Four-State Core and Its Equilibrium}
\label{sec:four-state-model}
The canonical model is the four-state system under the effort-saturation-corrected effort law, Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} (Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}); the ungated four-state system is analysed first because it is the direct extension of the three-state core's equation set, and the corrected-law results --- the canonical thresholds --- follow in the same subsection. The four-state core replaces Eq.~\eqref{eq:stock-core} with the coupled pair
\begin{align}
\dot N &= R(N,A^{\text{act}}) - qEN, \label{eq:fourstate-N}\\
\dot A^{\text{act}} &= -B(N,A^{\text{act}}) + \omega_A\bigl(A^{\text{act,eq}}-A^{\text{act}}\bigr), \label{eq:fourstate-A}
\end{align}
with $R$ and $B=R+\kappa_A N\cdot A^{\text{act}}/(A^{\text{act}}+A_0)$ (the donor-limited form, Section~\ref{sec:abiotic}) and $A^{\text{act,eq}}=A^{\text{act,eq,intrinsic}}+\kappa_A K/\omega_A$ exactly as in Eq.~\eqref{eq:aeq-derived} with $D^{\text{ret}}=0$ (this four-state core has no detritus compartment, so the correction term is retained in full, unlike the general framework's own use of the same formula once detritus is included; Section~\ref{sec:variant-registry}'s variant registry, and the resolution paragraph immediately following Eq.~\eqref{eq:detritus}); the deficit and effort equations, Eqs.~\eqref{eq:Z-core}--\eqref{eq:effort-core}, are retained unchanged except that $S(N)$ is replaced by $R(N,A^{\text{act}})$ wherever it appears. \textbf{This four-state core uses the original, ungated effort equation, Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core}, not the boundary-corrected Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} of Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}}: because Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix} shows that Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core} does not enforce $E\le E_{\max}$, the four-state core inherits the same boundary violation, confirmed directly by simulation ($E(t)$ exceeds $E_{\max}=30$, reaching $\approx80$--$90$ during the sustained oscillation at short $\tau$, e.g.\ $\tau=1$--$6$ yr, at the four-state Candidate A parameters below). This does not invalidate the equilibrium, linearisation, or Hopf-threshold results reported in this section --- these are unaffected by the boundary correction (Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix} shows the corrected equation leaves $N^*,Z^*,E^*$ exactly unchanged) --- but the reported large-amplitude cycle amplitudes, oscillation periods, and fold locations ($\tau_{\mathrm{SNPO,L}}\approx7.374$~yr and $\tau_{\mathrm{SNPO,R}}\approx130.77$~yr, reported below) should be read as properties of the ungated equation specifically; the corresponding corrected-law Hopf thresholds and cycle periods are reported below, and the corrected-law fold locations have now been located by dt-converged long-horizon persistence bisection: $\tau_{\mathrm{SNPO,L}}\approx5.62$~yr and $\tau_{\mathrm{SNPO,R}}\in(64.4,64.5)$~yr (Candidate A), giving a narrow lower bistable window $(3.78,5.62)$~yr and --- in contrast to the three-state limit's narrow upper window --- a wide upper bistable window $(64.4,150.12)$~yr in which the large-amplitude orbit coexists with the stable equilibrium but which histories reach it is history-dependent: histories beginning at high stock ($N_0\gtrsim1.2N^*$) are captured for delays as short as $\tau\approx85$--$95$~yr, depleted or near-equilibrium histories ($N_0\lesssim N^*$) only for $\tau\gtrsim140$~yr, the cycle's basin of attraction contracting as the delay is reduced across the upper window. The lower-fold location is Richardson-converged in the integration step ($5.646$~yr at $\Delta t=0.05$~yr to $\approx5.621$~yr in the zero-step limit), while the upper fold's bracket $(64.4,64.5)$~yr is stable across the same refinement; both are persistence-bisection values whose collocation-grade SNPO classification, and any small-unstable-branch family near the lower fold, remain to be established. Repeating this section's analysis under Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} (the effort-saturation-corrected law), the interior equilibrium is unchanged ($N^*=89.5256$, $A^*=397.8665$, $E^*=2.0896$), the $\tau=0$ complex pair shifts from $0.000836\pm0.02838i$ to $0.000372\pm0.02765i$, the Hopf thresholds relocate to $\tau_-\approx3.7849$ yr (from $6.982$) and $\tau_+\approx150.12$ yr (from $132.27$), and the $\kappa_A$ stability threshold moves only slightly to $\approx0.001330$ (from $0.0013163$, $+1.1\%$) --- the same qualitative signature as the three-state core's corrected law (lower $\tau_-$ by $\approx46\%$, higher $\tau_+$ by $\approx13\%$, equilibrium and $\kappa_A$ threshold essentially unchanged). The large-amplitude collapse cycle persists under the corrected law throughout both windows: periods $\approx316$--$375$ yr across the lower window ($\approx375$ yr at $\tau=4$, shortening to $\approx316$ yr at the fold) and $\approx73$--$158$ yr across the upper window ($\approx158$ yr near $\tau_+$, shortening to $\approx73$ yr at its lower edge). At any interior equilibrium, $qE^*N^*=R(N^*,A^{\text{act}*})$ identically, so the deficit driving $Z$ is exactly zero regardless of the specific functional form of regeneration --- consequently $Z^*=\delta$ and the quadratic determining $E^*$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:Estar-quadratic}) are \emph{unchanged} from the three-state core: $Z^*=\ln(2)/10\approx0.0693147$, $E^*=2.089623397737668$ for Candidate A, confirmed by direct substitution and independent of the regeneration law's functional form and of the donor-limiting correction (neither $A^{\text{act}}$ nor $\kappa_A$ appears in this quadratic). \textbf{Solving the coupled pair Eqs.~\eqref{eq:fourstate-N}--\eqref{eq:fourstate-A} numerically at fixed $E^*$ under the corrected, donor-limited $B$ gives $N^*=89.52562265496681$, $A^{\text{act}*}=397.8665350723071$ for Candidate A} (Table~\ref{tab:params} baseline; both residuals vanish to machine precision at this point), a small shift relative to the values $N^*=89.52486320175798$, $A^{\text{act}*}=386.6835910864438$ obtained before the donor-limiting form: $N^*$ shifts by $\approx0.0008\%$ (effectively unchanged, since $A^{\text{act}*}\gg A_0=0.01K$ at both the old and new equilibrium, so the donor-limiting factor is close to $1$ there in either case) while $A^{\text{act}*}$ shifts by $\approx2.9\%$ (the abiotic-pool equilibrium is more sensitive to the correction, since it depends on the donor-limiting factor directly rather than only through its small effect on $N^*$'s own equation). \textbf{This equilibrium shift has been propagated through the rest of this section's numerical pipeline, and the recomputed values are reported below}: the eigenvalues, Hopf thresholds $\tau_-,\tau_+$, and SNPO folds are recomputed under the donor-limited equilibrium (Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}); the shifts are small for the local quantities and the lower fold, while the upper fold is corrected substantially (see below).

Linearising the four-state system (finite-difference Jacobian, cross-checked against analytic partial derivatives) gives eigenvalues at $\tau=0$ of $\{-0.28350455,\ -0.00103152,\ 0.00083623\pm0.02837932i\}$ for Candidate A (at the donor-limited equilibrium; the corresponding values at the pre-correction equilibrium, $\{-0.28350371,\ -0.00100359,\ 0.00083660\pm0.02837824i\}$, are reproduced exactly by the same computation), confirming instability at zero delay, matching direct nonlinear DDE simulation. Repeating the full Hopf/SNPO pipeline of Sections~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected} on this four-state characteristic equation at the donor-limited equilibrium gives $\tau_-=6.982022$~yr (from $6.985285$), $\tau_+=132.272044$~yr (from $132.268712$), $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}\approx7.374$~yr, and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx130.77$~yr for Candidate A --- the two Hopf points and the lower fold shift by less than $1\%$, while the upper fold lies at $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\in[130.770,130.771]$, placed by carry-continuation of the converged cycle from $\tau=132.2$: no large-amplitude cycle exists at $\tau\in[118.9,130]$ at any horizon up to $1.5\times10^6$\,yr (a far-from-equilibrium history at $\tau=118.9$ sustains a near-cycle excursion for only a short initial interval before collapsing to the equilibrium). The four-state upper bistable window is therefore $(130.77,132.27)\approx1.5$~yr wide, comparable to (not an order of magnitude wider than) the three-state core's corresponding window $(131.24,132.4)$, and $\tau_-$ remains essentially unchanged from the three-state result (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}). For Candidate B, the four-state core's Hopf thresholds at the donor-limited equilibrium are $\tau_-\approx6.251$~yr and $\tau_+\approx76.33$~yr (characteristic-equation root-tracking under the ungated effort law, the same pipeline validated against the Candidate A values above; the next imaginary-axis crossing lies at $\tau\approx159.3$~yr), with the equilibrium weakly unstable at $\tau=0$ (rightmost pair $+0.00189\pm0.04096i$) and the stable window $\tau\in(6.25,76.33)$~yr; the equilibrium itself is $N^*\approx90.95$, $A^*\approx351.37$, $E^*\approx1.8056$. Candidate B's fold locations for the four-state core are not determined here. The $\kappa_A$-dependent stability structure and a dedicated dt-convergence check additionally show that the four-state core's large-amplitude attractor is a clean limit cycle, with no measurable quasi-periodic modulation (peak-height variation shrinks as $O(dt^2)$ under refinement, consistent with pure discretisation error) --- a cleaner result on this point than the three-state core's upper-window attractor, which a fixed-step and adaptive-integrator cross-check confirms is a period-1 limit cycle (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}).

\paragraph{A second bifurcation parameter: the abiotic turnover rate $\kappa_A$ itself.} Beyond the delay $\tau$, the four-state core has an internal stability structure in $\kappa_A$ (the abiotic turnover rate governing the donor-limited gross uptake $B=R+\kappa_A N\cdot A^{\text{act}}/(A^{\text{act}}+A_0)$) that has no analogue in the three-state core, since $\kappa_A$ does not appear in $S(N)$ at all. The equilibrium branch is continuous across the full literature-anchored range $\kappa_A\in[0,0.5]$ (Table~\ref{tab:params}), with $N^*$ rising from $\approx24$ at $\kappa_A\to0$ to $\approx89.5$ at $\kappa_A\gtrsim0.05$, but its linear stability at $\tau=0$ flips sign at $\kappa_A\approx0.0013163$ at the donor-limited equilibrium (the pre-correction value, $0.0038945$, is reproduced exactly by the same computation; the correction shifts the threshold by $-66\%$), confirmed at this specific point via three independent methods (finite-difference Jacobian, characteristic-equation root-finding, and direct nonlinear simulation on both sides of the threshold). At one representative point below this threshold ($\kappa_A=0.001$), direct simulation across $\tau\in[0,300]$~yr shows the equilibrium stable for every delay tested --- i.e., delay-independent stability, in contrast to the delay-dependent Hopf structure that holds above the threshold. Delay-independent stability holds across the full sub-threshold range rather than at a single point: a ten-point grid of $\kappa_A$ values from $2\times10^{-5}$ to $1.3\times10^{-3}$, each simulated at six delays spanning $\tau\in[0,300]$ yr (sixty $(\kappa_A,\tau)$ pairs, $2\times10^4$-yr horizons from a 2\% near-equilibrium perturbation), shows the equilibrium stable in every case, with decay to $|N-N^*|<1$ in all runs and to $\sim10^{-12}$ at the original $\kappa_A=0.001$ point on $6\times10^4$-yr horizons; a fine sweep of 1798 physical-branch equilibria over $\kappa_A\in[0,0.5]$ confirms that $\tau=0$ stability flips exactly once, at the bisected threshold $\kappa_A=0.001316298$, with $N^*$ rising from $\approx24$ at $\kappa_A\to0$ to $\approx89.5$ at $\kappa_A\gtrsim0.05$. Above the threshold, the two Hopf thresholds have been tracked continuously across $\kappa_A\in[0.0014,0.5]$ (sixty points, each yielding a full Hopf pair): $\tau_-$ falls from $\approx17.5$ yr just above the threshold to $\approx6.9$ yr at large $\kappa_A$, and $\tau_+$ from $\approx280$ yr to $\approx132.4$ yr, so the safe window narrows from $\approx262$ yr to $\approx125$ yr. Continuous fold tracking versus $\kappa_A$ has likewise been completed (nineteen $\kappa_A$ points over $[0.0015,0.5]$, both folds located by multi-seed carry-continuation bisection on $8\times10^5$-yr horizons): both folds move smoothly, the lower fold falling from $\approx11.4$ yr just above the threshold (at $\kappa_A\approx0.0039$) to $\approx7.3$--$7.4$ yr for $\kappa_A\gtrsim0.02$ (at $\kappa_A=0.085$: $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}\approx7.35$, matching the $\approx7.37$ reported at $\kappa_A=0.05$), and the upper fold converging from $\approx148.5$ yr near the threshold to $\approx130.8$--$131.1$ yr for $\kappa_A\gtrsim0.03$ (at $\kappa_A=0.085$: $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx130.77$, matching the reported $130.77$ at $\kappa_A=0.05$); the lower window stays narrow ($\approx0.3$--$2$ yr) and the upper window $\approx1.2$--$3$ yr, with the monostable interval $\approx122$--$124$ yr throughout. The two folds approach the threshold from above as $\kappa_A\to0.0013^+$ (the window structure collapses at the stability threshold itself), and the characterisation of further high-delay imaginary-axis crossings beyond $\tau_+$ at $\kappa_A=0.05$ (located at $\tau\approx270.2476,274.4528,416.6335$ yr, all Hopf with periods $142$/$263$ yr, rightmost real part everywhere marginal $10^{-6}$--$10^{-4}$, effectively stable on $2\times10^4$-yr horizons) and a sensitivity sweep over $\omega_A$, $A_0$, and $A^{\text{act,eq,intrinsic}}$ has likewise been completed (verified in \texttt{verify\_fourstate\_sensitivity.py}): varying each across its full literature range (Table~\ref{tab:params}) at the donor-limited equilibrium shifts the thresholds only mildly --- $\tau_-\in[6.89,7.42]$ yr, $\tau_+\in[131.8,132.4]$ yr, the lower fold $\in[7.3,7.65]$ yr, and the upper fold $\in[130.3,131.1]$ yr across $\omega_A\in[10^{-4},10^{-2}]$, $A_0\in[0.5,5]$, and $A^{\text{act,eq,intrinsic}}\in[20,100]$ --- and the equilibrium remains unstable at $\tau=0$ throughout the swept ranges (rightmost complex pair with positive real part, as at the baseline; the stabilising Hopf $\tau_-$ accordingly exists in every case). The four-state core's bifurcation structure is therefore robust to the three remaining abiotic parameters, whose baseline values were not varied in the base analysis. The further high-delay imaginary-axis crossings beyond $\tau_+$ at $\kappa_A=0.05$ have been characterised: the crossings at $\tau\approx270.2476$, $274.4528$, and $416.6335$ yr (periods $142$ and $263$ yr) are all Hopf, but the rightmost characteristic root's real part is everywhere marginal ($10^{-6}$--$10^{-4}$), and direct $1\%$-perturbation simulation shows the equilibrium effectively stable on $2\times10^4$-yr horizons in the high-delay windows --- real in the characteristic equation but so weakly unstable they do not manifest, confirming and quantifying the ``marginal'' character noted above; only $\tau$ just above $\tau_+$ lands on the large-amplitude collapse cycle. Subject to that caveat, the qualitative picture is that above the $\kappa_A$ threshold, institutional delay is \emph{stabilising} over most of the physically relevant range ($\tau\in(6.99,132.27)$, which contains the model's stated $\tau\in[2,25]$~yr range for $\tau>6.99$), with the destabilising threshold $\tau_+$ lying far outside real institutional timescales, consistent with the picture already established for the three-state core (Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}).

\paragraph{Near-invariance of $\tau_-$ under the four-state extension.} The near-invariance of $\tau_-$ under this extension is itself informative: it means the stabilising Hopf threshold identified as the model's headline result (Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}) --- whose magnitude is numerically comparable to documented governance lags, without case-level validation --- is not an artifact of the frozen-$A$ simplification, but persists once the single compartment identified as improperly eliminated is correctly restored. This four-state analysis is accordingly treated throughout the remainder of this manuscript as a robustness check on Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected} rather than as a replacement for them: all subsequent discussion, policy implications, and falsifiable predictions continue to refer to the three-state core's own thresholds unless explicitly noted, per Section~\ref{sec:falsifiable-predictions}'s own tiered evidentiary structure.

\paragraph{Oscillation period is pinned by ecological, not institutional, timescales.} Direct RK4 integration of the four-state core (Eqs.~\eqref{eq:fourstate-N}--\eqref{eq:fourstate-A} together with Eqs.~\eqref{eq:Z-core}--\eqref{eq:effort-core}) at Candidate A parameters, across $\tau\in\{0.25,1.0,3.0,5.0,6.5\}$~yr (i.e.\ throughout the sustained-oscillation regime $\tau<\tau_-$), gives a tail oscillation period of $\approx 250$--$390$ years at every tested $\tau$ (e.g.\ $\tau=0.25$: period $374.1$~yr; $\tau=1.0$: $378.1$~yr; $\tau=3.0$: $385.6$~yr; $\tau=6.5$: $260.7$--$260.8$~yr; all measured via peak-finding on the converged tail of a $2\times10^4$-year run at $dt=0.05$~yr), essentially independent of the institutional-response delay $\tau$ itself. A dedicated timescale-rescaling test --- uniformly raising the intrinsic growth rate $r$ from the baseline $0.02\,\text{yr}^{-1}$ toward literal cod-like values ($0.1$--$0.4\,\text{yr}^{-1}$), with $q,\eta,\delta_0,\omega_A,\kappa_A,1/\tau_m$ scaled proportionally to preserve the equilibrium's dimensionless structure --- does \emph{not} compress this period into a realistic fisheries-management range (years to a decade); instead it destroys the oscillatory regime altogether, driving the system to near-total depletion or divergence (tail amplitude approaching the full stock range, no stable periodic orbit recoverable) rather than to a faster cycle. This is a structural property of the model, not an artifact of a single parameterisation: oscillation frequency in this model is pinned by the resource's own regeneration rate $r$ and the deficit-memory filter $\tau_m$, not by the institutional-response delay $\tau$ whose threshold value is the model's central claim. Consequently, the model's oscillatory regime is intrinsically a centuries-long phenomenon at any biologically realistic parameterisation of $r$ --- a point taken up directly in Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt} below, where it emerges as one of two root causes for why no clean real-world confirmation of this regime has been found.

\subsection{An Empirical Audit of the $\tau_-$ Threshold Against Real-World Case Studies}
\label{sec:empirical-hunt}
Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected} identifies $\tau_-$ (rather than $\tau_+$) as the model's headline threshold, on the strength of an aggregate, cross-study empirical distribution of institutional response lags \cite{hocherman2025} indicating that delays of the right order of magnitude exist in real governance; this section tests whether that magnitude-comparability can be upgraded to case-level support. That distributional check establishes that delays of the right \emph{order of magnitude} exist in real environmental governance. It does not, by itself, establish that any \emph{specific} real resource system's institutional delay, stock trajectory, and outcome (sustained oscillation versus convergence) can be matched against the model's mechanism at the level of an individual case study. This subsection reports a systematic attempt to find such a case, carried out as a dedicated empirical exercise after the mathematical analysis above was complete, and its result: despite an extensive, multi-round search spanning more than thirty candidate systems across fisheries, aquaculture, groundwater, surface water, rangeland, wildlife harvest, forestry, and financial/commodity markets, \textbf{no candidate survives close scrutiny as a clean, unconfounded empirical test of either side of the $\tau_-$ claim.} We report this as a primary empirical finding of this work, not as an incidental limitation, and identify its root causes below. The empirically testable content is therefore not the century-scale cycle itself but the rate-regime diagnostic --- whether a real system sits inside the oscillatory band and at what rate it is approaching its thresholds --- which the critical-slowing-down and review-interval signatures (Sections~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} and the periodic-review paragraph of Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}) are built to detect.

\paragraph{Screening protocol.} Each candidate was screened against four criteria, applied with escalating rigor (individual-station rather than aggregated data; primary-source retrieval rather than secondary summary) whenever an initial pass appeared promising: (a) genuine continuous institutional feedback, of the kind the model's effort equation represents, rather than a one-time fixed cap or ban; (b) a short, dateable institutional response or implementation lag, estimable independently of the outcome being explained; (c) no major independent confound --- environmental, climatic, biological, or structural --- capable of producing the same outcome pattern by a different mechanism; and (d) individual-resource, rather than aggregated or multi-stock, primary data.

\paragraph{Candidates initially accepted, then reclassified on deeper scrutiny.} Three cases initially appeared to satisfy all four criteria on a first pass and were subsequently subjected to the same station-level, primary-source verification: the Sheridan-6 Local Enhanced Management Area (Kansas groundwater, continuous volumetric feedback since 2013), the Bangkok aquifer (continuous groundwater-pumping regulation since the 1980s), and the La Mancha Oriental Aquifer (Spain, continuous monitoring and sanctioning via the JCRMO since the 1990s, court-recognised since 2012). All three were reclassified as confounded once individual-well or individual-station records were retrieved directly. The Thomas County index well underlying the Sheridan-6 case (continuously logged since 2007, KGS annual reports) shows a decline-recovery-decline pattern, not a clean monotone response, and a regression against Kansas statewide annual precipitation explains roughly half the variance ($R^2\approx0.47$); separately, Sheridan-6's control instrument is a fixed five-year volumetric cap, not a continuously adjusting effort variable, a structural mismatch with the model's mechanism independent of the confound. Bangkok's pumping volume is genuinely monotonic post-1999 \cite{bremard2022}, but its outcome variable (subsidence rate) shows a documented secondary undulation --- central Bangkok overshot into uplift by 2007--2010, outlying provinces then resumed subsidence as late as 2017--2019, followed by a 2021--2022 partial recovery explicitly attributed by the primary literature to a rainfall anomaly rather than further pumping cuts \cite{ahmed2024} --- together with an independently documented soil-consolidation lag decoupling groundwater recovery from surface response. La Mancha Oriental's extraction record shows a documented 2019--2023 upturn (averaging $312\,\text{hm}^3/\text{yr}$, versus $<300\,\text{hm}^3/\text{yr}$ for 2007--2019) attributed by the primary source to a structural shift toward water-intensive woody crops and to climate-driven evapotranspiration increases, not to the institutional feedback loop \cite{hoogesteger2025}; much of the aquifer's earlier apparent stabilisation is separately attributable to an engineered surface-water substitution project (Júcar and Tajo-Segura transfers) layered on top of, rather than arising from, the institutional feedback loop under test.

\paragraph{The reclassification is not uniform in severity.} None of the four deep-checked cases (Sheridan-6, Bangkok, La Mancha, Iceland cod) survives as a \emph{completely} clean test, but the confounds are not all of comparable weight. Bangkok and La Mancha remain the \emph{closest} approximations to confirming cases: in both, the institutional signal is real, large, and plausibly still net-dominant over the confound, with the complications identified above better described as genuine secondary undulations layered on top of a still-largely-intact institutional story than as alternative explanations that supplant it entirely. Iceland cod, by contrast, carries a confound (cohort resonance) that operates on the same timescale as the very cycle being explained, compounded by an unresolved, order-of-magnitude ($15$--$25\times$) period mismatch against the model's own prediction --- a materially more severe and more structurally damaging problem than a secondary undulation on an otherwise-dominant trend. We record this distinction explicitly rather than collapsing all four cases into an undifferentiated ``confounded'' bucket, since it means the confirming side of the search came closer to succeeding than the oscillation side did, even though neither survives as fully clean.

\paragraph{The oscillation-side candidate.} The single most promising candidate for the model's opposite prediction --- that $\tau<\tau_-$ produces sustained oscillation --- was the Icelandic cod fishery under its 1995 harvest control rule (a continuously adjusting quota with an implementation lag of $\tau\approx0.2$--$0.3$~yr, deep inside the oscillation regime for both candidate parameter sets). Direct analysis of the full 1955--2023 ICES stock-assessment time series (stock cod.27.5a) shows genuine, persistent large-amplitude fluctuation in the post-1995 era (coefficient of variation $0.387$) rather than convergence, qualitatively consistent with the model's prediction at short $\tau$. Quantitatively, however, the observed cycle period ($\sim10$--$15$ years, consistent with the well-documented ``cohort resonance'' phenomenon in gadoid fisheries) is $15$--$25\times$ shorter than the four-state core's own predicted period at these parameters ($\approx250$--$390$ years; Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}), and the timescale-rescaling test described above shows this mismatch cannot be closed by re-parameterising $r$ to a literal cod-like growth rate without destroying the oscillatory regime entirely. The fishery is additionally confounded by an independent, well-documented cohort-resonance mechanism operating on the same timescale as the observed cycle. A parallel check against the Icelandic haddock fishery (same institutional mechanism, adopted 2013, stock had.27.5a) found a substantially \emph{lower} coefficient of variation in the post-implementation era (CV $=0.143$ versus cod's $0.387$) despite haddock's independently documented \emph{higher} intrinsic recruitment variability --- a result that complicates, without resolving, the cod finding, since it is difficult to reconcile with a shared institutional-oscillation mechanism common to both species under the same harvest-control rule. The stage and sampled-governance refinements of the structural-robustness and periodic-review paragraphs below, however, reject the institutional reading at cod's actual lag: at $\tau\approx0.25$~yr the equilibrium is stable under both continuous delay and periodic-review governance (and under the stage extension at every review interval tested), while the cohort mechanism's own predicted period is $2$--$3\times$ the observed one. Cod's residual fluctuation is therefore better read as cohort-driven than institutionally driven, superseding the period-mismatch framing above rather than merely complicating it.

\paragraph{The Peruvian anchoveta as the sharpest case for selection-bias cause (i).} The clearest single illustration that the negative result is structural rather than incidental is the Peruvian anchoveta (\emph{Engraulis ringens}), the world's largest single-species fishery. It meets every eligibility criterion except the confound gate: a fast intrinsic growth rate ($r\approx0.7$--$1.0$~yr$^{-1}$, above the model's instability window at even the maximal effort response), a documented scarcity-mobilisation lag on the order of $4$--$7$~yr, and a dramatic, well-monitored boom-bust history --- the 1970 peak ($16.4$~Mt reconstructed landings), the 1972 collapse, and repeated crashes in $1982$--$83$ and $1997$--$98$. It fails the confound gate decisively: spectral analysis of the reconstructed 1950--2019 catch series finds its only robust periodicity at $\approx3.7$~yr --- the El Ni\~no recurrence period --- and cross-correlation with the real NOAA Oceanic Ni\~no Index shows $|r|\approx0.31$ with ENSO leading the catch, while the model predicts anchoveta to be delay-independently stable at its $r$. The single case that best satisfies every other condition is therefore dominated by the largest climate driver on Earth; the same confound-dominance is why, under the sampled-governance analysis of the structural-robustness paragraph, the model predicts stability for Peru's sub-annual management reviews ($T_r<1$~yr) rather than an institutional cycle. This case makes selection-bias cause (i) literal: visibility (and thus record length) is itself correlated with the presence of a fast, non-institutional confound.

\paragraph{Two recurring, structurally distinct failure modes.} Across the full search --- which additionally examined, and rejected on the criteria above, orange roughy and other New Zealand quota-managed fisheries, Wisconsin's ceded-territory tribal walleye fishery (confounded by documented climate-driven recruitment failure \cite{hansen2018} operating independently of the harvest-quota feedback loop), the Edwards Aquifer critical-period management system, Cape Town's 2018 water crisis, the Bering Sea snow crab collapse, the South African hake Operational Management Procedure, Louisiana's alligator harvest program (nest counts increase monotonically throughout the program's history and never enter an oscillatory regime), the Yellowstone bison removal program (driven by interagency litigation rather than a continuous biomass-triggered rule), Murray-Darling Basin seasonal water allocations, and the Sea of Galilee's threshold-triggered pumping rule (progressively superseded by desalination) --- every rejected candidate falls into one of two recurring patterns:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Confounded by an independent driver operating on a comparable or faster timescale.} Nearly every short-institutional-lag system with a long, high-quality monitoring record is confounded by rainfall, ocean-climate regime shifts, cohort resonance, marine heatwaves, or a parallel engineered intervention (buyouts, water transfers, desalination) introduced precisely because the resource became visible as a crisis. This is not simply bad luck in case selection: the same mechanism that produces the long, well-monitored record required to test the model (sustained public and scientific attention following a dramatic event) is itself correlated with the presence of a confound, since gradual, purely delay-driven cycles rarely generate the kind of alarm that funds multi-decade monitoring programs in the first place.
\item \textbf{The genuinely unconfounded delay-oscillators found are structurally outside the model's scope.} The clearest unconfounded real-world examples of delay-driven oscillation identified in this search --- the cattle/hog cycle \cite{rosen1994} and electricity-generation capacity-investment cycles --- are produced-capital-stock systems with no autonomously regenerating natural resource distinct from the controlling investment/breeding decision, collapsing the stock/effort separation the present model is built around.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{A structural root cause beyond selection bias.} Independent of the confound pattern above, Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}'s timescale-rescaling finding identifies a second, more fundamental obstacle: at any biologically realistic regeneration rate $r$, the model's own oscillation period is measured in centuries, not years or decades. Almost no human resource-management institution has held a stable structure (same $\tau$, same monitoring regime, same governing rule) for even one full period of that length, let alone the multiple periods needed to distinguish a genuinely sustained oscillation from a slow transient or from low-frequency noise; most real institutional records span a few decades to, at most, a century. A perfectly unconfounded case, were one to exist, would very likely still be unable to confirm or refute the $\tau_-$ claim purely because of this record-length mismatch, for the base and four-state cores and late-maturing stocks whose model-implied oscillation periods are centuries long; the structural-robustness and periodic-review paragraphs below show how fast-maturing stocks under sampled governance escape this barrier. This reframes the empirical programme's central obstacle: it is not merely that confounds are common, but that the joint requirement of (i) a long-enough, stable-enough institutional record and (ii) freedom from a competing fast driver is close to self-contradictory for the specific regime this model's oscillatory branch describes. A further, related observation is that real institutions confronting visible scarcity typically respond by changing their instrument set entirely (introducing buyouts, engineered transfers, or new infrastructure) rather than holding a single continuous response gain $\tau$ fixed and observing the outcome --- which is itself evidence that $\tau$, as a stable, isolable, empirically fixed parameter of a real governance system over a period of decades, is closer to a necessary modelling idealisation than to a routinely observable empirical quantity.

\paragraph{The structural obstacle is a property of the model class, not of the specific core we chose, and one extension restores testability for a specific stock class.}
Put plainly, the difficulty above is not that we chose unlucky equations: in the base core and its four-state variant the delay-instability window in the regeneration rate $r$ is a bounded interval $(\approx0.008,\approx0.022)$~yr$^{-1}$ at Candidate~A's effort response, up to $(\approx0.007,\approx0.061)$~yr$^{-1}$ at the maximal response $\eta=3.0$, and every real fish stock ($r\approx0.2$--$1.5$~yr$^{-1}$) sits an order of magnitude above it. To test whether this confinement is an artifact of the core's simplicity, we added, one at a time, the two structural ingredients a skeptical reader would most likely name --- (i) a Droop-style nutrient/quota limitation and (ii) stage structure with a maturation delay --- each preserving the same interior equilibrium ($N^*=89.55$, $Z^*=0.069$, $E^*=2.090$), so that the comparison is clean; the pipeline was validated by reproducing the manuscript's own $\tau_-=3.666$~yr and $\tau_+=150.36$~yr at $r=0.02$ and the window bounds above (\texttt{droop\_test.py}, \texttt{stage\_r\_window.py}, \texttt{stage\_decomp2.py}). The two extensions behave differently, and the difference is itself informative.

\textbf{(i) Nutrient/quota coupling (Droop) is a clean negative.} Adding an external nutrient pool $S$ and an internal quota $q$ with the quota-limited growth law $\mu(q)=r\,(1-q_{\min}/q)$ (so that $r$ remains the species' maximal rate) does not move the window: its upper edge stays $\le0.023$~yr$^{-1}$ at $\eta=0.914$ and $\le0.071$~yr$^{-1}$ at $\eta=3.0$ across four variants spanning rich to very-lean, slow nutrient pools, and no variant shows a single Hopf crossing at any $r\ge0.2$~yr$^{-1}$. The reason is structural, not parametric: the quota's self-relaxation is exactly $-r$ (verified eigenvalues), and the nutrient pool's relaxation grows with the throughput $r\,q^* N^*$, so a growth-coupled pool is slow ($50$--$127$~yr) precisely where the base model is already unstable and fast ($\le\sim5$~yr) at fish-like $r$. A nutrient pool can be slow at large $r$ only if its exchange rate is independent of the stock's growth --- which is exactly the four-state core's $\omega_A$-type abiotic exchange --- and that design \emph{narrows} the window (to $\approx(0.0074,0.0247)$~yr$^{-1}$). In plain terms: one cannot make a fast-growing fish look slow by coupling it to food, because the food supply itself turns over faster the more the fish grows.

\textbf{(ii) Stage structure with a maturation delay is three-part, and it restores testability in one stock class.} Using the delayed-recruitment (maturation-delay) form --- the same class as the laboratory blowfly oscillator cited as this model's mechanism evidence \cite{gurney1980} --- the result is three-part, and we report all three parts rather than a single verdict:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Late-maturing stocks ($g\ge5$~yr): the fast-$r$ prediction survives.} No institutional crossings exist at $r\ge0.6$~yr$^{-1}$ for $g=5,10$~yr at either $\eta$ (the highest-$r$ institutional window at $g=5$ tops out near $r\approx0.33$). The decadal oscillations such stocks show are the \emph{biological} delayed-recruitment (cohort) mechanism, already present at zero institutional delay (e.g.\ $r=0.5$, $g=5$: period $\approx20$~yr) --- precisely the cohort-resonance periods identified above as the confound in the Iceland-cod case.
\item \textbf{Slow $r$: the centuries-long barrier is reinforced.} Any maturation delay $g\ge1$~yr at $r=0.02$~yr$^{-1}$ already makes the system oscillate at zero institutional delay (period $\approx250$--$360$~yr; $g=5$: $P=358.8$~yr, stock swinging $28$--$95$, physically bounded). The slow-$r$ system is not even quietly stable once recruitment is lagged; the institutional-delay question is moot there, strengthening the record-length argument of the root cause above.
\item \textbf{Short maturation delays ($g=1$--$2$~yr) relocate the institutional window into fish range.} For fast-maturing, fast-growing stocks the window reappears on a common locus $r\,g\approx1.5$--$1.6$ for the $g=1$--$5$ bands reported here: at $g=2$~yr the band is $r\in(0.77,0.81)$~yr$^{-1}$ at $\eta=0.914$ (widening to $(0.71,0.86)$ at $\eta=3.0$), with a $\tau$-window of roughly $(2.6,7.8)$~yr --- inside the documented governance-lag distribution \cite{hocherman2025} --- and a period of $\approx8$~yr ($r=0.80$, $g=2$, $\tau=5.5$: $P=8.04$~yr, amplitude $12.4$, dt-converged); at $g=1$~yr the band sits at $r\approx1.54$--$1.61$~yr$^{-1}$ ($\eta=3.0$; narrower, $r\approx1.565$--$1.585$, at $\eta=0.914$) with a $\tau$-window $(1.6,3.5)$~yr and period $\approx4$~yr. The same locus contains the cod-class middle band ($g=5$: $r\approx0.28$--$0.33$, $\tau$-window $\approx(9.9,20.3)$~yr, period $\approx17$~yr); as established in the periodic-review paragraph below, this continuous-delay band does not survive realistic sampled governance. These bands were located by the linear two-delay characteristic criterion, verified nonlinearly (dt-converged RK4) at representative points, and mapped at finite parameter resolution; they have not been collocation-classified, and their widths are parameter-sensitive.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Periodic-review (sampled) governance: the mechanism's testable form, and the cod-band's fate.} Real multi-year governance is not a continuous delay but a review interval: effort is held between reviews and adjusted against a sampled observation of the deficit signal (sample-and-hold), which recovers the continuous $\tau=0$ system as the review interval $T_r\to0$. Under this form, verified by dt-converged integration: annual review ($T_r=1$~yr, the modal small-pelagic governance) is stable at every $\eta$ tested --- matching the observed quiescence of annual-managed small pelagics and confirmed cross-sectionally (no robust spectral peak in the predicted institutional bands among 42 annual-managed RAM stocks; \cite{ricard2012} --- e.g.\ Baltic sprat (SPRAT22-32), whose SSB variance is low-frequency/regime-dominated, CV $0.40$, rather than institutional-cyclic); the anchovy-class window relocates to $T_r\approx3$--$4$~yr with a $\approx4$-yr cycle in biomass, robust to $30\%$ multiplicative assessment error (the $T_r=2$ edge is amplitude-weak); the sprat-class window relocates to $T_r\approx6$--$12$~yr with an $\approx8$-yr biomass cycle; and the cod-class middle band disappears --- the equilibrium is stable for every review interval $T_r\in[1,20]$~yr --- so that band is an artifact of the continuous-delay idealisation, not a property of periodic institutions. The institutional signal itself is carried not by biomass but by effort and quota utilisation: in the oscillatory cells biomass swings only $1$--$2\%$ of $N^*$ while effort swings $80$--$240\%$ of $E^*$, and the effort oscillation runs at a substantially longer period than the biomass cycle ($\approx12$~yr for the anchovy class versus $4$~yr in biomass; $\approx60$~yr for the sprat class versus $8$~yr, roughly the review interval times the biomass period). The effort oscillation also grows to full amplitude only over $\sim10^3$~yr, so on practical observation horizons its spectrum is dominated by the growth transient; a spectral power analysis of the effort signal (Lomb-Scargle band power against a per-series AR(1) red-noise null, the same test used for the RAM cross-section) finds the sprat-class signal detectable on $100$--$200$~yr horizons only at low noise (power $1.0$ at $\sigma=0.1$, falling to $0.24$--$0.58$ at $\sigma=0.3$) and the anchovy-class signal effectively undetectable (power $0.02$--$0.14$), and an effort-based cross-section over the same 42 annual-managed RAM stocks finds no robust peak in the $4$--$8$~yr or $12$--$60$~yr effort bands. Switching observables from biomass to effort therefore does not rescue detectability: the effort signal is larger in amplitude but longer in period and slower to grow, so detection on realistic records remains marginal. The same sampled-governance mechanism extends to slow-regenerating systems (forests, aquifers): for regeneration rates $r\in(0.01,0.05)$ yr$^{-1}$ and responsive review intervals $T_r\lesssim20$--$30$~yr the state variable itself oscillates with large amplitude ($60$--$500\%$ of $N^*$, growing with $r$ and peaking at intermediate $T_r$; the upper figure reflects near-basal-extinction swings exceeding $N^*$), with three stability boundaries --- $r\lesssim0.007$ yr$^{-1}$ (below the window's low edge), $T_r\gtrsim30$--$50$~yr, and $r=0.05$ yr$^{-1}$ at large $T_r$ (oscillating at short review intervals but stabilising beyond $T_r\approx30$). The oscillation periods are centuries, so the field test there is not spectral but qualitative: a responsive multi-year management plan adjusted against measured changes in the resource is predicted to risk century-scale boom-bust in extraction, whereas a frozen multi-year plan with fixed limits is predicted stable --- a falsifiable, decades-scale comparison across real basins managed under the two institutional archetypes. Assessment noise does not induce oscillation at annual review (zero false positives at every error level tested), so the annual-versus-review discrimination is not an artifact of the clean deterministic model. A governance screen of primary management sources finds no responsive multi-year review system among real small pelagics: the multi-year schedules that exist are fixed or discontinuous rather than deficit-responsive --- the GFCM Adriatic small-pelagic plan's predetermined 2022--2026 reduction path, data-limited stocks whose TACs are simply held between sparse assessments, and the North-East Atlantic coastal-state quota deadlocks --- which is why the predicted $T_r\approx3$--$4$~yr window is currently untested rather than falsified.

\textbf{What this does and does not establish.} The fast-$r$ ``delay-independent stability'' prediction is \emph{not} universal: it survives Droop coupling and long maturation delays ($g\ge5$~yr), but short maturation delays restore the mechanism inside fish range. For fast-maturing, fast-growing stocks --- sprat-class ($r\approx0.8$, $g\approx2$) and anchovy-class ($r\approx1.6$, $g\approx1$) --- the model now predicts sustained \emph{decadal} institutional oscillation for implementation lags of roughly $2$--$8$~yr (periods $\approx8$ and $\approx4$~yr respectively), a claim that is falsifiable in principle and unavailable from the base or four-state cores. This is the sharpest new empirical avenue opened by these tests, and it narrows, rather than removes, the ``multi-century, untestable'' obstacle: that obstacle applies to the base and four-state cores and to late-maturing stocks, not to this stock class. The Droop negative and the $r\,g\approx1.5$--$1.6$ regularity are robustness facts; the short-delay bands are flagged, not claimed as closed (finite parameter resolution, no collocation classification, and cohort-resonance periods overlap the prediction and must be discriminated explicitly in any candidate case). The sampled-governance form of the preceding paragraph turns the anchovy-class band into a concrete, noise-robust prediction (review interval $T_r\approx3$--$4$~yr, $\approx4$-yr cycle) and removes the cod-class middle band from the empirically live set.

\paragraph{An experimentally-grounded qualification: the underlying mechanism class is not merely theoretical.} Although no field case survives as a clean test of the model's specific institutional-delay claim, the broader class of bifurcation the model relies on --- a system transitioning between a stable equilibrium and a sustained oscillation as a single parameter crosses a critical value, without requiring any external environmental forcing --- has been directly and repeatedly demonstrated under controlled laboratory conditions, free of the confounds that defeat every field case above. Costantino, Cushing, Dennis and Desharnais experimentally manipulated the adult mortality rate of a laboratory flour beetle (\emph{Tribolium castaneum}) population and observed transitions from a stable fixed point, through periodic cycles, to aperiodic oscillation, matching a fitted stage-structured population model's bifurcation predictions with no environmental confound by construction \cite{costantino1995}. Gurney, Blythe and Nisbet's classic delay-differential model of Nicholson's laboratory blowfly population \cite{gurney1980} is, more specifically, a case in which a \emph{delay} (there, the insect's generation time) is the literal, named mechanism producing an observed, quantitatively matched oscillation. Moxnes's renewable-resource management experiments \cite{moxnes1998}, in which human subjects with genuine, commons-problem-free property rights over a simulated fishery still produced systematic $\sim60\%$ capacity overshoot attributable to misperception of stock-and-flow delays, provide a third, more directly institutional line of evidence that delay-blindness of exactly the kind this model formalises is a real driver of resource-system overshoot under controlled conditions. None of these three literatures is a structural match for the present claim --- the first two lack an institutional/regulatory actor altogether, and the third does not sweep a delay parameter across a stable/oscillating threshold in the way $\tau_-$ requires --- but together they establish that the qualitative mechanism (parameter-crossing bifurcation between equilibrium and sustained oscillation in a delay- or stock-and-flow-structured system) is empirically real and observable, even though no real-world institutional case has yet been found clean enough to test the model's specific quantitative threshold.

\paragraph{What the empirical search establishes, and what it leaves open.} The claim that $\tau_-$ sits within a documented distribution of institutional response lags is unaffected by the search reported here (Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}). What the search over more than thirty real-world systems yields is a \textbf{characterized negative result}: no clean, unconfounded test of the fast threshold exists, for two structural reasons rather than incidental ones --- selection bias in long-monitored records (visibility is itself correlated with the presence of a fast, non-institutional confound) and the record-length mismatch between the multi-century periods the base and four-state cores predict at biologically realistic regeneration rates and the length of any real institutional record (Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}). The search therefore does not falsify the mechanism; it identifies why the base- and four-state-core regime is empirically intractable, and it supplies the form under which the mechanism becomes observable: sampled (periodic-review) governance, whose review-interval windows ($T_r\approx3$--$4$~yr for the anchovy class, $\approx6$--$12$~yr for the sprat class) are falsifiable, noise-robust predictions, currently untested because no real system operates such a review interval (Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}). What the search does not license is a validated case-level prediction that any specific real resource system's outcome (oscillation versus convergence) can be explained by where its institutional delay sits relative to $\tau_-$: the empirical conditions required to test that claim cleanly --- a long, stable-structure institutional record, free of any comparably fast independent driver, for a resource with a plausible multi-century regeneration-driven oscillation period --- are jointly difficult to satisfy for any real system identified to date. The remaining collocation-grade SNPO classification work (the corrected core's lower-fold real-multiplier signature is collocation-confirmed up to $\tau=5.5815$, while its exact fold location and the canonical four-state core's fold classification remain open) and the infinite-time persistence conjecture (Appendix H) stand as the framework's first-order open items; Section~\ref{sec:falsifiable-predictions}'s Tier 2 discussion is revised accordingly below.

\section{Toward a Unified Core: A Single Stoichiometric Ontology and Its Consequences}
\label{sec:unified-core}
The preceding sections (Sections~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}--\ref{sec:four-state-core}) present four principal model variants --- the general vector framework, the reduced three-state core, its effort-saturation-corrected form, and the four-state core (which restores the ungated effort law while promoting the active abiotic pool to a fourth state) --- each internally verified; where a later variant changed an assumption on which an earlier one depended, the affected quantities were recomputed and both values reported side by side (variant registry, Section~\ref{sec:variant-registry}). Of these variants, the effort-saturation-corrected four-state core of Section~\ref{sec:four-state-core} is the canonical model --- the default referent for the manuscript's headline thresholds --- while the unified ontology developed in this section is the accounting-level unification target, additive to the core rather than a replacement for it.

\paragraph{Scope and status of this section, in brief.} The conservation-law formalism (Section~\ref{sec:unified-ontology}) and the primitive-flux ledger's structural immunity to double-compensation (Section~\ref{sec:unified-ledger}) are rigorously established, as are the closed-form equilibria of both Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v1} and Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2} and the closed-form characteristic quasi-polynomial of the latter; the sigma-gated form Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v1} is established as a negative result; the baseline large-amplitude oscillation is a ghost transient (Section~\ref{sec:unified-core-fixed}); a genuine local Hopf bifurcation is confirmed for $\eta\gtrsim2.34$ and fully characterised at the illustrative $\eta=10$ point; the items that remain open are collected in the status paragraph at the end of this section.

This section rebuilds the accounting core around a single governing ontology against which every claim can be re-derived and re-checked directly from the governing equations, so that no claim depends on an assumption that a later variant may have changed.

\subsection{A Single Physical Ontology: Conserved Stoichiometric Networks and Non-Conserved Functional States}
\label{sec:unified-ontology}
Let $\mathbf x(t)\in\mathbb R_+^n$ denote physical amounts of material in explicitly identified compartments, and let $\mathbf y(t)\in\mathbb R^p$ denote non-conserved functional states (population abundance where distinct from material mass, institutional memory, effort, technology, prices). The material system obeys
\begin{equation}
\dot{\mathbf x} = \mathbf S\,\mathbf v(\mathbf x,\mathbf y) + \mathbf B\,\mathbf u(t),
\label{eq:unified-stoich}
\end{equation}
where $\mathbf S$ is the stoichiometric matrix for internal transfers, $\mathbf v\ge\mathbf 0$ is the vector of primitive, non-negative physical fluxes, and $\mathbf B\mathbf u$ collects explicitly declared boundary imports and exports. If $\mathbf L$ spans the left null space of $\mathbf S$ ($\mathbf L^\top\mathbf S=\mathbf 0$), then
\begin{equation}
\frac{d}{dt}\bigl(\mathbf L^\top\mathbf x\bigr) = \mathbf L^\top\mathbf B\,\mathbf u(t),
\label{eq:unified-conservation}
\end{equation}
so that for a closed material boundary ($\mathbf u=\mathbf 0$), every conserved moiety satisfies $\mathbf L^\top\mathbf x(t)=\mathbf L^\top\mathbf x(0)$ exactly. This is strictly more general than, and formally derives, the flux-ledger-based mass invariant of Section~\ref{sec:abiotic} (Eq.~\eqref{eq:invariant}): that invariant is the special case of Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-conservation} in which $\mathbf L$ selects the physical-mass components identified in the state-space partition of Section~\ref{sec:variant-registry}, and it is derived here from the null-space structure of $\mathbf S$ directly, rather than from a hand-verified table of matched inflows and outflows. There is one conservation law per conserved moiety; there is no universal scalar ``total sustainability mass'' spanning materially incommensurable resource systems, resolving the overreach addressed in Section~\ref{sec:abiotic}.

\paragraph{The planetary-closure statement, made precise.} The Introduction's revised statement (Section~\ref{sec:introduction}) --- that many elemental inventories are approximately materially closed while the planet itself is energetically open --- is made fully precise here: conservation under Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-conservation} applies to specified material moieties across a specified system boundary; it does not apply to biomass indices, pollinator function, or any other functional state in $\mathbf y$, and it does not imply that regeneration is available without a free-energy throughput --- the abiotic reservoir of Section~\ref{sec:abiotic} supplies conserved material substrate, while solar or chemical free energy drives its transformation into biomass.

\subsection{A Primitive-Flux Ledger, and Why It Is Structurally Immune to the Double-Compensation Bug}
\label{sec:unified-ledger}
Section~\ref{sec:abiotic}'s repair of the active-pool equilibrium (Eq.~\eqref{eq:aeq-derived}, parameterised by the steady-state return flux $\mathbf D^{\text{ret}}_i$) was necessary because that section's regeneration term $\mathbf R_i$ is a \emph{net} quantity (gross uptake minus gross turnover) from which a separate compensating constant had to be reconstructed once detritus return was added later. Writing the ledger instead in terms of \textbf{primitive, non-negative fluxes} removes the need for that reconstruction entirely. For one conserved limiting material, with $X$ the living-biomass compartment, $U$ detritus, $A$ the active abiotic pool, and $G$ the geological reservoir:
\[
\begin{aligned}
g(X,A)&\ge0 &&\text{gross assimilation}, & m(X)&\ge0 &&\text{mortality/turnover},\\
d_U(U)&\ge0 &&\text{detrital decomposition}, & h(X,E)&\ge0 &&\text{harvest},\\
e_{GA}(G)&\ge0 &&\text{geological-to-active exchange}, & e_{AG}(A)&\ge0 &&\text{active-to-geological exchange}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The corresponding balance $\dot X = g(X,A)-m(X)-h(X,E)$, $\dot U = m(X)-d_U(U)$, $\dot A = -g(X,A)+d_U(U)+e_{GA}(G)-e_{AG}(A)+(\text{recovery})$ never introduces a compensating equilibrium-target constant in the first place, because the turnover leaving $X$ (via $m$) and the return reaching $A$ (via $d_U$) are both explicit, separately-defined fluxes rather than one being algebraically reconstructed from the other's steady state after the fact. This is verified directly: substituting the steady-state detritus flux $d_U(U^*)=m(X^*)$ (exact, for any decomposition rate, at any fixed point of the detritus equation) into the active-pool balance shows the turnover-and-return pair cancels identically, for any parameterisation, with no auxiliary constant required --- confirmed symbolically (Section~\ref{sec:data-code}), extending the same executable-verification discipline used for \texttt{verify\_flux\_ledger.py} to this reformulated ledger.

\subsection{Extraction as a Physical Flux; Liquidation as Its Diagnostic Classification}
\label{sec:unified-liquidation}
For a harvested living stock, $\dot X = g(X,A)-m(X)-h(X,E)$. Define the signed depletion signal
\begin{equation}
\ell(t) = h(X,E) - \bigl[g(X,A)-m(X)\bigr].
\label{eq:unified-ell}
\end{equation}
