Because $h-(g-m)=-\dot X$ identically (confirmed by direct algebraic substitution: this is a restatement of the stock equation itself, not a new assumption), $\ell(t)=[-\dot X(t)]_+$ whenever other stock losses are absent, and more generally $\ell(t)=-\dot X(t)$ exactly. \textbf{No softplus, shift, floor, or sharpness parameter is required for this diagnostic}: unlike the deficit $qEN-S(N)$ of Section~\ref{sec:deficit-definitions}, whose shifted-softplus treatment exists specifically to give a well-defined positive equilibrium value $\Delta^*=\delta$ under a physical deficit that is \emph{exactly} zero at equilibrium, $\ell$ is already exactly signed and requires no such regularisation for its own sake. This resolves the extraction/liquidation conflation identified in Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected}'s own repair of the two-term formulation: $\ell(t)$, or equivalently $\Lambda_X(t)=[\ell(t)]_+$, is a diagnostic classification of the single physical harvest flow $h(X,E)$, exactly as $\Lambda(t)=\max(0,qEN-S(N))$ already is in Section~\ref{sec:core-model-eqs} --- this section's contribution is to show that the same diagnostic follows from an even simpler, unregularised signed identity once extraction and regeneration are both stated as primitive fluxes.

\subsection{A Verified, Unified Core: Structure, Forward Invariance, and a Critical Structural Finding}
\label{sec:unified-core-model}
Assume harvested material returns to the active pool on a timescale fast relative to the focal dynamics, so that $X+U+A=\mathcal M$ is constant and $A=\mathcal M-X-U$. Choosing $g(X,A)=\mu X\,A/(K_A+A)$ and $m(X)=dX+cX^2$ (density-dependent mortality, replacing the frozen-$A$ logistic law's negative self-term with an explicit, bounded-below primitive flux) and $h(X,E)=qEX$, a first candidate unified core replaces $Z$'s shifted-softplus deficit with the signed $\ell$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-ell} and gates effort with a bounded response function $\sigma(s)=1/(1+e^{-s})$:
\begin{equation}
\dot X = g(X,\mathcal M-X-U)-m(X)-qEX,\quad
\dot U = m(X)-\gamma U,\quad
\dot Z = \frac{\ell(t)-Z}{\tau_m},\quad
\dot E = \alpha(E_{\max}-E)\,\sigma\!\Bigl(\frac{Z(t-\tau)}{Z_0}\Bigr)-\beta E.
\label{eq:unified-core-v1}
\end{equation}
\paragraph{Forward invariance (verified exactly).} On $\Omega=\{(X,U,E): X,U\ge0,\ X+U\le\mathcal M,\ 0\le E\le E_{\max}\}$: at $X=0$, $\dot X=0$ (since $g(0,\cdot)=m(0)=0$); at $U=0$, $\dot U=m(X)\ge0$; at $A=\mathcal M-X-U=0$, $\dot A=-\dot X-\dot U=\gamma U+qEX\ge0$ (since $g(X,0)=0$); at $E=0$, $\dot E=\alpha E_{\max}\sigma(Z_\tau/Z_0)\ge0$; at $E=E_{\max}$, $\dot E=-\beta E_{\max}<0$. Hence $\Omega$ is forward invariant for non-negative admissible histories, confirmed symbolically in \texttt{verify\_unified\_ledger.py}, without the multiplicative $(1-E/E_{\max})$-on-the-whole-equation device that Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix} required to repair the original effort equation's boundary behaviour.

\paragraph{Closed-form equilibrium.} At any interior equilibrium, $\ell^*=0$, so $Z^*=0$ and $E^*=\alpha E_{\max}\sigma_0/(\beta+\alpha\sigma_0)$ with $\sigma_0=\sigma(0)=1/2$ --- confirmed by direct algebraic solution of $\dot E=0$ at $Z^*=0$.

\paragraph{A critical structural finding: this specific gating function suppresses the model's central bifurcation.} The delay-coupling coefficient governing how strongly a perturbation in the delayed signal $Z(t-\tau)$ feeds back into effort is $\partial\dot E/\partial Z_\tau$, evaluated at equilibrium. For Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v1}, this is $\alpha(E_{\max}-E^*)\sigma'(0)/Z_0$ --- proportional to $(E_{\max}-E^*)$, which \emph{shrinks} as the equilibrium effort approaches its ceiling. This is the opposite sign-dependence from the original effort law of Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort} (Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core}), whose corresponding coefficient, $\eta E^*/\Delta_{\text{ref}}$ plus a baseline term, \emph{grows} with $E^*$ --- a genuine autocatalytic structure in which more deployed effort makes the system more sensitive to future delayed signals, which is the actual destabilising mechanism responsible for the Hopf crossings $\tau_-,\tau_+$ established in Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}. We verified the consequence of this sign difference directly, escalating through three independent methods exactly as the established verification practice requires when a linearisation-based claim needs confirmation (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}): (i) Newton-continuation eigenvalue tracking across $\tau$, as used throughout Sections~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}; (ii) a more rigorous, artifact-resistant search --- direct joint minimisation of $|{\det(\lambda I-\mathbf J_{\text{inst}}-\mathbf D e^{-\lambda\tau})}|$ over $(\mathrm{Im}\,\lambda,\tau)$ jointly, which exposed several apparent crossings found by method (i) as Newton branch-jumping artifacts rather than genuine roots; and (iii) direct nonlinear RK4-DDE simulation, the same direct-simulation standard used throughout Sections~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}--\ref{sec:four-state-core}. Across more than $300$ randomized parameterisations spanning wide ranges (including parameter regimes specifically targeted to favour the coupling mechanism, e.g.\ small equilibrium effort combined with a sharply saturating $\sigma$), \textbf{no genuine delay-induced Hopf bifurcation was found for Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v1} as stated}: every apparent candidate identified by method (i) was traced, via methods (ii)--(iii), to either a branch-jumping artifact, a near-zero real (non-oscillatory) eigenvalue, or an equilibrium already unstable at $\tau=0$ whose long-run behaviour is convergence to a different, stable equilibrium rather than a delay-induced cycle. Adopting Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v1} as the manuscript's unified core would therefore have silently eliminated the delay-amplified-instability phenomenon --- this manuscript's central dynamical claim --- while presenting as a strict mathematical simplification of the existing core.

\subsection{A Verified Repair: Retaining the Autocatalytic Mechanism Within the Unified Ontology}
\label{sec:unified-core-fixed}
The repair is to retain the signed, softplus-free diagnostic $\ell$ (Section~\ref{sec:unified-liquidation}) while restoring an effort law whose delay-coupling strengthens, rather than weakens, with effort:
\begin{equation}
\dot X = g(X,A)-m(X)-qEX,\qquad
\dot U = m(X)-\gamma U,\qquad
\dot Z = \frac{\ell(t)-Z}{\tau_m},\qquad
\dot E = \Bigl(1-\frac{E}{E_{\max}}\Bigr)\Bigl[\eta E\frac{Z(t-\tau)}{\Delta_{\text{ref}}}+\delta_0-\eta\frac{E^2}{E_{\max}}\Bigr],
\label{eq:unified-core-v2}
\end{equation}
with $A=\mathcal M-X-U$ as before. Two features of this repair are load-bearing and were verified directly: \textbf{(a)} the bilinear term $\eta E(Z_\tau/\Delta_{\text{ref}})$ is retained from Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core}, restoring the $E^*$-proportional delay-coupling the sigma-gated form lacked; \textbf{(b)} the baseline term is a \emph{true constant} $\delta_0$, not the original $\delta_0 Z_\tau/(Z_{\text{ref}}+Z_\tau)$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core}): because the new signed $\ell$ makes $Z^*=0$ exactly at equilibrium (unlike the shifted-softplus $Z^*=\delta>0$ of Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core}), a baseline term that is itself proportional to $Z_\tau$ vanishes at $Z_\tau=0$ and would zero out the baseline effort entirely --- a second, independent structural trap that a naive substitution of the new diagnostic into the old baseline term falls into, distinct from the sigma-gating problem of Section~\ref{sec:unified-core-model} but verified via the same method (symbolic evaluation of the equilibrium condition) before being reported here. The multiplicative $(1-E/E_{\max})$ gate is retained from the already-verified repair of Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}.

\paragraph{Closed-form equilibrium (verified), and the conditions under which it exists and strengthens with effort.} At $Z^*=0$, the bracket's equilibrium condition $\delta_0-\eta(E^*)^2/E_{\max}=0$ gives
\begin{equation}
E^* = \sqrt{\delta_0 E_{\max}/\eta},
\label{eq:unified-Estar}
\end{equation}
confirmed by direct substitution; $X^*,U^*,A^*$ then follow, as in Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}, from solving $g(X^*,A^*)-m(X^*)=qE^*X^*$ jointly with $U^*=m(X^*)/\gamma$ and $A^*=\mathcal M-X^*-U^*$. This is an \emph{interior} equilibrium ($0<E^*<E_{\max}$) only when $0<\delta_0<\eta E_{\max}$; outside this range Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-Estar} returns $E^*\ge E_{\max}$, and the equilibrium instead sits at or beyond the boundary, requiring separate treatment not attempted here. Section~\ref{sec:unified-core-model}'s critical finding about Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v1} was that its delay-coupling coefficient $\partial\dot E/\partial Z_\tau|_*$ \emph{weakens} monotonically with $E^*$; for the repaired Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2}, the full gated coefficient is $(1-E^*/E_{\max})\,\eta E^*/\Delta_{\text{ref}}$, whose derivative with respect to $E^*$ is $(\eta/\Delta_{\text{ref}})(1-2E^*/E_{\max})$ (confirmed symbolically) --- positive, i.e., genuinely strengthening with equilibrium effort as claimed below, only for $E^*<E_{\max}/2$, equivalently $\delta_0<\eta E_{\max}/4$. Both the existence condition and the strengthening condition are satisfied by the illustrative parameterisation used below ($\delta_0=0.0118$, $\eta=2.23$, $E_{\max}=35.8$: $\eta E_{\max}/4\approx19.96\gg\delta_0$, giving $E^*\approx0.435\ll E_{\max}/2=17.9$), but the qualitative claim that this repaired form's coupling strengthens with effort is conditional on $\delta_0<\eta E_{\max}/4$ specifically, not automatic from retaining the bilinear term alone; the multiplicative $(1-E/E_{\max})$ gate reverses the sign of that dependence once $E^*$ exceeds $E_{\max}/2$, and this boundary has not been explored elsewhere in this manuscript's parameter ranges for the unified core.

\paragraph{Direct confirmation of a sustained large-amplitude attractor.} Using an illustrative, Table-consistent parameterisation ($\mathcal M=100$, $\mu=0.340$, $K_A=24.5$, $d=0.072$, $c=0.00995$, $\gamma=0.388$, $q=0.0384$, $E_{\max}=35.8$, $\eta=2.23$, $\delta_0=0.0118$, $\Delta_{\text{ref}}=2.29$, $\tau_m=5.13$, all within or close to the ranges of Table~\ref{tab:params}), the equilibrium solves to $X^*\approx16.68$, $U^*\approx10.23$, $E^*\approx0.435$ (confirmed to machine precision by direct substitution). Direct nonlinear RK4-DDE simulation confirms a genuine, sustained large-amplitude oscillation reachable from far-from-equilibrium histories: starting from $(X_0,U_0,E_0)=(1.5X^*,U^*,0.5E^*)$, the tail amplitude is confirmed near zero at $\tau=30$ and converges to a stable, non-decaying value of $\approx16.8$ (roughly the full range between $X\approx0$ and $X\approx X^*$) by $\tau=50$, confirmed non-transient over a $6\times10^4$-time-unit run at $\tau=50$ specifically (amplitude stationary to four significant figures across the run's final three quarters), and persists at comparable amplitude throughout $\tau\in[75,300]$. This confirms that Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2} restores genuine delay-driven large-amplitude instability within the unified ontology.

It does \emph{not}, however, establish that this onset is a local Hopf bifurcation of the equilibrium itself. Section~\ref{sec:unified-characteristic} derives the exact closed-form characteristic quasi-polynomial for Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2} and uses it, together with an independently-validated argument-principle root count, to show that at this same illustrative parameterisation the interior equilibrium remains locally asymptotically stable for every delay tested in $0\le\tau\le500$: a small perturbation from $(X^*,U^*,0,E^*)$ decays back to the equilibrium at every one of $\tau=30,50,75,100$ tested directly by simulation, and the characteristic quasi-polynomial has no imaginary-axis root anywhere in that range. The large-amplitude oscillation above is therefore reachable only from histories outside the equilibrium's basin of attraction, not born through a bifurcation of the equilibrium itself. Direct bisection on the same far-from-equilibrium history used above brackets the onset of this global attractor between $\tau\approx33.4$ and $\tau\approx33.6$. The transition is consequently an \textbf{apparent global attractor-birth / fold-like event}, which persistence testing shows to be a ghost transient (the oscillation collapses to the locally stable equilibrium within $\approx2\times10^4$ time units throughout the bracket) rather than a saddle-node of periodic orbits or a torus-related fold as for Candidates A and B's folds (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}) --- rather than a local Hopf crossing.

\subsection{Closed-Form Characteristic Equation and Local Hopf Exclusion for Unified Core v2}
\label{sec:unified-characteristic}
The closed-form equilibrium of Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2} admits an exact local bifurcation calculation. To avoid a symbol collision with the CES exponent $\gamma$ used elsewhere in this manuscript (Section~\ref{sec:layer4}), write $\gamma_U$ for the detritus decomposition rate $\gamma$ appearing in Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2}.

Let $G(X,U):=g(X,\mathcal M-X-U)$, so that the active-pool constraint $A=\mathcal M-X-U$ is already incorporated. At the interior equilibrium $Z^*=0$, $E^*=\sqrt{\delta_0E_{\max}/\eta}$, define
\[
G_X=\left.\frac{\partial G}{\partial X}\right|_*, \qquad G_U=\left.\frac{\partial G}{\partial U}\right|_*, \qquad m'_*=m'(X^*).
\]
For the primitive fluxes used in Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2}, $g(X,A)=\mu XA/(K_A+A)$ and $m(X)=dX+cX^2$, these derivatives are
\[
G_X=\frac{\mu A^*}{K_A+A^*}-\frac{\mu X^*K_A}{(K_A+A^*)^2}, \qquad G_U=-\frac{\mu X^*K_A}{(K_A+A^*)^2}, \qquad m'_*=d+2cX^*.
\]
Introduce the linearised stock coefficients
\[
J_X:=G_X-m'_*-qE^*, \qquad J_U:=G_U, \qquad J_E:=-qX^*,
\]
and the effort coefficients, re-derived directly from Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2} rather than assumed,
\[
C_E:=-2\Bigl(1-\frac{E^*}{E_{\max}}\Bigr)\frac{\eta E^*}{E_{\max}}, \qquad C_Z:=\Bigl(1-\frac{E^*}{E_{\max}}\Bigr)\frac{\eta E^*}{\Delta_{\text{ref}}}
\]
(the latter is exactly the delay-coupling coefficient already established in Section~\ref{sec:unified-core-fixed}). With perturbations $x=X-X^*$, $u=U-U^*$, $z=Z-Z^*$, $e=E-E^*$, the linearised system is
\[
\dot x = J_X x + J_U u + J_E e, \qquad \dot u = m'_* x - \gamma_U u, \qquad \dot z = \frac{-J_X x - J_U u - J_E e - z}{\tau_m}, \qquad \dot e = C_E e + C_Z z(t-\tau),
\]
the third equation using the primitive-flux identity $\ell=-\dot X$ directly (the linearised depletion signal is exactly the negative of the linearised stock derivative). The resulting characteristic determinant, expanded and verified symbolically (\texttt{verify\_unified\_characteristic.py}), reduces to the closed-form quasi-polynomial
\begin{equation}
\mathcal Q(\lambda) - \mathcal K\,\lambda(\lambda+\gamma_U)e^{-\lambda\tau} = 0,
\label{eq:unified-char-eq}
\end{equation}
where
\[
\mathcal Q(\lambda) := \Bigl[(\lambda-J_X)(\lambda+\gamma_U)-J_U m'_*\Bigr](\lambda-C_E)(1+\tau_m\lambda), \qquad \mathcal K:=-J_EC_Z = qX^*\Bigl(1-\frac{E^*}{E_{\max}}\Bigr)\frac{\eta E^*}{\Delta_{\text{ref}}}.
\]
For any interior equilibrium with $X^*>0$ and $0<E^*<E_{\max}$, $\mathcal K>0$ strictly, confirmed symbolically together with five limiting-case checks that all correctly send $\mathcal K\to0$: $q\to0$ (no harvest coupling), $\eta\to0$ (no effort-adjustment response), $\Delta_{\text{ref}}\to\infty$ (deficit signal diluted to nothing), $E^*\to E_{\max}$ (the boundary-exact gate of Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix} suppresses the delay feedback entirely at the ceiling), and $X^*\to0$ (nothing left to extract).

\paragraph{Exact Hopf-locus necessary condition.} A local Hopf bifurcation requires $\lambda=i\omega$, $\omega>0$, to solve Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-char-eq}, equivalently
\[
F(\omega) := |\mathcal Q(i\omega)|^2 - \mathcal K^2\omega^2(\omega^2+\gamma_U^2) = 0,
\]
using the identity $|i\omega(i\omega+\gamma_U)|=\omega\sqrt{\omega^2+\gamma_U^2}$ (confirmed symbolically). If a positive root $\omega$ exists, the corresponding delays are $\tau_n(\omega)=[\pi/2+\arctan(\omega/\gamma_U)-\arg\mathcal Q(i\omega)+2\pi n]/\omega$ for integer $n$, retaining only positive values; the smallest such value would be the local Hopf threshold.

\paragraph{Applied to the baseline: no local Hopf bifurcation.} At the illustrative parameterisation of Section~\ref{sec:unified-core-fixed}, direct global minimisation of $F(\omega)$ over $\omega\in(0,100]$ finds \textbf{no root}: $F$ remains strictly positive throughout, with its minimum value (at $\omega\approx0.080$) still $\approx15\%$ of $F(0)$'s own scale, not a near-miss numerical artifact. Independently, an argument-principle (winding-number) count of the unstable roots of the full quasi-polynomial Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-char-eq} --- validated first against the published Candidate A three-state thresholds, correctly recovering two unstable roots below $\tau_-\approx6.88$~yr and zero in the monostable interval, with the destabilisation above $\tau_+\approx132$~yr confirmed by direct root continuation once the method's known insensitivity near critical slowing down (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) is accounted for --- gives zero unstable roots for the unified core v2's own equilibrium at every $\tau\in\{0.001,10,30,50,75,100,150,200,300,500\}$ tested; at the two largest $\tau$ values, the contour's fixed angular resolution is coarser than the delay term's own oscillation period, so this cross-check was independently re-run at $\sim16$--$27\times$ finer resolution specifically for $\tau=300,500$ and gives an identical zero winding number, confirming the coarser resolution's result was not an artifact of under-sampling. \textbf{The $\tau$-independent $F(\omega)>0$ result above is nonetheless the primary evidence for the absence of a local Hopf at any $\tau$}; the winding-number sweep is a secondary, per-$\tau$ cross-check of that primary result, not an independent source of the conclusion. Both methods agree: \textbf{the interior equilibrium of Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2} is locally asymptotically stable throughout this entire delay range at the illustrative baseline.} The characteristic equation is nonetheless retained as a permanent verification instrument: it provides an exact necessary condition for a local Hopf under any future parameterisation, and it is precisely what separates the now-verified local stability of the equilibrium from the independently-verified global existence of the large-amplitude attractor described above.

\paragraph{This absence is parametric, not structural: the baseline sits just below its own local-Hopf threshold.} The preceding result does not imply that the unified ontology cannot support a local Hopf bifurcation at all: the absence at the baseline reflects parameter values, not model structure. Sweeping $\eta$ alone, holding every other parameter at the illustrative baseline, confirms this directly: a genuine local Hopf bifurcation \emph{appears} for $\eta\gtrsim2.34$, bisected to $\eta_{\text{crit}}\approx2.337$, compared with the baseline's own $\eta=2.23$ --- a gap of under $5\%$, inside the literature-anchored range for the effort-adjustment rate ($\eta\in[0.5,3.0]$, Table~\ref{tab:params}). The predicted crossing is confirmed by direct nonlinear simulation, not asserted from linearisation alone: at $\eta=2.5$ (just above $\eta_{\text{crit}}$), a small perturbation from equilibrium has tail amplitude $\approx0.001$ at $\tau=50$ and grows to $\approx0.13$ by $\tau=150$, matching the characteristic equation's own predicted local Hopf threshold of $\tau\approx54.2$ at that $\eta$; at $\eta=10$, a small perturbation visibly amplifies for $\tau\lesssim17.5$, decays within a narrow window $\tau\in(17.6,18.4)$, and destabilises again beyond $\tau\approx18.4$ --- exactly the local-instability/local-stability/local-instability signature a genuine Hopf pair produces, confirmed at both predicted crossings ($\tau_-\approx17.568$, $\tau_+\approx18.362$) (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}). Decomposing the original core's delay-coupling coefficient into its two additive channels also shows the removed baseline-panic-derivative term contributes $\lesssim0.5\%$ of the total coupling strength at Candidate A's own parameters ($\delta_0Z_{\text{ref}}/(Z_{\text{ref}}+\delta)^2\approx0.0087$ against a bilinear-term contribution $\eta E^*/\Delta_{\text{ref}}\approx1.91$): removing it is a genuine but minor structural change, not the dominant cause of the baseline's local stability. The dominant cause is instead the smaller equilibrium effort that the unified ontology's equilibrium condition produces at these parameters ($E^*\approx0.435$ versus Candidate A's $E^*\approx2.09$), which under-drives the surviving bilinear coupling term $(1-E^*/E_{\max})\eta E^*/\Delta_{\text{ref}}$ relative to the instantaneous damping of the stock/effort subsystem. \textbf{The correct and fully verified statement is therefore that the illustrative baseline sits just below its own local-Hopf boundary in $\eta$-space} --- a fact about this specific parameterisation, not a proof that the unified stoichiometric ontology cannot support a local Hopf bifurcation under any parameterisation. The global-fold threshold identified below at the baseline itself, and its classification as an SNPO, a torus-related bifurcation, or something else, remain open --- to be located and classified by periodic-orbit or invariant-torus continuation rather than by the local Hopf-locus formula derived here.

\paragraph{A full characterisation of the $\eta=10$ local-Hopf point: supercritical onset at $\tau_-$, and open complexity near $\tau_+$.} The $\eta=10$ case confirms \emph{that} a genuine local Hopf pair exists; this paragraph characterises \emph{what kind}. $\eta=10$ lies outside the literature-anchored range for the effort-adjustment rate ($\eta\in[0.5,3.0]$, Table~\ref{tab:params}) and is used purely as an illustration well inside the local-Hopf regime confirmed above, not as a claim about a realistic parameterisation. The exact Hopf-locus crossings for $\eta=10$, re-derived numerically from Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-char-eq}, are $\tau_-\approx17.568$ and $\tau_+\approx18.362$~yr, a narrow monostable interval bracketed by direct simulation on both sides (unstable growth for $\tau<\tau_-$, decay to equilibrium inside the window, unstable growth again for $\tau>\tau_+$). The two crossings are qualitatively different from each other, and both differ from Candidates A/B's own Hopf points. \textbf{Near $\tau_-$}, the bifurcation is supercritical-like: a near-equilibrium and a far-from-equilibrium initial condition converge to the \emph{same} limit-cycle amplitude rather than to two coexisting attractors (confirmed at $\tau=17$: near- and far-initial-condition amplitudes agree to within $0.1\%$), and that shared amplitude shrinks \emph{continuously} to zero as $\tau\to\tau_-^-$ --- a power-law fit over $\tau\in\{14,15,16,17,17.2,17.4\}$ gives an exponent of $\approx0.59$, broadly consistent with (though, given the DDE's nonlinearity, not required to exactly equal) the $0.5$ scaling expected of a classical supercritical Hopf, and confirmed dt-convergent (relative spread $<2\%$ across $dt\in\{0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01\}$). This is the opposite qualitative picture from Candidates A/B, where the large-amplitude cycle coexists with the stable equilibrium via a subcritical Hopf and a separate SNPO fold (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}); here, at this parameter point, there is no coexistence and no separate fold to locate near $\tau_-$. \textbf{Near $\tau_+$}, by contrast, the picture is more complex. Near-equilibrium trajectories just above $\tau_+$ do not settle into any stationary state, small or large, over any of the horizons tested here (up to $10^5$ time units): they instead show a genuine, permanently recurring \textbf{relaxation-oscillation/intermittency}, alternating between long quiet intervals near the equilibrium and shorter excursions to the large-amplitude attractor. A $20{,}000$-time-unit run, partitioned into $1000$-time-unit windows and classified by whether each window's peak-to-peak amplitude exceeds $5$ (a threshold well between the quiet-state amplitude, $\lesssim1$, and the large-attractor amplitude, $\approx18$), shows the \textbf{fraction of time spent in the large-amplitude excursion increasing smoothly and monotonically with $\tau$}, from $0\%$ at $\tau=18.4$ through $5\%$ at $\tau=18.5$, $20\%$ at $\tau=18.8$, $50\%$ at $\tau\approx19.5$, $80\%$ at $\tau=20.5$, to $100\%$ by $\tau\approx22$ --- with no sharp threshold separating a ``quiet'' regime from a ``captured'' one anywhere in this range. At $\tau=18.45$, all tested $dt\in\{0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01\}$ show the same recurring intermittency (six escape events over $10^5$ time units, dt-convergent), merely out of phase with each other, so a shorter horizon can show escape at one $dt$ and not another depending on which phase of the same cycle it ends on. $\tau=18.8$ and $\tau=18.9$ likewise continue to show recurring, though shorter-period, returns to the quiet state within a $20{,}000$-time-unit run. The qualitative type of this intermittency is resolved by three independent diagnostics (a broad Fourier spectrum of the excursion-onset intervals with no sharp peak; a thin, map-like Poincar\'e section on $Z$-crossings; and an irregular inter-excursion-interval distribution, CV $1.58$, with anticorrelated successive excursion peaks, $r=-0.47$): it is a homoclinic-like slow-fast (relaxation-oscillation) intermittency, not a torus-related phenomenon and not period-doubling, and the precise $\tau$ at which the recurrence period diverges (if it does) or the quiet phase disappears entirely has not been determined. The resulting picture is therefore: $\tau_-$ is a genuine supercritical-like local Hopf point with no coexisting fold; the region above $\tau_+$ shows a smooth, monotonically increasing tendency toward permanent large-amplitude capture rather than a sharp second threshold, and no $\tau$ tested here (up to $\tau=22$) or beyond has been confirmed free of recurrence over arbitrarily long horizons, so ``permanent capture'' should be read as a strong tendency at large $\tau$ within the horizons tested, not as a located threshold.


\paragraph{A shared feedback architecture; no reduction between the governing equations.} Both cores' local stability is governed by one general feedback characteristic equation, Eq.~\eqref{eq:general-feedback}, verified symbolically to reduce exactly to each core's own characteristic equation (Section~\ref{sec:general-feedback}): the two models are realizations of a single feedback architecture under different ecological Jacobians and gains. No parameter-reduction link between their governing equations is claimed: the two cores differ in state space, regeneration law, deficit diagnostic, and effort law simultaneously (variant registry, Section~\ref{sec:variant-registry}), and no limiting procedure connecting them has been derived or attempted. The most natural candidate --- the unsaturated Michaelis--Menten limit $K_A\to0$ of $g(X,A)=\mu X A/(K_A+A)$, intended to recover the frozen-$A$ logistic law $S(N)=rN(1-N/K)$ --- does not in fact do so: $g\to\mu X$ identically as $K_A\to0$ (confirmed symbolically), an unbounded, self-unlimited growth term with no analogue of the logistic ceiling, not a reduction to $S(N)$. No other candidate limit has been checked. Consequently, the two cores should currently be read as \textbf{independent existence proofs of the delay-amplified-instability mechanism under different ontological assumptions}, unified at the level of the general feedback architecture of Eq.~\eqref{eq:general-feedback} rather than at the level of the governing equations themselves, and not as competing quantitative predictions for the same physical system; where they disagree (e.g., unified core v2's baseline global fold versus the three-state core's local Hopf), this reflects the two models' different ecological Jacobians $\mathbf J$ and gains $g$ within the same shared architecture, not one correcting the other, and no protocol currently exists for deciding which better describes a specific real resource without independently estimating that resource's own governing parameters in both ontologies.

\paragraph{Falsifiable predictions for the unified core, stated separately from Section~\ref{sec:falsifiable-predictions}'s Tiers 1--3.} Section~\ref{sec:falsifiable-predictions}'s Tier 2 threshold ($\tau_-\approx3.7$--$6.9$~yr) is the effort-saturation-corrected \emph{three-state} core's threshold (Candidates A/B), not unified core v2's; nothing established in this section changes that threshold or its empirical status. Unified core v2 makes a \emph{different}, and currently less mature, empirical claim, stated here explicitly rather than left implicit: at its illustrative baseline ($\eta=2.23$), the model predicts (i) small perturbations from the interior equilibrium decay at every delay tested, so no local-Hopf early-warning signature (growing small-amplitude oscillations, oscillatory critical slowing down, a return-rate crossing zero) should appear; and (ii) far-from-equilibrium histories should exhibit a large-amplitude oscillation for sufficiently large delays, which persistence testing reclassifies as a ghost transient (collapse to the stable equilibrium within $\approx2\times10^4$ time units for $\tau\in(33.4,34.8)$; Section~\ref{sec:unified-core-fixed}), with a basin-selective slow-fast oscillation only at $\tau\gtrsim35$ for specific histories. This is falsified for a given candidate system if small perturbations near an estimated equilibrium instead show growing oscillations as delay increases (evidence for a local Hopf rather than a global-fold route), or if large historical shocks fail to produce any sustained large-amplitude response where the model predicts capture. Separately, and only for parameter regimes with $\eta\gtrsim2.34$ (inside Table~\ref{tab:params}'s own range but well above the $\eta=2.23$ baseline), the model predicts a genuine local Hopf pair instead (Section~\ref{sec:unified-characteristic}); which of these two regimes applies to a given real system is an empirical question about that system's own $\eta$, not a fixed property of the unified core as a whole. None of this has case-level support: unlike Tier 2's Candidates A/B, no attempt has yet been made to locate a real system's $\eta$, and the $\tau\approx33.4$--$33.6$ global-onset bracket itself is provisional (one history, not continuation-verified). This paragraph is offered as a scope-appropriate starting point for that future empirical work, not as a claim that it has been carried out.

\subsection{A General Feedback Architecture Unifying the Three-State and Unified-Core Characteristic Equations}
\label{sec:general-feedback}
Section~\ref{sec:exact-cubic} reduced the three-state core's characteristic equation to an exact cubic; Section~\ref{sec:unified-characteristic} derived a structurally similar-looking closed-form quasi-polynomial, Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-char-eq}, for the unified core v2. The two derivations were carried out independently, in different notation, from different governing equations. This subsection shows they are not merely analogous: both are the \emph{same} general feedback characteristic equation, evaluated at two different points in one parameter space, and states that general equation explicitly as the connective architecture this manuscript's local bifurcation analyses share.

\paragraph{The general architecture.} Let $\boldsymbol\xi$ denote small perturbations of an ecological subsystem's state (excluding the memory variable $Z$ and effort $E$), linearised as
\[
\dot{\boldsymbol\xi} = \mathbf J\boldsymbol\xi + \mathbf b_E\,e,
\]
where $\mathbf J$ is the ecological Jacobian, $\mathbf b_E$ is the sensitivity of that subsystem to a perturbation $e$ in effort, and let $\mathbf c^\top\boldsymbol\xi$ select the focal stock's own perturbation. The memory variable's linearised source is a gain-$g$, possibly-filtered projection of $-\mathbf c^\top\dot{\boldsymbol\xi}$ (the negative rate of change of the focal stock, i.e., a signed liquidation-type signal), and effort responds to the delayed memory signal:
\[
\tau_m\dot z = g\bigl(-\mathbf c^\top\dot{\boldsymbol\xi}\bigr) - z, \qquad \dot e = C_E e + C_Z z(t-\tau).
\]
Substituting the ecological mode $\boldsymbol\xi=(\lambda\mathbf I-\mathbf J)^{-1}\mathbf b_E\,e$ (valid for $e\propto e^{\lambda t}$) gives the characteristic equation
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\ \lambda - C_E - C_Z\,e^{-\lambda\tau}\,\frac{-g\lambda\,\mathbf c^\top(\lambda\mathbf I-\mathbf J)^{-1}\mathbf b_E}{1+\tau_m\lambda} = 0.\ }
\label{eq:general-feedback}
\end{equation}
This equation is verified by direct symbolic computation to reduce \emph{exactly} to both of the local characteristic equations derived for the three-state core (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) and the unified core v2 (Section~\ref{sec:unified-characteristic}):

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Three-state core (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}):} take $\boldsymbol\xi=x$ (the stock perturbation alone, a $1\times1$ ecological block), $\mathbf J=A_N$, $\mathbf b_E=A_E$, $\mathbf c=1$, and $g=\text{softplus}_k'(0)=1/2$ (the shifted-softplus's own derivative at the equilibrium deficit, Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}). Clearing denominators in Eq.~\eqref{eq:general-feedback} under this substitution reproduces the characteristic quasi-polynomial, Eq.~\eqref{eq:char-eq-corrected}, term for term (zero symbolic residual), \emph{provided} the coefficients $B_N,B_E$ are identified as $B_N=-gA_N/\tau_m$, $B_E=-gA_E/\tau_m$ --- a relationship confirmed to hold exactly at the Candidate A and Candidate B parameter sets (not merely asserted), since both $B_N,B_E$ and $A_N,A_E$ are independently re-derived from the same underlying stock equation and softplus-linearisation.
\item \textbf{Unified core v2 (Section~\ref{sec:unified-characteristic}):} take $\boldsymbol\xi=(x,u)^\top$ (the two-state stock--detritus ecological block), $\mathbf J=\begin{pmatrix}J_X&J_U\\m'_*&-\gamma_U\end{pmatrix}$, $\mathbf b_E=(J_E,0)^\top$, $\mathbf c=(1,0)^\top$, and $g=1$ (no softplus regularisation is needed here, since $\ell=-\dot X$ is already exactly signed, Section~\ref{sec:unified-liquidation}). Clearing denominators in Eq.~\eqref{eq:general-feedback} under this substitution reproduces Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-char-eq} exactly, with $\mathcal K=-J_EC_Z$ emerging automatically from $-g\mathbf c^\top(\lambda\mathbf I-\mathbf J)^{-1}\mathbf b_E$'s own numerator structure, not introduced separately.
\end{itemize}
Both reductions are confirmed by independent symbolic computation (Section~\ref{sec:data-code}).

\paragraph{What this unifies, and what it does not.} This is \emph{not} a claim that unified core v2 is a parameter limit of the three-state core, or vice versa: the "No reduction link" finding below is unaffected, since the two models still use different ecological Jacobians $\mathbf J$ (one built from the frozen-$A$ logistic law, the other from the primitive-flux stoichiometric ontology of Section~\ref{sec:unified-ontology}), different gains $g$, and different dimensions for $\boldsymbol\xi$. What Eq.~\eqref{eq:general-feedback} does establish is that both models' local stability is governed by \emph{the same three ingredients in the same algebraic combination}: an ecological resolvent $\mathbf c^\top(\lambda\mathbf I-\mathbf J)^{-1}\mathbf b_E$ that supplies admissible oscillation frequencies and their intrinsic phase, an institutional loop gain $C_Z\cdot g$ that must be large enough to close the feedback loop, and a delay $\tau$ that supplies the additional phase shift required to satisfy the loop's own phase condition at a given frequency. For a Hopf root $\lambda=i\omega$, Eq.~\eqref{eq:general-feedback} splits into the modulus condition
\[
\left|C_Z\,g\,\frac{i\omega\,\mathbf c^\top(i\omega\mathbf I-\mathbf J)^{-1}\mathbf b_E}{1+i\omega\tau_m}\right| = |i\omega-C_E|
\]
and the phase condition
\[
-\omega\tau + \arg\!\left[C_Z\,g\,\frac{i\omega\,\mathbf c^\top(i\omega\mathbf I-\mathbf J)^{-1}\mathbf b_E}{1+i\omega\tau_m}\right] = \arg(i\omega-C_E) + \pi + 2\pi n,
\]
exactly the gain/phase decomposition already used implicitly in both Sections~\ref{sec:exact-cubic} and~\ref{sec:unified-characteristic}'s own modulus-and-argument constructions (Eqs.~\eqref{eq:hopf-cubic}--\eqref{eq:hopf-tau-formula} and the paragraph following Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-char-eq}, respectively), now stated once in variable-independent form. This also gives a precise, general reading of why $\tau$'s effect on stability need not be monotone (already documented case-by-case in Sections~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} and~\ref{sec:unified-characteristic}: the three-state core has a stabilising \emph{and} a destabilising Hopf crossing, and unified core v2 at $\eta=10$ has a supercritical crossing at $\tau_-$ and a qualitatively different recurring-intermittency crossing near $\tau_+$): $\tau$ enters the phase condition linearly through $-\omega\tau$ while the gain condition is $\tau$-independent, so as $\tau$ increases at fixed $\omega$, the loop's phase rotates through the point of exact match, past it, and — if a further $\omega$-branch exists with the gain condition also satisfied — back into match again, without the gain condition itself changing. Whether a particular $(\mathbf J,\mathbf b_E,\mathbf c,g,\tau)$ combination produces zero, one, or several such crossings is exactly the question each section's own polynomial (cubic for the three-state core, quartic-derived quasi-polynomial for the unified core) answers case by case; Eq.~\eqref{eq:general-feedback} explains \emph{why} that count and its non-monotone $\tau$-dependence are the generic behaviour of this feedback architecture, not a coincidence specific to either core.



\paragraph{Status and open items, stated with the same evidentiary tiering used throughout.} \textbf{Rigorously established:} the conservation-law formalism (Section~\ref{sec:unified-ontology}), the primitive-flux ledger's structural immunity to double-compensation (Section~\ref{sec:unified-ledger}), the $\ell=-\dot X$ identity and forward-invariance proof (Sections~\ref{sec:unified-liquidation}--\ref{sec:unified-core-model}), the closed-form equilibria of both Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v1} and Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2}, the closed-form characteristic quasi-polynomial Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-char-eq} for Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2}, and --- at the illustrative baseline parameterisation specifically --- the \emph{absence} of a local Hopf bifurcation over $\tau\in[0,500]$, confirmed by two independent methods, together with the confirmation that this absence is a parametric fact about the baseline (the equilibrium sits just below its own local-Hopf boundary in $\eta$-space, $\eta_{\text{crit}}\approx2.337$ against a baseline $\eta=2.23$, itself inside Table~\ref{tab:params}'s literature-anchored range) rather than a structural consequence of the unified ontology: a genuine local Hopf bifurcation is confirmed, by both the characteristic equation and direct nonlinear simulation, to exist for $\eta\gtrsim2.34$ at otherwise-unchanged parameters; at the illustrative $\eta=10$ point specifically, this Hopf pair is fully located ($\tau_-\approx17.568$, $\tau_+\approx18.362$~yr) and the $\tau_-$ crossing is confirmed supercritical-like (continuous amplitude onset, no coexisting fold, dt-convergent power-law scaling exponent $\approx0.59$). \textbf{Rigorously established as a negative result:} the sigma-gated form Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v1} does not exhibit the manuscript's central bifurcation, verified via three independent, escalating-rigor methods. \textbf{Ghost transient.} The baseline far-from-equilibrium large-amplitude oscillation is not a sustained attractor: with the same far-from-equilibrium history used above ($X_0=1.5X^*$, $U_0=U^*$, $E_0=0.5E^*$), the oscillation collapses to the locally stable equilibrium within $\approx2\times10^4$ time units throughout the $33.4$--$34.8$ bracket, robust across $dt$ and seeds (Section~\ref{sec:unified-core-fixed}); observation windows shorter than this collapse timescale can misidentify the transient as a sustained oscillation. A large-amplitude oscillation does persist for $\tau\gtrsim35$, but only for specific histories ($Z_0=0$ in the above family) and not for others ($Z_0=0.5$ collapses), so it is basin-selective and slow-fast modulated (a sharp $\approx40.7$-unit line plus a frequency-unstable slow component) rather than a robust single-cycle attractor; separately, the local-Hopf regime confirmed to exist for $\eta\gtrsim2.34$ has been characterised in full only at the single illustrative point $\eta=10$ (itself outside Table~\ref{tab:params}'s literature range, used solely for tractability): its $\tau_-$ crossing is supercritical-like with no coexisting fold, while its $\tau_+$ crossing shows genuine, dt-convergent, recurring intermittency (a relaxation-oscillation-like alternation between the quiet and large-amplitude states, confirmed non-transient over $10^5$ time units at $\tau=18.45$) with no sharp second threshold: the fraction of time spent in the large-amplitude excursion instead increases smoothly and monotonically from $0\%$ at $\tau\approx18.4$ to $100\%$ by $\tau\approx22$, and no $\tau$ tested here has been confirmed free of eventual recurrence over arbitrarily long horizons; $\eta=2.5$, the only in-range value examined, was found to have a qualitatively richer multi-crossing structure (at least four alternating stable/unstable delay bands over $\tau\in[0,300]$, not the simple two-Hopf-point picture) and has not been further pursued; a robustness sweep across the full $\eta\in(2.337,3.0]$ in-range interval (fourteen points; verified in \texttt{verify\_unified\_eta\_sweep.py}) reveals \emph{two} distinct local-Hopf pairs: a large-delay pair born at $\eta_{\text{crit}}\approx2.337$ with a narrow window at large delay ($\tau_-\approx71.2$, $\tau_+\approx72.9$ yr), which widens and migrates to smaller delays as $\eta$ rises ($\tau_-\approx4.5$, $\tau_+\approx41.2$ yr at $\eta=3.0$); and a small-delay pair born at $\eta\approx2.454$ with $\tau_-\to0$ at its onset, which grows with $\eta$ ($\tau_-\approx0.56$, $\tau_+\approx54.2$ yr at $\eta=2.5$; $\tau_-\approx3.19$, $\tau_+\approx44.9$ yr at $\eta=2.8$). The interleaving of the two pairs reproduces the reported multi-crossing structure at $\eta=2.5$ (crossings at $\tau\approx0.6,54.2,92.9,113.1$ yr, i.e. at least four alternating delay bands), and the sweep reproduces the fully characterised pair at $\eta=10$ ($\tau_-\approx17.568$, $\tau_+\approx18.362$ yr). Because the first (large-delay) Hopf pair is born at $\tau\approx71$ yr far above the baseline's global-fold location ($\tau\approx33.4$--$33.6$ yr), the local-Hopf regime does \emph{not} connect continuously to the baseline's global-fold regime as $\eta$ is lowered: the two are separate instability mechanisms (a local delay-induced Hopf pair born at large delay versus a global attractor-birth event at moderate delay), resolving the connectivity question. A fold search along the $\eta=2.5$ and $\eta=10$ $\tau_+$ branches remains open; the $\eta=10$ recurring intermittency has been classified as homoclinic-like slow-fast (relaxation-oscillation) intermittency (broad onset-interval spectrum, thin Poincar\'e section, CV $1.58$, return-map $r=-0.47$), not a torus. \textbf{Resolved:} the baseline's global fold is an apparent (ghost-transient) fold, not an SNPO or torus (persistence testing shows collapse to equilibrium within $\approx2\times10^4$ units throughout the $\tau\in(33.4,34.8)$ bracket; Section~\ref{sec:unified-core-fixed}). \textbf{Not yet attempted:} mapping the full $\eta$-dependent boundary between the local-Hopf and global-fold regimes; formally re-deriving the general vector framework of Sections~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}--\ref{sec:economic-layer} as an exact specialisation of Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-stoich} in full vector (not scalar) generality; and empirically testing the falsifiable predictions stated above for the unified core (a starting statement of which predictions apply, and to which parameter regime, is now given above, but no case-level empirical search analogous to Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}'s has been attempted for it). Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2} is presented as a verified unification target with a confirmed central instability mechanism --- expressed as a global fold at the baseline and as a genuine local Hopf bifurcation nearby in parameter space --- additive to Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:four-state-core} rather than yet a full replacement for their quantitative results, pending the further verification enumerated above.

\section{Empirical Grounding and Parametric Anchoring}
With the ontology, its unification, and the reduced-core mathematics settled in Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:unified-core}, every quantitative result above depends on the parameter values used; this section grounds those values in the literature, documents their sources and ranges, and audits how far the empirical reach of the framework extends.

\subsection{Literature-Anchored Parameter Ranges}
All simulations and bifurcation analyses use parameter values drawn from the public summary statistics listed in Table~\ref{tab:params}. The baseline values are derived from FAOSTAT crop yields, IPCC forest growth rates, GRACE groundwater recharge fractions, USGS mineral production, and World Bank macroeconomic indicators. Each parameter is assigned a plausible range that spans the observed interannual and cross-country variability. For the reduced core numerical illustration, we use the validated Candidate A (\(\eta = 0.914, E_{\max} = 30, \delta_0 = 0.01, \Delta_{\text{ref}} = 1.0, \tau_m = 5\)), which places the stabilising Hopf at \(\tau_- \approx 6.88\) years, the nearby lower SNPO fold at $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}\approx7.36$ years, the upper SNPO fold at \(\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}} \approx 131.24\) years, and the destabilising Hopf at \(\tau_+\approx132.4\) years (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}); these are the original ungated three-state core's values, and the canonical effort-saturation-corrected four-state core relocates them to $\tau_-\approx3.78$, $\tau_{\mathrm{SNPO,L}}\approx5.62$, $\tau_{\mathrm{SNPO,R}}\in(64.4,64.5)$, and $\tau_+\approx150.12$~yr, which are the headline thresholds used in the Abstract and Conclusion (Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}).

\begin{table*}[t]
\centering
\small
\caption{Literature-anchored and stylised parameters for the augmented vector framework and the three-state core. Baseline values are used for the bifurcation diagrams; ranges are used for sensitivity sweeps. Sources: FAOstat (crops), IPCC (forests), GRACE (water), USGS (minerals), World Bank (macroeconomic). New entries for the abiotic reservoir ($A_0$) and critical threshold ($\epsilon$) are marked with $\dagger$. The \textbf{Core?} column flags whether a parameter is active in the three-state core's equilibrium and bifurcation analysis (Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}) or is used only in the general vector framework (Sections~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}--\ref{sec:deficit-dynamics}); see the discussion following the table for a full audit of empirical grounding by category.}
\label{tab:params}
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{l X l l l l}
\toprule
\textbf{Symbol} & \textbf{Description} & \textbf{Baseline} & \textbf{Range} & \textbf{Units} & \textbf{Core?} \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{6}{c}{\textbf{Core Ecological Parameters (Reduced Core)}} \\
\(r\) & Intrinsic growth rate (frozen-$A$ approximation) & 0.02 & 0.01--0.05 & yr\(^{-1}\) & yes \\
\(K\) & Carrying capacity (normalised) & 100.0 & -- & dimensionless & yes \\
\(q\) & Catchability coefficient & 0.001 & -- & (effort\(\cdot\)yr)\(^{-1}\) & yes \\
\(S(N)\) & Sustainable yield function & \(rN(1-N/K)\) & -- & stock/yr & yes \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{6}{c}{\textbf{Effort and Deficit Parameters (Corrected Core)}} \\
\(\eta\) (Candidate A) & Effort adjustment rate & 0.914 & 0.5--3.0 & yr\(^{-1}\) & yes \\
\(E_{\max}\) (Candidate A) & Maximum dimensionless effort & 30.0 & 15--50 & dimensionless & yes \\
\(\Delta_{\text{ref}}\) & Reference deficit for normalisation & 1.0 & 0.5--5.0 & stock/yr & yes \\
\(\delta_0\) & Baseline panic effort (persists even as \(N \to 0\)) & 0.01 & 0.005--0.05 & yr\(^{-1}\) & yes \\
\(\tau_m\) & Memory-filter relaxation timescale & 5.0 & 2--15 & years & yes \\
\(Z_{\text{ref}}\) & Reference filtered deficit & 1.0 & 0.5--5.0 & stock/yr & yes \\
\(\delta\) & Baseline-panic deficit (shifted-softplus baseline; sets $Z^*=\delta$, Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core}) & \(\ln(2)/10\approx0.0693\) & 0.02--0.20 & stock/yr & yes \\
\(k\) & Softplus sharpness (does not affect the equilibrium or the local Hopf thresholds once $\delta$ is fixed, since $\operatorname{softplus}_k'(0)=1/2$ for all $k$; the \emph{global} fold locations and cycle amplitudes are $k$-dependent --- e.g., the corrected core's lower fold is $\approx5.0$, $\approx5.57$, and $\le3.5$\,yr at $k=5,10,20$ in fixed-step persistence, rising to $\gtrsim5.58$\,yr at the collocation's effectively-exact floor, Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}) & 10.0 & 5--50 & (deficit unit)\(^{-1}\) & yes \\
\(\tau\) & Policy/institutional delay & varied & 2--25\(^{*}\) & years & yes \\

\(A^{\text{geo}}_0\) & Initial geological reservoir (frozen-$A$ limit parameter) & \(10^2 \times K\) & \(10^1\)--\(10^4 \times K\) & stock units & no \\
\(I_0\) & Initial inert reservoir & 0 & -- & stock units & no \\
\(\epsilon\) & Critical threshold fraction for \(A^{\text{act}},A^{\text{geo}}\) (service collapse) & 0.05 & 0.01--0.30 & dimensionless & no \\
\(\mathcal{T}^{\text{rec},\text{act}}\) & Recovery timescale of the active abiotic pool & computed & -- & years & no \\
\(\kappa_A\) & Abiotic turnover rate (donor-limited gross uptake beyond net regeneration; $B=R+\kappa_A N\cdot A^{\text{act}}/(A^{\text{act}}+A_0)$, Eq.~\eqref{eq:abiotic}) & 0.05 & 0--0.5 & yr\(^{-1}\) & no \\
\(\gamma_U\) & Detritus decomposition rate, $U\to A^{\text{act}}$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:detritus}); literature-anchored via Olson-type litter decomposition constants \cite{olson1963}, spanning tropical to boreal systems & 0.2 & 0.02--4 & yr\(^{-1}\) & no \\
\(\omega_A\) & Geochemical exchange rate, \(A^{\text{act}}\leftrightarrow A^{\text{act},\text{eq}}\), Eq.~\eqref{eq:abiotic} (sets $\mathcal T^{\text{rec},\text{act}}\le1/\omega_A$ uniformly in $N$) & \(10^{-3}\) & \(10^{-4}\)--\(10^{-2}\) & yr\(^{-1}\) & no \\
\(A^{\text{act},\text{eq,intrinsic}}\) & Geochemical equilibrium level of the active pool in the absence of biological turnover (independent of $N$); $A^{\text{act},\text{eq}}$ itself is the \emph{derived} quantity $A^{\text{act},\text{eq,intrinsic}}+(\kappa_A K-D^{\text{ret}})/\omega_A$, Eq.~\eqref{eq:aeq-derived}, so that the active pool's steady state at $N=K$ equals $A^{\text{act},\text{eq,intrinsic}}$ exactly, for any $\kappa_A,\omega_A$ in their stated ranges and any $D^{\text{ret}}$ consistent with the model variant in use (Section~\ref{sec:variant-registry}): $D^{\text{ret}}=\kappa_A K$ when the detritus compartment is present (general framework), collapsing the correction term to zero; $D^{\text{ret}}=0$ when it is absent (four-state core, Section~\ref{sec:four-state-core}), retaining the correction term in full & \(0.5\times K\) & \(0.2\)--\(1\times K\) & stock units & no \\
\(\mathbf{C}^A\) & Direct anthropogenic mining flow from \(A^{\text{geo}}\), Eq.~\eqref{eq:abiotic-geo} & 0.0 (illustrative) & resource-specific & stock/yr & no \\
\(A_0\) & Regeneration-saturation constant in $R(N,A^{\text{act}})=rN(1-N/K)\cdot A^{\text{act}}/(A^{\text{act}}+A_0)$ (Section~\ref{sec:four-state-rootcause}); chosen $\ll A^{\text{act}}_0$ so the frozen-$A$ limit $A^{\text{act}}/A_0\to\infty$ holds at the stated initial pool sizes above & \(0.01\times K\) & \(0.005\)--\(0.05\times K\) & stock units & yes (four-state core only) \\

\par\medskip
\noindent \textbf{Note on the memory-filter formulation:} The deficit memory $Z(t)$ is defined as a leaky integrator (Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}), under which $\tau_m$ is the ordinary relaxation timescale of a first-order filter; the characteristic equation Eq.~\eqref{eq:char-eq-corrected} is used directly, with finite $\tau_m=5$ years, for all Hopf and SNPO analyses (Appendix B). \textbf{$^{*}$See the discussion below and Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected} for why $\tau$'s range is broadened relative to the values used to derive $\tau_+$ and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$, and for which of the model's two Hopf thresholds this broader range actually supports.} \textbf{$^{\dagger\dagger}$} $\chi$ is fixed at 1 (institutional failure) for all core analysis in this manuscript (Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}); its general-framework role of interpolating toward $\chi=0$ (rational optimisation) is not exercised in Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}.
\end{table*}

\paragraph{An audit of this table's empirical grounding.}
\label{sec:parameter-audit}
Beyond the $\tau$ discussion above, a full audit of Table~\ref{tab:params} by category clarifies which entries are genuinely empirically anchored and which are not. On why treating $\tau$ as a fixed parameter does not make the delay-driven dynamics self-referential, see the discussion of mechanism versus phenomenology in Section~\ref{sec:mechanism-vs-phenomenology}.

\emph{Units and completeness.} Because $E$ is dimensionless, $\dot E$ must have units yr\(^{-1}\); since the term $Z/(Z_{\text{ref}}+Z)$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core} is dimensionless (both $Z$ and $Z_{\text{ref}}$ carry the same units), $\delta_0$ itself must be yr\(^{-1}\), as listed above. In the leaky-integrator formulation (Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}, Appendix A), the memory relaxation rate is exactly $1/\tau_m$; no separate leakage-rate parameter is needed or used anywhere in the model. $Z_{\text{ref}}$, which appears in Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core} and the equilibrium quadratic Eq.~\eqref{eq:Estar-quadratic}, is included in the table above for completeness.

\emph{A parameter with no empirical referent, isolated from the local (equilibrium and Hopf) results but not from the global folds.} The softplus sharpness $k$ is a regularisation constant controlling how closely the smooth softplus approximates $\max(0,\cdot)$; it has no physical interpretation and cannot be calibrated against any of the empirical sources listed in this table's caption. Its presence alongside genuinely literature-anchored entries risks implying an empirical grounding it does not have. Because the leaky-integrator memory equation (Eq.~\eqref{eq:Z-core}) uses the \textbf{shifted, floored} softplus, $\max(0,\text{softplus}_k(\cdot)-\ln(2)/k+\delta)$, rather than the plain softplus, the equilibrium memory is exactly $Z^*=\delta$ (Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core}), and $E^*$, $N^*$, and both Hopf thresholds $\tau_-,\tau_+$ are unchanged to machine precision under $k\in\{5,10,20,40\}$ at fixed $\delta$. Using the plain, unshifted softplus instead would make $Z^*=\ln(2)/k$ and hence let this purely numerical constant determine the model's headline bifurcation thresholds, which is precisely the failure mode the shift is designed to prevent. The outer floor is a separate, necessary correction: without it, the shift's own floor value $\delta-\ln(2)/k$ is negative whenever $\delta<\ln(2)/k$ (a real possibility at the lower end of $\delta$'s own range against the table's baseline $k=10$), which can drive $Z$ and, through the baseline-panic term, effort $E$ itself negative under a sustained large surplus; direct simulation confirms this failure mode occurs absent the floor and that the floored equation avoids it while leaving the equilibrium and Hopf thresholds unchanged (Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}). $k$ is retained in the table for completeness (it is needed to reproduce the reported results at any finite sharpness), but its baseline and range reflect numerical sensitivity testing, not an empirical distribution; $\delta$, not $k$, is the parameter that should be calibrated against any real institutional baseline-effort data.

\emph{A jointly-determined scaling system with an unremarked consequence.} Because effort $E$ is dimensionless, $q$ and $E_{\max}$ are not independently identifiable from any extraction dataset; only their product-like combinations with $r$ and $\Delta_{\text{ref}}$ are pinned down once an equilibrium is specified. At the Candidate A baseline, the equilibrium works out to $E^*\approx2.09$ and $N^*/K\approx0.90$ — that is, \textbf{the core's own equilibrium describes a resource at roughly 90\% of carrying capacity, a lightly-exploited stock, not a depleted one}; the overshoot narrative in this manuscript is entirely a statement about the dynamics \emph{away from} this equilibrium (Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:hopf-verified}), not about the equilibrium itself. This value is also sensitive to $\Delta_{\text{ref}}$: halving it from 1.0 to 0.5 (still within the table's own stated range) raises $E^*$ to $\approx4.16$ and lowers $N^*/K$ to $\approx0.79$, both computed directly from the closed-form equilibrium of Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core}. Table~\ref{tab:params} does not explain how $q=0.001$, $E_{\max}=30$, and $\Delta_{\text{ref}}=1.0$ jointly correspond to any specific resource's observed exploitation rate; these should be understood as a consistent but empirically under-determined normalisation of a dimensionless effort variable, not as independently measured quantities.

\emph{Parameters inactive in the core.} The waste damage rate $\mu_{CO_2}$, the Allee threshold $N_{\text{Allee}}$, the elasticity of substitution $\sigma_S$, the immediate waste fraction $\alpha$, and the product decay timescale $\tau_P$ belong to the general vector framework (Sections~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}--\ref{sec:deficit-dynamics}) and do not enter the three-state core's equations: the core uses pure logistic growth with no Allee term, a single dominant resource (no cross-service substitution), and decouples waste and product stocks via $\|\boldsymbol\mu\|,\|\boldsymbol\rho\|\to0$ (Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}). We have marked these with ``Core? no'' in the table above; a reader attempting to reproduce the Hopf/SNPO results from Table~\ref{tab:params} alone needs only the rows marked ``yes.''

\emph{The macroeconomic block is the table's best-grounded content.} The savings rate $\varsigma$, capital depreciation $\delta_K$, population growth $r_L$, and TFP growth $\eta_A$ are standard, well-documented quantities in economic growth accounting and are consistent with World Bank cross-country data and standard macroeconomic modelling ranges; unlike the ecological and effort parameters, they require no further caveat here.

\emph{Order-of-magnitude plausible but not independently cited.} The regeneration rate $r$, effort-adjustment rate $\eta$, and memory timescale $\tau_m$ are plausible for the kind of slow-growing renewable resource this manuscript's biological citations (IPCC forest growth, GRACE recharge) describe, but the manuscript does not cite a specific empirical study identifying $\eta=0.914$/yr or $\tau_m=5$ years for any real institutional-memory process, and — as shown in Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected} — the qualitative bifurcation sequence scales with $1/r$, so results calibrated to one resource's $r$ do not transfer to a resource with a substantially different regeneration rate without re-deriving the thresholds.

\emph{Candidates A and B lack a resource-specific narrative.} The two parameter sets used throughout Sections~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected} differ only in $\eta$ and $E_{\max}$, and neither is explicitly tied to a real resource or institutional setting (e.g., ``Candidate A represents a slow-adjusting forestry concession; Candidate B a fast-adjusting fishery''). Without such a mapping, the range $\eta\in[0.5,3.0]$ functions as a sensitivity band for the mathematics, not as a literature-anchored empirical distribution, and we recommend that any future resource-specific application of this framework construct and justify such a narrative explicitly, in the same way we did for the liquidation-channel parameter $\psi$ in Section~\ref{sec:psi-assignment}.

\emph{Demand and per-unit service requirements are not fixed coefficients.} Unlike scalar overshoot accounting, this framework does not treat per-capita consumption or service requirements as fixed technical coefficients: demand $\mathbf D_i$ is derived endogenously in Layer 4 (Section~\ref{sec:layer4}) from CES utility maximisation against prices $p_{i,c}(t)$ and disposable income $(1-\varsigma)Q(t)$, both of which evolve with the state of the economy ($K(t), L(t), A(t), T(t)$) and with scarcity itself, since $p_{i,c}$ diverges as $S_{i,c}\to0$. A genuine constant in the model is the CES exponent $\gamma$ governing the curvature of that response (addressed above), not the demand level itself.

\subsection{Unobserved Proxies}
Proxies for soil microbiome function, pollinator abundance, and freshwater lens integrity are constructed from:

- Soil organic carbon (SoilGrids) as a linear proxy for microbial C-cycling rate.

- MODIS NDVI phenology × FAO hive counts × IPBES crop dependency fractions for pollinator service.

- GRACE terrestrial water storage anomalies for groundwater lens depletion rates (see Appendix E for detailed protocols).
