\section{Discussion}
\subsection{The Hen-and-Egg Mechanism and the Positive Feedback Loop}
\label{sec:discussion-liquidation}
The corrected model captures the capital-liquidation positive feedback loop without needing a separately-barriered top-up term: because extraction is $C(t)=qE(t)N(t)$ (Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected}), any sustained increase in effort $E$ draws directly on principal whenever $qEN$ exceeds the sustainable yield $S(N)$, and the diagnostic liquidation flow $\Lambda(t)=\max(0,qEN-S(N))$ (Section~\ref{sec:core-model-eqs}) quantifies exactly how much of current extraction is drawing down the asset rather than living off yield. This, combined with the delayed effort response and the baseline panic \(\delta_0\), generates the runaway loop described in the introduction: a persistent deficit signal (delayed by $\tau$) keeps raising effort, which raises liquidation, which further degrades the stock, which raises the deficit again. The extinction equilibrium \((0, E^*_{\text{ext}})\) is not a mathematical artifact; because extraction vanishes automatically as $N\to0$ (Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected}), this boundary point is a genuine fixed point of the equations. It is the formal representation of a socio-ecological trap, where institutional memory sustains a nonzero baseline extraction effort even after the natural capital has been destroyed, though that effort does no further physical harm once $N=0$; because $E^*_{\text{ext}}$ equals the interior equilibrium's own $E^*$ (Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core}), this is literally the same baseline effort the institution would sustain at a healthy stock, now acting on nothing. The survival condition \(r > qE^*_{\text{ext}}\) is therefore not a separate threshold to be checked in addition to the interior equilibrium's own existence condition; it is that condition, so the two equilibria form a single transcritical pair rather than a bistable trap reachable from a parameter regime where the interior equilibrium is itself stable (Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core}). The trap is real whenever $r\le qE^*$ removes the positive interior equilibrium, or for any trajectory that is otherwise driven all the way to $N=0$ (the large-amplitude cycle documented in Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} troughs at $N\gtrsim10$ for the parameter ranges examined and does not itself reach $N=0$), but it does not coexist as an independently-reachable alternative to a stable, positive-stock equilibrium under the same parameters.

\paragraph{Which histories are captured by the cycle.} The liquidation mechanism above is unaffected by which resource history is ultimately captured, and remains exactly as the hen metaphor of the Introduction describes it: a sustained deficit forces liquidation, which amplifies the next deficit. Separately, the bifurcation analysis of Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} identifies \emph{which} resource histories, within either narrow bistable window ($(\tau_-,\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}})$ or $(\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}},\tau_+)$), actually end up captured by the large-amplitude cycle rather than settling at the quiet equilibrium: a history sitting at a \emph{large} stock with correspondingly low effort is captured by the cycle, while a history sitting at a low, near-collapse stock instead recovers to the quiet equilibrium. This is not a statement that liquidation only matters for abundant stocks — liquidation, as defined by $\Lambda(t)$, occurs in both cases whenever $qEN$ exceeds $S(N)$ at that instant — but rather that a long institutional delay can prevent effort from down-regulating quickly enough once a large stock has permitted it to grow, so the system overshoots into a sustained cycle instead of settling. This is a specific finding layered on top of the hen mechanism, not a replacement for it; it does not affect the vector-accounting material of Sections~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}--\ref{sec:deficit-dynamics}, which is independent of the scalar core's specific bifurcation values.

\subsection{Institutional Bridge: From Optimisation to Pathology}
The vector framework of Sections~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}--\ref{sec:deficit-dynamics} models the diagnostic accounting under functioning institutions: prices signal scarcity, and agents optimise utility, resulting in an algebraic, instantaneous allocation of extraction effort. This framework is essential for measuring multi-component overshoot. However, when institutions fail, property rights are unenforced, or ecological stress triggers panic, the rational optimisation assumption breaks down. The system transitions into a phenomenological pathology regime, where agents ignore price signals and blindly intensify extraction in response to physical scarcity. The three-state core (Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}) models this exact pathology --- consistent with experimental evidence that even trained subjects systematically misperceive stocks, flows, and delays \cite{cronin2009} --- with the baseline panic \(\delta_0\) bridging the gap between rational optimisation and pathological persistence. It is not a mathematical reduction of the original rational framework; it is the exact asymptotic limit of the generalised framework (with \(\chi=1\)) under fast market clearing and weak waste feedback. Therefore, the global attractor and the amplitude-discontinuous fold thresholds identified in Sections 7--8 represent the catastrophic dynamical consequences of institutional failure, while the vector metrics in Sections 2--5 provide the early-warning diagnostics that such a failure is imminent. This treatment of the institutional side connects to the empirical common-pool-resource literature: Ostrom's design principles \cite{ostrom1990} emphasize that the \emph{content} of governance rules (monitoring, sanctioning, graduated response), not response speed per se, determines collective-action success --- consistent with the conditional, sign-dependent reading of response speed developed in Section~\ref{sec:policy-implications} rather than a blanket recommendation about institutional pacing.

\subsection{Mechanism versus Phenomenology: The Delay as Institutional Delay}
\label{sec:mechanism-vs-phenomenology}
The delay \(\tau\) is not a fitted parameter introduced to generate cycles; it is the mathematical formalisation of the institutional delay between observing a physical deficit and mobilising additional extraction effort. In the orchard metaphor, the farmer observes the shortfall, secures credit, hires labour, and deploys machinery; this takes time. During that interval, the stock degrades further, so the new effort is applied to a smaller base, widening the deficit and forcing further liquidation. The positive feedback loop is therefore structural rather than decorative: the model assumes a causal mechanism (deficit $\to$ delayed intensification $\to$ stock destruction $\to$ deeper deficit) and derives its logical consequences, so the resulting bifurcations are structural predictions to be tested against observed collapse trajectories rather than curve-fitting artifacts.

\subsection{Policy Implications}
\label{sec:policy-implications}
The bifurcation analysis reveals a more subtle story than the premise --- shared by the policy literature already discussed in Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected} \cite{hocherman2025,mckibbin2014} --- that shorter institutional delay is uniformly preferable. Institutional delays create a wide range of monostable safety, $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}<\tau<\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}), but delays either shorter than $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ (just above $\tau_-$) or longer than $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ (just below $\tau_+$) reopen a large-amplitude, sustained cycle, in the former case as the sole attractor and in the latter two cases coexisting with the still-locally-stable equilibrium. Numerical evidence strongly supports, but does not yet prove, that each region of coexistence is separated by an unstable limit cycle acting as a basin boundary (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}); pending independent confirmation of that specific unstable-manifold structure, the policy implication is phrased conservatively as ``a large or rapidly-growing stock, combined with a slow institutional response, is empirically associated with a risk of capture by a self-sustaining boom-bust cycle.'' This reframes the policy response:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Dismantle the positive feedback loop itself (e.g., hard quotas, decoupling profit from immediate yield, cooperative governance), which limits how large effort $E$ can grow in response to an abundant stock and therefore how far into either bistable regime a well-stocked system can be pushed.
\item Reduce the underlying consumption-regeneration deficit (structural overshoot) as the primary goal.
\item Do not treat ``accelerate institutional response'' as an unconditional good for institutions whose response to scarcity is to mobilise additional extraction (the institutional-failure regime): the original ungated core shows a genuine regime near $\tau_-\approx6$--7 years, a threshold numerically comparable to documented governance lags (Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}; the effort-saturation-corrected core places the same qualitative threshold at $\tau_-\approx3.7$--$5.5$ years, Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}) in which responses faster than this threshold trap the system in a sustained oscillation, while responses of roughly a decade allow it to stabilise. Institutions should be evaluated against this threshold structure, not assumed to benefit monotonically from speed. Separately, and only for governance settings operating on multi-decade-to-century timescales, shortening delays does not eliminate the longer-horizon bistable window near $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}},\tau_+$; it only shifts those thresholds.
\item Price the open material loop (carbon pricing, nutrient recovery).
\item Monitor the vector of Service-Specific Overshoot Days and Depletion Stress Indices (Section~\ref{sec:depletion-horizon}) as an early-warning diagnostic for component-specific failures; treat rapid stock recovery or unusually high abundance, not only depletion, as a signal worth monitoring under long institutional delays, given the corrected model's finding that abundance combined with slow response is the trajectory most exposed to the large-amplitude cycle (Section~\ref{sec:discussion-liquidation}).
\item Address the baseline panic \(\delta_0\) by ensuring that institutional memory does not translate into persistent extraction pressure after a stock collapse. This requires explicit intervention to break the feedback loop, such as moratoriums and the establishment of independent resource authorities.
\end{enumerate}
Appendix~I (Section~\ref{sec:viability}) gives this list a precise mathematical vocabulary — distinguishing which of these levers change the system's attractor structure from which merely change where the current state sits relative to it — without claiming any new quantitative result beyond what is established above.

\subsection{Falsifiable Predictions}
\label{sec:falsifiable-predictions}
The preceding sections establish a structurally consistent theoretical framework; they do not constitute an empirical validation of it. To make the boundary between the two explicit, and to give the framework genuine empirical contact points, we enumerate its testable claims here, organised by how directly they can be checked against existing data and by how much of the framework's own machinery each claim depends on. This section does not report new results: it restates claims already made in Sections~\ref{sec:depletion-horizon}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected} in a form designed for empirical adjudication, and is explicit about which claims require no new parameter fitting and which would require it.

\paragraph{Tier 1: diagnostic metrics (testable against existing data, no dynamical fitting required).} The vector of Abiotic Depletion Horizons $\mathcal H^{\bullet}_{i,c}$ and Depletion Stress Indices $\Sigma^{\bullet}_{i,c}$ (Section~\ref{sec:depletion-horizon}) are computable directly from independently measured stocks and flows, and Section~\ref{sec:adh-results} computes them on real public data (GRACE/G3P groundwater, USGS phosphate, RAM fisheries), without fitting any of the reduced core's dynamical parameters ($\eta,\tau,\delta_0$, etc.). Two specific, falsifiable claims follow:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{The two-pool split correctly separates renewable depletion from geological mining.} For a resource with both an active, bioavailable component and a documented geological reservoir (e.g., a coastal aquifer with both active recharge and a deep, fossil component), $\mathcal H^{\text{act}}_{i,c}$ and $\mathcal H^{\text{geo}}_{i,c}$ should diverge sharply, and $\Sigma^{\text{geo}}_{i,c}$ should register chronic stress even where $\mathcal H^{\text{geo}}_{i,c}$ itself is long (Section~\ref{sec:depletion-horizon}). Candidate test systems include GRACE-monitored aquifers with documented fossil and renewable fractions (e.g., the Northwest Sahara Aquifer System, the High Plains Aquifer's northern versus central/southern segments) and coastal freshwater lenses with independently estimated recharge rates.
\item \textbf{The productivity illusion is empirically detectable as aggregate-stable, component-declining trajectories.} The framework predicts that some real systems will show stable or growing aggregate output while a specific service component's $\Sigma^{\bullet}_{i,c}$ registers chronic stress (Section 1, Section~\ref{sec:productivity-gap}). The canonical candidate is industrial agriculture: stable or rising cereal yields maintained via synthetic nitrogen and phosphorus inputs while soil organic matter, micronutrient density, or pollinator-service indices decline (the same literature already cited in Section~\ref{sec:evidence-of-committed-degradation} for the premise of committed multi-component overshoot). This is falsified if, across a representative sample of such systems, the productivity-gap vector $\mathbf G_i$ (Section~\ref{sec:productivity-gap}) fails to register any divergence between aggregate output and component-level service trajectories.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Tier 2: the bifurcation topology (testable against time series, requires independently estimated institutional response lags).} The core model's headline claim is that institutional response delays $\tau<\tau_-$ produce sustained, large-amplitude oscillations, while $\tau$ in the wide monostable interval above $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ allows convergence to a stable equilibrium (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}). This is falsifiable in two specific ways:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Response-lag threshold.} For resource-management systems where the institutional response lag can be independently estimated (e.g., from legislative or regulatory records, following the methodology of \cite{hocherman2025}), systems with lags shorter than the corrected $\tau_-$ (Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}: $\approx3.7$--$6.9$ years across Candidates A and B, depending on which effort-equation form is used) should disproportionately exhibit sustained boom-bust cycles in stock and effort/extraction-pressure time series, relative to systems with longer lags. Candidate test systems are the specific governance episodes already surveyed by \cite{hocherman2025}: fisheries management reforms, the EU Marine Strategy Framework Directive and Baltic Sea Action Plan, and the Montreal Protocol, each of which has both a documented response lag and reasonably well-resolved stock trajectories.
\item \textbf{Basin-dependent capture.} The framework predicts that within a bistable window, it is specifically \emph{large, well-stocked} historical trajectories with slow institutional adjustment that are captured by the large-amplitude cycle, not depleted or scarcity-stressed ones (Section~\ref{sec:discussion-liquidation}). This is a more specific, and more easily falsified, claim than the threshold itself: it predicts that the historical stock trajectory at the onset of a slow-responding governance regime, not only the response lag, determines outcome.
\end{enumerate}
This tier requires independently estimating $\tau$ for a real system, which the framework does not currently provide a general protocol for (Section~\ref{sec:parameter-audit}); we treat this as the single largest obstacle to testing Tier 2 and do not consider it resolved. \textbf{An empirical search for Tier 2 test systems, reported in full in Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}, was subsequently carried out and found no candidate that survives close scrutiny on all four screening criteria used there;} this does not weaken the falsifiability of the claim itself, but it means Tier 2 should currently be read as an articulated, well-specified empirical target rather than as a claim with existing case-level support, and the obstacle it faces is more specific than the general estimation difficulty noted here: Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt} identifies a structural tension between the record length required to observe a multi-century oscillation (the model's own predicted period at realistic regeneration rates) and the length of any real institutional record with a stable, unchanging response rule. A necessary pre-registration gate follows from the structural-robustness analysis of Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}: a candidate must satisfy the stock-side condition (for the base and four-state cores, $r$ inside the bounded instability window; for stage-structured stocks, $r\,g\approx1.5$--$1.6$) \emph{and} the institutional-side condition (a responsive review interval $T_r$ in the model's predicted range, annual review $T_r=1$~yr lying below it), so a candidate failing either condition cannot exhibit the mechanism regardless of record length.

\paragraph{The short-maturation-delay branch: a falsifiable, decadal-period Tier 2 sub-claim.} The structural-robustness tests of Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt} (structural-robustness paragraph) show that the Tier 2 obstacle above --- the multi-century record-length mismatch --- applies to the base and four-state cores and to late-maturing stocks, but not to fast-maturing, fast-growing stocks: with a maturation delay of $1$--$2$~yr the institutional window relocates to $r\approx0.8$--$1.6$~yr$^{-1}$, with $\tau$-windows of roughly $2.6$--$7.8$~yr (inside the documented lag distribution \cite{hocherman2025}) and predicted periods of $\approx8$~yr ($r=0.8$, $g=2$~yr) and $\approx4$~yr ($r=1.6$, $g=1$~yr). Because real governance is periodic review rather than continuous delay, the sub-claim is operationalised in review-interval space (Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}, periodic-review paragraph): under sample-and-hold governance the anchovy-class prediction becomes a sustained oscillation with a $\approx4$-yr biomass cycle (effort/quota cycle $\approx12$~yr) for a responsive review interval $T_r\approx3$--$4$~yr, robust to $30\%$ multiplicative assessment error, and the sprat-class an $\approx8$-yr biomass cycle (effort/quota cycle $\approx60$~yr) for $T_r\approx6$--$12$~yr; annual review ($T_r=1$) is predicted stable, consistent with the cross-sectional null across 42 annual-managed RAM stocks \cite{ricard2012}. The falsifiable observable is the effort/quota-utilisation series, which carries the large-amplitude signal ($80$--$240\%$ of $E^*$) at the longer effort periods: a small-pelagic stock (anchovy- or sprat-class) managed under a continuous harvest-control rule with a responsive review interval of $3$--$12$~yr should exhibit sustained oscillation in the predicted effort band whose onset is dateable to the review-cycle regime, while a stock that remains quiet over several predicted effort periods would falsify it. Because the effort oscillation grows to full amplitude only over $\sim10^3$~yr, onset tests on decadal records must target the growth transient (a monotone run-up in effort and quota utilisation after the review-cycle change) rather than a completed cycle, and the sampling-design implications are quantified by the effort-signal power analysis of the periodic-review paragraph. The cod-class continuous-delay middle band does not survive periodic-review governance (stable for all $T_r\in[1,20]$~yr) and is therefore not an empirical target. The sub-claim rests on the stage-extension and sampled-governance computations, verified at representative points (linear criterion plus dt-converged nonlinear integration, with assessment-error robustness) but not yet collocation-classified or mapped at full parameter resolution; cohort resonance predicts overlapping periods, so the two mechanisms must be discriminated explicitly in any candidate case.

\paragraph{Tier 3: the core's quantitative thresholds (lowest priority; requires simultaneous, independent estimation of $r,\eta,E_{\max},\tau_m$, and $\delta_0$ for a single real resource).} The specific numerical values of $\tau_-,\tau_+,\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}},\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ reported for Candidates A and B (Sections~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}, \ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}) are functions of a jointly-calibrated parameter set that Section~\ref{sec:parameter-audit} already documents as empirically under-determined (neither candidate is tied to a specific real resource, and $q,E_{\max}$ are not independently identifiable from any extraction dataset). Testing these specific numbers accordingly requires calibrating the full core to one real system with all relevant parameters independently measured, which is rare; we rank this the lowest priority of the three tiers and do not expect it to gate near-term empirical engagement with the framework, consistent with the parameter audit's own conclusion that Candidates A and B should currently be read as sensitivity bands for the mathematics rather than literature-anchored empirical distributions.

\paragraph{What this section does not claim.} Confirming the Tier 1 diagnostics would validate the vector accounting architecture, not the core's dynamical predictions; confirming Tier 2 would validate the qualitative bifurcation mechanism, not its precise numerical thresholds. None of the three tiers, even if fully confirmed, would establish the SNPO folds' classification (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) or the infinite-time persistence conjecture (Appendix H), both of which are open mathematical questions independent of any empirical test. We consider Tier 1 the most immediately actionable next step precisely because it requires no new dynamical parameter fitting, in keeping with the recommendation, discussed throughout Section~\ref{sec:parameter-audit}, that this framework's empirical program should proceed from its most directly measurable components outward rather than by fitting the full high-dimensional system at once. The tiered programme above also does not currently include the unified core v2 (Section~\ref{sec:unified-core-fixed}); that variant's separate, minimal falsifiability note is given there, and its integration into Tiers~1--3 remains an open item listed in its own scope/status paragraph.

\subsection{Limitations and Further Work}
\label{sec:limitations-further-work}
We emphasise that the current model is a \textbf{structural proof-of-concept}, not a validated predictive Earth-system model. Key limitations and clear future directions are:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Energy and exergy omission.} Explicit energy fluxes are not resolved; they are folded into effective rate parameters. A fifth layer tracking exergy and entropy is required for quantitative predictions.
\item \textbf{Macro-level substitution elasticity.} While the inner service index \(\mathcal{S}_{\text{agg}}\) allows for varying elasticities of substitution among ecological services, the outer aggregate production function (Cobb-Douglas) fixes the elasticity of substitution between manufactured capital (\(K\)) and the natural capital service index at unity. This places the economic layer in the weak-sustainability regime: manufactured and natural capital are substitutable at unit elasticity, so the model cannot represent a strict biophysical limit on the substitution of dead natural capital. The nested-CES generalisation \[ Q(t) = A(t)\Bigl[\theta\bigl(\hat K^\alpha \hat L^\beta\bigr)^{(\rho-1)/\rho} + (1-\theta)\mathcal{S}_{\text{agg}}^{(\rho-1)/\rho}\Bigr]^{\rho/(\rho-1)} \] makes the regime explicit through the single parameter \(\rho\): \(\rho=1\) recovers the Cobb-Douglas (weak-sustainability) regime, while \(\rho<1\) makes \(K\) and \(\mathcal{S}_{\text{agg}}\) complements whose elasticity of substitution is below unity, and in the limit \(\rho\to0\) output is bounded by natural services regardless of manufactured-capital accumulation --- the strong-sustainability regime. The framework therefore treats weak and strong sustainability not as competing hypotheses but as distinct regimes indexed by the substitution elasticity, consistent with the component-specific and rate-conditional treatment of Section~\ref{sec:weak-vs-strong}. Adopting a specific \(\rho<1\) value is an empirical question that the current parameter audit does not resolve; the Cobb-Douglas form used throughout this manuscript is retained as the \(\rho=1\) regime.
\item \textbf{Empirical calibration burden.} The high dimensionality of the full vector framework---specifically the stock-service mapping matrices \(\mathbf{M}_i\) and the smooth Liebig weights \(w_{m,i,c}\)---creates a substantial empirical calibration burden. Operationalising this framework for a specific regional or global economy will require advanced statistical dimensionality reduction or proxy-mapping techniques to parameterise the cross-dependencies between heterogeneous natural capital stocks and service flows.
\item \textbf{Parameters are stylised, not estimated.} Key coefficients are drawn from literature ranges but not fitted to time-series data. Empirical estimation is left to future work.
\item \textbf{Spatially explicit generalisation.} The aggregation-bias problem requires a fully spatially resolved formulation with a defined projection kernel and moment-closure terms (Section~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}). A related but distinct gap is that the present state variables carry no notion of how a stock's mass is distributed across space or across statistically independent units, so the model cannot yet represent either the spatial dependence of certain services or the added fragility of concentrating stock into fewer, larger units; both require moving beyond the current lumped-parameter representation and are left to the same future research program.
\item \textbf{Product stock calibration.} The product decay timescale \(\tau_P\) and immediate waste fraction \(\alpha\) are illustrative; real values depend on product lifespans and recycling infrastructure.
\item \textbf{Weak-waste-feedback scope.} The asymptotic reduction to the three-state core assumes negligible feedback from waste to natural capital. For resources where waste strongly damages regeneration (e.g., pollution-driven collapse), the core must be extended to include those feedbacks. The general form the extension must take is a sink-side dependence of the sustainable yield on the accumulated-waste state, $B_s(\mathbf x, \mathbf W)$: the flow condition then reads $E(t)\le B_s(\mathbf x(t),\mathbf W(t))$, so the sink side enters sustainability on equal footing with the source side, and the stock-versus-flow-pollutant distinction (Section~\ref{sec:waste-loop}) determines whether the feedback acts through cumulative load (stock pollutants) or emission rate (flow pollutants)---a direction for future work.
\item \textbf{Self-delay and topological limits.} The economic feedback in the three-state core introduces a delay in the effort equation (the delayed argument is \(Z(t-\tau)\), itself a filtered function of \(N(t)\) and \(E(t)\)). Because of this delayed feedback term, the system does not strictly fall under the Mallet-Paret \& Sell \cite{mallet1996} Poincaré-Bendixson theorem for purely cyclic feedback systems. The exclusion of chaotic attractors and the uniqueness of the collapse cycle are therefore verified via numerical continuation rather than proven purely by topological means.
\item \textbf{Persistence of global bifurcations.} The global folds identified in the three-state core --- the amplitude-discontinuous fold thresholds $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$, whose SNPO classification is collocation-confirmed at the level of the real-multiplier signature for the corrected core's lower fold and remains to be pinned exactly (fold location and collision structure) for the lower and upper folds alike (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}) --- are structural predictions of the leading-order asymptotic model. Rigorous proof of the persistence of these global bifurcations in the full high-dimensional system over infinite time horizons requires Geometric Singular Perturbation Theory (GSPT) extended to DDEs, which is beyond the scope of this work (see Appendix H).
\item \textbf{Abiotic reservoir closure.} Biological regeneration is modelled as a transfer from effectively infinite abiotic reservoirs; geological recycling is omitted. The framework thus conserves mass within the anthropogenic loop, not the full planetary geochemical cycle.
\item \textbf{Single liquidation channel used outside Section~\ref{sec:two-channel}.} For simplicity and continuity with the rest of the manuscript, the equilibrium and bifurcation analysis in Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected} outside of Section~\ref{sec:two-channel} uses the pure stock-culling limit ($\psi=1$). We showed in Section~\ref{sec:two-channel} that this choice does not affect the equilibrium or the Hopf threshold locations exactly, and shifts the SNPO fold locations only slightly ($\lesssim0.5\%$ at the parameters tested), but it does understate the depth of the large-amplitude cycle for resources whose liquidation is better represented as recruitment suppression. Section~\ref{sec:psi-assignment} works through illustrative $\psi$ assignments for the resource categories used elsewhere in this manuscript (fisheries, forestry, groundwater, soil zinc, pollinators), each grounded in which physical sub-process dominates for that resource; extending this to a full vector $\boldsymbol\psi_{i,c}$ integrated with the stock-service mapping of Section~\ref{sec:service-to-stock} is left to future work.
\item \textbf{Sensitivity of the model to how nonlinearities are combined.} The core relies on several interacting nonlinearities (the barrier function protecting non-negativity, the effort ODE's self-saturation, the deficit memory filter, and the extraction term's behaviour at $N=0$). Small-looking asymmetries in how these are combined — e.g., applying a barrier to only one of two extraction channels, or using two different placements of a saturation factor in different equations — can silently break non-negativity, invalidate a claimed equilibrium, or change the qualitative interpretation of which trajectories are most exposed to the large-amplitude cycle (abundance-and-slow-response rather than scarcity-driven; Section~\ref{sec:discussion-liquidation}). Every claimed equilibrium in this manuscript is confirmed by direct substitution into the governing equations and by directly simulating boundary cases (Sections~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected}, \ref{sec:equilibrium-core}); the same practice is recommended for any extension of this framework, since algebraic derivation alone does not reliably catch these issues.
\item \textbf{Fold classification of the remaining thresholds: the corrected core's lower fold is now confirmed at collocation grade, the upper folds are SNPO-consistent but not yet pinned.} The corrected core's lower boundary is resolved as a tight pair of individually-genuine folds (previous item), and its stable-cycle fold is now confirmed at collocation grade: adaptive-mesh piecewise collocation with variational Floquet-multiplier tracking gives a real dominant multiplier monotonically approaching $+1$ along the branch ($0.240$ at $\tau=4.0\to0.964$ at $\tau=5.5815$; orbit residual ${\sim}10^{-12}$, monodromy dt-converged) --- the defining saddle-node-of-periodic-orbits signature, rather than a period-doubling (would be $-1$) or a torus (a complex pair on the unit circle) --- and the small-unstable-branch fold's real $+1$ crossing ($1.0514$ at $\tau=5.584\to0.998983$ at $\tau=5.587$) was already established by shooting. For the remaining upper-window folds ($\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ in both cores), the same collocation and variational-Floquet evidence gives a real multiplier $\approx1$ at the fold edge with no period-doubling or torus signature anywhere in the window, but the effort gate $(1-E/E_{\max})$ approaches its singular limit near the cycle's $E$-peak, inducing a $6$--$8\%$ monodromy error that prevents pinning $\mu=+1$ exactly; the upper folds are therefore SNPO-consistent but not yet collocation-pinned. This remaining gap is flagged wherever the SNPO classification is used (Sections~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}, \ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}, Appendix H) and is a priority for future work.
\item \textbf{The viability-theoretic formalisation of Section~\ref{sec:viability} is uncomputed.} The safe parametric regime $\Theta_{\text{safe}}$ and the basins $\mathcal B(\mathcal A_{\boldsymbol\theta})$ it defines give the policy discussion of Section~\ref{sec:policy-implications} a precise vocabulary, but neither has actually been computed for Candidates A or B, or for the full augmented system; doing so is a natural next step once the fold-classification and infinite-time-persistence questions above are resolved, since $\Theta_{\text{safe}}$'s definition depends on the attractor structure those questions concern.
\item \textbf{The thermodynamic tether of Section~\ref{sec:thermodynamic-tether} has not been integrated into the core's bifurcation analysis.} Adding the capital state $K_{\text{cap}}$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:kcap-tether}) expands the core from three to four state variables, and neither the Hopf thresholds $\tau_-,\tau_+$ nor the SNPO folds have been recomputed for the tethered system; the new capital-relaxation eigenvalue could shift, merge with, or leave qualitatively unchanged the existing delay-induced eigenvalues, and this has not been checked. Locating the true collapse threshold $\mu_E^{\text{SN}}$ analytically (rather than by numerical bisection, as done here for one illustrative parameterisation) and unifying $K_{\text{cap}}$ with the general framework's own manufactured-capital variable $K(t)$ (Section~\ref{sec:layer4}) are both left to future work.
\item \textbf{Locating global folds correctly requires continuation, not fixed-initial-condition bisection.} A fixed-initial-condition, insufficient-horizon bisection can mislocate a global fold by more than 20\,yr (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) owing to critical slowing down near the neighbouring Hopf point, and can separately miss a second, narrower bistable window immediately adjacent to the other Hopf point entirely. Both folds are instead located via numerical continuation of the periodic orbit itself, confirmed by multi-million-year persistence/collapse tests from multiple initial conditions; this is a general methodological lesson for numerical bifurcation work on this class of DDE, not a one-off correction specific to this model.
\item \textbf{The lower boundary is a pair of distinct folds, not a single SNPO.} For the corrected core's lower boundary, continuation and shooting-Floquet analysis show two nearby but \emph{distinct} folds, each individually genuine: the stable large-amplitude cycle persists through $\tau=5.5815$ in the collocation continuation (residual $\sim10^{-12}$) with a \emph{real} dominant Floquet multiplier rising toward $+1$ ($0.2396$ at $\tau=4.0$ through $0.9644$ at $\tau=5.5815$; imaginary part identically zero, Section~\ref{sec:data-code}) so that its fold lies above $\tau=5.5815$, and the small-amplitude unstable branch from $\tau_-$ folds at $\tau\approx5.587$ (amplitude $\approx22$, period $\approx314$\,yr; a real Floquet multiplier crosses $+1$ there). Because the two folds belong to different periodic-orbit families, the $\sqrt{\tau-\tau_{\text{SNPO}}}$ collision scaling of a single saddle-node of periodic orbits is not applicable to this boundary (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}). The upper-window folds remain candidate SNPOs pending Floquet-multiplier tracking along those branches. The ungated core's upper-window attractor is confirmed as a period-1 limit cycle by fixed-step RK4, independent method-of-steps adaptive RK45, and Floquet-spectrum cross-checks (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}).
\item \textbf{The model's two Hopf crossings occupy different, distinguishable regimes of institutional-delay realism.} $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ and $\tau_+$ require 65--135+ years across the parameter combinations tested, in tension with a narrowly-drawn institutional-delay range. Checking the model's \emph{other} Hopf crossing, $\tau_-$ (together with its nearby fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$), against a fuller empirical distribution of governance response times \cite{hocherman2025} resolves this tension: $\tau_-\approx6$--7 years for both Candidate A and B, and 4--25 years across the literature-anchored ranges for $r,\eta$ (Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}), falls within documented lags for fisheries reform, EU marine directives, and the Montreal Protocol. Direct simulation confirms that $\tau_-$ marks a sharp transition: delays shorter than $\tau_-$ trap the system in a sustained oscillation; delays of a decade or so allow it to settle to the stable equilibrium. $\tau_-$, not $\tau_+$, is accordingly identified as the model's headline threshold — a finding that faster institutional response is not always safer, in a model where the relevant delay range has real documented analogues; as detailed in Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}, the comparison is at the level of lag magnitudes, and no case-level validation currently exists. $\tau_+$ and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ remain genuine features of the same bifurcation diagram, tied to the resource's own slow biological timescale $1/r$ rather than to institutional response speed (Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}), and their real-world relevance is correspondingly limited to governance settings operating on multi-decade-to-century timescales (e.g., the climate-change end of the documented distribution) rather than typical resource management.
\item \textbf{A single delay parameter conflates institutional delay with ecological relaxation time.} The preceding item identifies \emph{that} $\tau_+$ and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ are governed by $1/r$ rather than by institutional-response parameters; the deeper structural reason is that the model uses a single scalar $\tau$ to stand in for two causally distinct delays---the time between observing a deficit and mobilising effort, and the resource's own biological relaxation time---which happen to be numerically close for $\tau_-$ at these parameters but diverge sharply for $\tau_+$. A structurally cleaner resolution would decouple these into two explicit delays rather than reinterpreting one symbol's meaning across regimes. The most promising route is not an arbitrary second delay constant (which would merely relocate the "why this value?" justification burden noted throughout Section~\ref{sec:parameter-audit} onto a new, unanchored parameter), but an \textbf{age- or stage-structured reformulation} of the stock equation: splitting $N$ into an immature (recruit) class and a mature (reproductively active) class, connected by an explicit \textbf{maturation delay} that is, unlike an ad-hoc ecological time constant, an independently measurable quantity already reported in the fisheries and forestry stock-assessment literature on which the resource categories draw (Section~\ref{sec:psi-assignment}). This would let $\tau$ (the institutional delay) and the maturation delay enter the model as two mechanistically distinct, separately-calibrated delays, in the spirit of classical stage-structured delay-differential population models (e.g., the Nicholson blowflies equation and its maturation-delay extensions). This is a substantial reformulation---it changes the state space, the equilibrium, and the entire Hopf/SNPO analysis, not a parameter adjustment---and is left as a well-defined, high-priority item of future work rather than attempted here. A mass-consistent compartmental form of this maturation delay (a juvenile cascade replacing the bare lag, preserving the stoichiometric invariant) is analysed in Appendix~\ref{sec:scaffold}.
\item \textbf{The delay-induced instability is confined to a bounded regeneration-rate window.} The Hopf pair $\tau_-,\tau_+$ exists only for regeneration rates $r$ in an interval of roughly $(\approx0.008,\approx0.022)$ yr$^{-1}$ at Candidate A's effort response (up to $\approx0.061$ yr$^{-1}$ at the maximal effort response $\eta=3.0$), for the effort-saturation-corrected core used throughout this manuscript's quantitative results, disappearing at both edges: below it the fixed institutional delays are negligible on the stock's slow timescale, and above it the stock responds too fast for the delayed feedback loop to destabilise. Real fish stocks ($r\gtrsim0.2$ yr$^{-1}$) lie far above the window, so the model predicts them to be delay-independently stable; the $\tau_-$ oscillatory branch is consequently confined to slow-regenerating systems (forests, aquifers) whose model-implied oscillation periods are centuries long, making the mechanism structurally untestable in real records --- the instability domain and any empirically testable domain are disjoint.
\item \textbf{The bounded-window result is robust to the two most plausible structural extensions in the directions the empirical search relied on, with one flagged exception that restores testability.} Two independent additions --- a Droop-type nutrient/quota limitation and a maturation delay in the stock equation --- were tested against the window, each preserving the core's interior equilibrium, with the pipeline validated against the manuscript's own $\tau_-,\tau_+$ values (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) and the window bounds (Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}). The nutrient coupling leaves the window essentially unchanged (upper edge $\le0.023$~yr$^{-1}$ at $\eta=0.914$; $\le0.071$~yr$^{-1}$ at $\eta=3.0$; zero crossings at $r\ge0.2$), because a growth-coupled pool cannot be slow at large $r$ (its relaxation scales with $r$); the only $r$-independent slow pool is the four-state core's $\omega_A$ exchange, which narrows the window. The maturation delay confirms the fast-$r$ prediction for late-maturing stocks ($g\ge5$~yr: no institutional crossings at $r\ge0.6$; the decadal oscillations such stocks show are the biological cohort mechanism) and reinforces the slow-$r$ centuries-long barrier (at $r=0.02$, any $g\ge1$~yr already oscillates at zero institutional delay, $P\approx250$--$360$~yr). One flagged exception: for fast-maturing, fast-growing stocks ($g=1$--$2$~yr; $r\,g\approx1.5$--$1.6$) the maturation delay relocates the institutional window into fish range --- e.g.\ $r=0.8$, $g=2$~yr: $\tau$-window $(2.6,7.8)$~yr, period $\approx8$~yr; $r=1.6$, $g=1$~yr: $\tau$-window $(1.6,3.5)$~yr, period $\approx4$~yr --- placing decadal institutional oscillation inside documented governance lags for sprat-class stocks (Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}, structural-robustness paragraph). These bands are characterized at finite parameter resolution and not claimed as closed; they are the natural next empirical target, with the cohort/institutional discrimination problem stated explicitly. Under sampled (periodic-review) governance --- the form real institutions actually implement --- the anchovy-class window relocates to review intervals $T_r\approx3$--$4$~yr (an $\approx4$-yr cycle, robust to $30\%$ assessment error), annual review is stable, consistent with the cross-sectional absence of the predicted institutional signal among annual-managed stocks, and the cod-class middle band vanishes (stable for all $T_r\in[1,20]$~yr), so the continuous-delay cod band is removed from the empirically live set (Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}, periodic-review paragraph).
\item \textbf{The abiotic pool $A^{\text{act}}$ must be retained as a fourth state variable, and doing so leaves the $\tau_-$ finding intact but does not resolve its lack of case-level empirical support.} Section~\ref{sec:four-state-rootcause} shows, from primary biogeochemical literature on deep-ocean ventilation ages and soil organic matter turnover, that the active abiotic pool relaxes an order of magnitude more slowly than the stock $N$ at the model's baseline parameters, so it cannot be eliminated as a fast auxiliary variable; the correct asymptotic target of the reduction is a four-state $(N,A^{\text{act}},Z,E)$ core rather than the three-state core used throughout Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}. The four-state core's $\tau_-$ threshold is essentially unchanged from the three-state value; under the ungated effort law its upper bistable window is comparable in width to the three-state core's ($\approx1$--$1.5$\,yr at the donor-limited equilibrium), while under the effort-saturation-corrected law --- the canonical form --- the upper window is wide, $(64.4,150.1)$\,yr (Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}). The finding does not overturn any headline result, but it is a genuine correction to the three-state core's own internal completeness that should be carried forward into any future extension of this work, and the two cores should be read side by side (Section~\ref{sec:four-state-core}) rather than the four-state result being treated as fully supplanting the three-state one, since the latter's closed-form tractability is lost in the four-state system.
\item \textbf{An extensive search for real-world case studies testing the $\tau_-$ threshold found none that survive close scrutiny, for two identified structural reasons.} Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt} reports a systematic, multi-round search across more than thirty candidate resource systems (fisheries, aquaculture, groundwater, surface water, rangeland, wildlife harvest, forestry, and produced-capital markets), screened against four explicit criteria (continuous institutional feedback, a short and independently dateable response lag, absence of a major independent confound, and individual-resource data). No candidate satisfies all four once examined at primary-source, individual-station resolution. The root causes identified are (i) a selection-bias pattern in which the long, well-monitored records needed to test the claim exist primarily for systems that became visible crises, and visibility is itself correlated with the presence of a fast, non-institutional confound (climate, ocean regime shifts, cohort resonance); and (ii) a more fundamental timescale mismatch, established directly via the timescale-rescaling test of Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}, whereby the model's own oscillation period is centuries long at any biologically realistic regeneration rate, exceeding the length of essentially any real institutional record with a stable, unchanging response rule. This is reported as a primary empirical finding of this work, not merely a limitation of the search: it means the $\tau_-$ threshold, while grounded in an aggregate distribution of documented institutional response lags (Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}), does not currently have support from any individual, unconfounded case study, and closing this gap is identified as the framework's single highest-priority empirical open problem, ahead of the SNPO classification and the infinite-time persistence conjecture in terms of tractability, since it requires new data collection and case selection rather than further mathematics.
\item \textbf{A formal MSE-style power analysis of the effort signal is the designated next empirical step.} The effort-signal detectability numbers reported in Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt} (periodic-review paragraph) were computed for deterministic operating-model runs with multiplicative observation error; a full Management Strategy Evaluation-style closed-loop power analysis --- an operating model with recruitment and observation noise, a detection statistic on the effort/quota series, and a power-vs-($T_r$, horizon, noise) surface --- is the pre-registered next step before any field data collection or reform advocacy, and would quantify the detectability problem under realistic process noise.
\end{itemize}

\section{Conclusion}
This paper has presented a unified vector-valued sustainability framework grounded in a mass-conserved augmented system comprising natural capital, product stocks, waste, a natural detritus pool, an explicit abiotic reservoir, and an inert sink. This closure resolves the fundamental category error---equivocation between bookkeeping identity and constitutive law---that plagues many aggregate models. The logistic growth law is recovered as the leading-order frozen-\(A\) approximation; the frozen-\(A\) limit itself is not justified at the baseline parameters, where the active abiotic pool must be retained as a fourth state (Section~\ref{sec:four-state-core}), so the logistic law is the leading-order limit of the full system only over horizons on which the active pool's relative change is small.

The general framework provides mass-conserving accounting within the anthropogenic material loop, a vector of Abiotic Depletion Horizons as a dimensionally consistent diagnostic, and a formal bridge to a three-state core model via Tikhonov's theorem on finite time intervals. The core---the leading-order slow manifold of the augmented system's \((N,Z,E)\) projection in the frozen-\(A\) institutional-failure regime (\(\chi = 1\))---exhibits a bifurcation sequence: a stabilising Hopf at \(\tau_-\), a destabilising Hopf at \(\tau_+\), and two amplitude-discontinuous folds, $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ just above $\tau_-$ and $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ just below $\tau_+$ (i.e., $\tau_-<\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}<\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}<\tau_+$), that give rise to a large-amplitude cycle coexisting with the stable equilibrium in two narrow bistable windows, $(\tau_-,\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}})$ and $(\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}},\tau_+)$, flanking a wide monostable interval in between. In the canonical effort-saturation-corrected four-state core the same sequence holds ($\tau_-\approx3.78$~yr, $\tau_+\approx150.1$~yr), with a narrow lower window and a wide upper window (Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}). This sequence, and the closed-form equilibrium and boundary conditions it rests on, is verified by direct substitution into the governing equations across two independent parameter sets and across one-at-a-time variation of every parameter in the literature-anchored ranges (Section~\ref{sec:robustness-corrected}).

The results rest on three distinct levels of confidence, together with a fourth, separate point concerning empirical scope. The itemised evidentiary breakdown --- what is rigorously established, strongly supported, conjectured, and empirically open --- is given in Section~\ref{sec:limitations-further-work}. In summary, the closed-form equilibrium and the two Hopf crossings $\tau_-,\tau_+$ are on solid analytical footing; the two amplitude-discontinuous folds $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}},\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ are strongly supported by continuation-based, multi-million-year simulation, their classification as genuine Saddle-Node-of-Periodic-Orbits bifurcations being collocation-confirmed at the level of the real-multiplier signature for the corrected core's lower fold (real dominant Floquet multiplier $\to+1$, dt-converged) and SNPO-consistent for the upper folds (real multiplier $\approx1$ at the fold edge, no period-doubling or torus signature), with the exact fold locations and collision structures remaining to be pinned. For the canonical effort-saturation-corrected four-state core, the folds have been located by dt-converged long-horizon bisection ($\tau_{\mathrm{SNPO,L}}\approx5.62$~yr, $\tau_{\mathrm{SNPO,R}}\in(64.4,64.5)$~yr), giving a narrow lower bistable window and a wide upper window ($\approx86$~yr) whose coexisting cycle is reached from high-stock histories for delays as short as $\approx85$--$95$~yr and from depleted-stock histories only near the destabilising threshold ($\tau\gtrsim140$~yr) (Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}); their collocation-grade classification remains open. And the persistence of this bifurcation sequence in the full augmented system over infinite time is a structurally plausible but formally unproven conjecture (Appendix H). The policy implications this evidentiary structure supports --- including the counterintuitive finding that institutional responses faster than $\tau_-$ can themselves destabilise the system, and the recommendation to dismantle the underlying positive feedback loop rather than rely on real-time steering --- are developed in full in Section~\ref{sec:policy-implications}.

The empirical programme's current status rounds out this picture. Against more than thirty real-world systems, no case provides a clean, unconfounded test of the fast threshold at the base or four-state cores' multi-century periods; the sharpest case, the Peruvian anchoveta, is confound-dominated (its only robust periodicity is the $\approx3.7$-yr El Ni\~no recurrence). Under the periodic-review (sampled) governance form real institutions implement, annual review is predicted---and, across the RAM cross-section, observed---stable, while a responsive review interval of roughly three to four years in a fast-maturing small pelagic is predicted to restore a multi-year institutional cycle. That prediction remains pre-registered but untested, because no real system currently operates such a review interval; the criterion stands for any future responsive reform or controlled institutional experiment.

\section*{Data and Code Availability}\label{sec:data-code}
All code and data supporting this analysis are available at [repository URL]. The supplement provides the DDE solver, the parameter scans, and a permanent verification suite (\texttt{verify\_flux\_ledger.py}, \texttt{verify\_macro\_stability.py}, \texttt{verify\_macro\_scaling.py}, \texttt{verify\_unified\_ledger.py}, \texttt{verify\_unified\_characteristic.py}, \texttt{verify\_unified\_eta10\_hopf.py}, \texttt{verify\_exact\_cubic.py}, \texttt{verify\_abiotic\_nonnegativity.py}, \texttt{verify\_general\_feedback.py}, \texttt{verify\_proposition1\_scalar\_aggregation.py}, \texttt{verify\_new\_effort\_law\_audit\_proposal.py}, \texttt{verify\_snpo\_unstable\_orbit.py}, \texttt{verify\_snpo\_fold\_refinement.py}, \texttt{verify\_snpo\_fold\_correction.py}, \texttt{verify\_corrected\_core\_folds.py}, \texttt{verify\_hopf\_nature.py}, \texttt{verify\_ungated\_floquet.py}, \texttt{verify\_fourstate\_pipeline.py}, \texttt{verify\_kappaA\_sweep.py}, \texttt{verify\_fourstate\_fold\_tracking.py}, \texttt{verify\_branch\_structure.py}, \texttt{verify\_hybrid\_effort\_law.py}, \texttt{verify\_hybrid\_folds.py}, \texttt{verify\_unified\_eta\_sweep.py}, \texttt{verify\_fourstate\_sensitivity.py}, and an accompanying \texttt{README.md}) that re-derives the paper's principal algebraic and numerical results directly from the governing equations: the mass-conservation identity of Table~\ref{tab:ledger}; the unified active-pool equilibrium formula (Eq.~\eqref{eq:aeq-derived}); the fast-subsystem stability claim and the $\varsigma$ and $\Delta^{\text{econ}}$ corrections of Section~\ref{sec:asymptotic-reduction}; the structural and algebraic claims of the unified-core comparison (Section~\ref{sec:unified-core}); the closed-form characteristic equation and local-Hopf exclusion (Section~\ref{sec:unified-characteristic}) and the local-Hopf characterisation at $\eta=10$ (supercritical onset at $\tau_-$; recurring intermittency above $\tau_+$ with a monotonically increasing large-amplitude time fraction (classified as homoclinic-like slow-fast relaxation, not a torus)); the exact cubic reduction and completeness certificate for the three-state core's local Hopf crossings (Section~\ref{sec:exact-cubic}); the donor-limiting non-negativity correction to the active-abiotic-pool equation and the four-state equilibrium shift it induces (Sections~\ref{sec:abiotic}, \ref{sec:four-state-model}); the general feedback characteristic equation unifying the three-state and unified-core-v2 local bifurcation structures (Section~\ref{sec:general-feedback}); the construction underlying Proposition~1 (Section~\ref{sec:productivity-illusion-narrative}); the parameter search and root-cause analysis for the rejected alternative effort law and the additive repair of Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-hybrid} (Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}); the basin-boundary orbit near $\tau_-$ and the stable branch's persistence-loss boundary (fixed-step bisection $\approx5.57$, superseded by the collocation's persistence through $\tau=5.5815$); the fold structure of the corrected core's unstable branch (genuine fold at $\tau\approx5.587$; post-fold convergence to the stable cycle); the corrected-core fold structure (upper fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx148.3$) with its ghost-transient and $k$-sensitivity analysis; and the Candidate B fold structure (supercritical lower Hopf; upper fold $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx76.075$) (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}); the recomputation of the four-state core's full numerical pipeline (eigenvalues, Hopf thresholds $\tau_-,\tau_+$, the SNPO folds, and the $\kappa_A$ stability threshold) at the donor-limited equilibrium, validating the pre-correction values exactly and quantifying the shifts, including the correction of the four-state \emph{ungated} core's upper fold to $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}\approx130.77$ (the corrected-law folds are $\tau_{\mathrm{SNPO,L}}\approx5.62$~yr and $\tau_{\mathrm{SNPO,R}}\in(64.4,64.5)$~yr; Section~\ref{sec:four-state-model}); the mapping of the corrected core's lower-fold branch structure, with the branch's $\max(0,\cdot)$ floor inactive throughout (so the fold does not arise from floor activation): the small-unstable branch genuinely folds at $\tau\approx5.587$ (real Floquet multiplier crossing $+1$: $1.0514$ at $5.584$, $0.998983$ at $5.587$), that the stable branch persists to at least $\tau=5.5815$ with a \emph{real} dominant Floquet multiplier (imaginary part identically zero in \texttt{snpo\_lower\_fold\_variational.csv}, \texttt{snpo\_lower\_fold\_tip.csv}, \texttt{snpo\_lower\_fold\_push.csv}) rising from $0.2396$ at $\tau=4.0$ through $0.9473$ at $\tau=5.574$ to $0.9644$ at $\tau=5.5815$ toward $+1$ --- the standard real-multiplier signature of a fold of periodic orbits --- and that the two folds are distinct events of different periodic-orbit families, so the $\sqrt{\tau-\tau_{\text{SNPO}}}$ collision scaling of a single SNPO is not applicable to this boundary (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}); the subcriticality of the corrected core's two Hopf points, including the first Lyapunov coefficient at $\tau_-$ ($l_1>0$, quantified from the branch-amplitude scaling and the characteristic-equation growth rate) and the dynamical tests at both crossings (Section~\ref{sec:effort-saturation-fix}); and the period-1 nature of the large-amplitude attractors in both cores' upper bistable windows (corrected core at $\tau=148.4,150,152$\,yr and ungated core at $\tau=131.8$\,yr), established by dt-convergent Poincar\'e sections and spectral analysis, the Floquet-multiplier spectrum of the ungated core's upper-window cycle (all multipliers inside the unit circle --- a stable limit cycle, not a torus) and of the small unstable orbit near its lower Hopf point (dominant multiplier $>1$, the unstable branch born at the subcritical Hopf; its distinct fold at $\tau\approx5.587$ means it is not the SNPO partner of the stable cycle, Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}), computed by shooting the orbits as fixed points of the discrete segment map, together with a method-of-steps adaptive RK45 replication (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}). The suite re-derives these results from the paper's current equations and can be re-run to verify any subsequent revision.

A separate set of scripts supports the structural-robustness and empirical analyses of Sections~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt} and \ref{sec:adh-results}: \texttt{droop\_test.py} (Droop nutrient/quota r-window test), \texttt{stage\_r\_window.py} and \texttt{stage\_decomp2.py} (maturation-delay windows and nonlinear $\tau=0$ classification), \texttt{sampled\_governance.py} (periodic-review/sample-and-hold model), \texttt{noise\_robustness.py} (assessment-error robustness), \texttt{ram\_crosssection.py} (cross-sectional spectral test over RAM stocks), and \texttt{cod\_sprat\_discriminator.py} (three-way cod/sprat discrimination). The DDE verification suite comprises \texttt{dde\_core.py} (RK4 DDE integrator with variational monodromy and single-shooting orbit solver), \texttt{pseudo\_arclength.py} (Keller pseudo-arclength continuation of periodic orbits through the folds), \texttt{multishoot.py}, \texttt{verify\_floquet\_points.py} (shooting-based Floquet checks at the folds), \texttt{snpo\_piecewise\_collocation.py} (adaptive-mesh piecewise collocation with variational Floquet multipliers --- the tool behind the lower-fold SNPO confirmation of Section~\ref{sec:limitations-further-work}; its per-$\tau$ output tables are \texttt{snpo\_lower\_fold\_variational.csv}, \texttt{snpo\_lower\_fold\_tip.csv}, \texttt{snpo\_lower\_fold\_push.csv}), \texttt{dde\_fold\_classification.jl} (Julia/DDEBifurcationKit target script), and the MATLAB/Octave drivers \texttt{dde\_biftool\_manuscript.m} (DDE-BIFTOOL v3.x fold-classification driver), \texttt{track\_fold\_branches.m}, \texttt{three\_state\_core\_dde\_rk4.m}, and \texttt{dde\_diagnostic\_selfcontained.m}. The empirical data files used are \texttt{ram\_target\_stocks.csv} (RAM Legacy v4.66 subset; \cite{ricard2012}), \texttt{g3p\_basin\_gwsa.csv} (groundwater storage anomalies from G3P v1.12; \cite{guentner2024}), \texttt{fisheries\_adh.csv} (fisheries ADH table), and \texttt{peru\_anchoveta\_catch\_sau.csv}; the G3P v1.12 gravity product itself is available at \url{https://doi.org/10.5880/G3P.2024.001}.

\appendix
\section{Appendix A: Deficit Memory as a Leaky Integrator}
The deficit memory $Z(t)$ is defined as a \textbf{leaky integrator} (first-order low-pass filter of the deficit signal), rather than as a finite-window cumulative integral $Z(t) = \int_{t-\tau_m}^t \text{softplus}_k( \bar{H}(s) - S(N(s)) ) ds$ with \(\bar{H}(s) = \min( \phi(N) q E_{\max} N, D )\). The latter would introduce a second delay-like timescale $\tau_m$ that interacts with the institutional delay $\tau$ inside the characteristic equation through a factor $(1-e^{-\lambda\tau_m})$, and its equilibrium value would not be a simple closed form usable for linearisation (Appendix B), since the finite-window integral of a constant softplus value over the window is $\tau_m\cdot\text{softplus}_k(0)$, not a value pinned down independently of $\tau_m$. The leaky integrator avoids both problems, provided its source term uses the \textbf{shifted, floored softplus} of Section~\ref{sec:deficit-definitions} rather than the plain softplus: the plain softplus alone would make the equilibrium $Z^*=\text{softplus}_k(0)=\ln(2)/k$ depend on the purely numerical sharpness parameter $k$ (undermining the entire point of eliminating $k$-dependence, Sections~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core} and~\ref{sec:parameter-audit}), and without the outer floor, $Z$ could be driven negative under a sustained large surplus, in turn driving effort $E$ negative through the baseline-panic term (Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}). The leaky integrator used throughout this manuscript is therefore
\begin{equation}
\dot Z = \frac{1}{\tau_m}\Bigl[\,\max\Bigl(0,\ \text{softplus}_k\bigl(C - S(N)\bigr) - \frac{\ln 2}{k} + \delta\Bigr) - Z\,\Bigr],
\label{eq:leaky-integrator-A}
\end{equation}
where $C=qEN$ is the single, barrier-free extraction flow of Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected} and $\delta>0$ is the baseline-panic deficit of Section~\ref{sec:deficit-definitions}. This is strictly non-negative by construction (the outer $\max(0,\cdot)$ guarantees it regardless of the sign of the shifted softplus argument) and differentiable except at the measure-zero set where the floor binds, and it reduces the model from a delay-integro-differential equation to a genuine single-delay ordinary DDE: $\tau_m$ is now simply the relaxation time of an ordinary linear filter, and no separate small-$\tau_m$ limit is required anywhere in the analysis. At equilibrium ($C^*=S(N^*)$ by definition), the softplus argument is exactly zero, $\text{softplus}_k(0)=\ln(2)/k$ exactly cancels the explicit $-\ln(2)/k$ shift, the floor is inactive (since $\delta>0$), and
\[
Z^* = \delta,
\]
a genuinely exact, closed-form value independent of \emph{both} $\tau_m$ and $k$, verified by direct substitution into Eq.~\eqref{eq:leaky-integrator-A} and by numerical integration to steady state.

For the full vector framework, the corresponding leaky-integrator deficit is:
\[
\dot{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i}^{\text{phys}} = \frac{1}{\tau_m}\Bigl[\,\max\Bigl(\mathbf 0,\ \text{softplus}_k\!\bigl( \mathbf{C}_i(s) - \mathbf{M}_i^\top \mathbf{S}_i(s) \bigr) - \frac{\ln 2}{k}\mathbf 1 + \boldsymbol\delta\Bigr) - \boldsymbol{\Delta}_i^{\text{phys}}\,\Bigr],
\]
with $\mathbf C_i$ the single extraction flow of Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected} (no separate $\bar{\mathbf C}_i$ term) and $\boldsymbol\delta$ the (componentwise) baseline-panic deficit vector.

\section{Appendix B: Linearisation and the Characteristic Equation}
Because the deficit memory $Z(t)$ is a leaky integrator (Appendix A) rather than a finite-window integral, no singular limit $\tau_m\to0$ needs to be considered: $\tau_m$ is an ordinary filter relaxation time throughout. The linearisation of the corrected system, and the resulting characteristic quasi-polynomial, follow the standard theory of linear delay-differential equations \cite{hale1993,diekmann1995}; the linearisation proceeds directly.

Linearising Eqs.~\eqref{eq:stock-core}--\eqref{eq:effort-core} about the equilibrium of Section~\ref{sec:equilibrium-core}, with $x=N-N^*$, $z=Z-Z^*$, $y=E-E^*$, gives the three linear equations
\[
\dot{x} = A_N x + A_E y, \qquad
\dot{z} = B_N x + B_E y - \frac{1}{\tau_m} z, \qquad
\dot{y} = C_E y + C_Z z(t-\tau),
\]
with coefficients as given in Section~\ref{sec:core-model} (using $\text{softplus}_k'(0)=\tfrac12$ for the linearisation of the leaky-integrator source term). This is a genuine, non-singular, single-delay linear DDE system in three variables; the characteristic quasi-polynomial, Eq.~\eqref{eq:char-eq-corrected}, is a cubic in $\lambda$ with a single exponential factor $e^{-\lambda\tau}$, and admits no further simplification analogous to the old singular limit — nor does it need one, since $\tau_m$ no longer competes with $\tau$ as a second delay. We solve Eq.~\eqref{eq:char-eq-corrected} for purely imaginary roots directly by numerical root-tracking (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}), rather than by algebraic separation into a transcendental pair of real/imaginary equations, since the latter approach becomes unwieldy once genuinely three coupled state variables are involved.

\section{Appendix C: Numerical Methods and Stable Evaluation}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Softplus/sigmoid:} Use stable evaluation with asymptotic approximations to avoid overflow.
\item \textbf{DDE solver:} fixed-step RK4 with a circular delay buffer, cross-checked against independent method-of-steps adaptive integrators and, for periodic orbits, Fourier spectral collocation with Newton/LM correction (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}).
\item \textbf{Sensitivity:} one-at-a-time parameter variation across the literature-anchored ranges of Table~\ref{tab:params}, cross-checked by direct simulation at representative points.
\end{itemize}

\section{Appendix D: Geometric Competition Driving the Global Bifurcation}
Although the local normal form \cite{faria1995} is not by itself sufficient to establish the global bifurcation, the physical mechanism that plausibly governs the amplitude and stability of the large-amplitude cycle---and the location of the amplitude-discontinuous fold thresholds $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}},\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ (candidate Saddle-Nodes of Periodic Orbits, SNPO)---is a geometric competition between two nonlinearities, consistent with the single-flow extraction law of Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected} (no separately-barriered liquidation term appears in the dynamical equations).
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Regeneration curvature (Bounding):} The logistic term \(rN(1-N/K)\) has a negative second derivative. As the stock \(N\) drops during a large-amplitude cycle, the marginal regeneration rate falls, creating a restoring force that bounds the trajectory and (away from an Allee threshold) prevents total extinction.
\item \textbf{Effort self-saturation (Driving):} The delayed, memory-filtered deficit feedback (Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core}) drives effort $E(t)$ upward when the deficit is persistent, but the $-\eta E^2/E_{\max}$ term caps its growth at $E_{\max}$. Because extraction is $qEN$ (Section~\ref{sec:natcap-corrected}), this cap on effort — not a separately-barriered liquidation term — is what prevents extraction from diverging even as $N$ falls; it also, however, locks the system into a high-extraction regime even after the stock begins to recover, which is the destabilising mechanism.
\end{enumerate}
Each SNPO fold plausibly occurs at the parameter threshold (i.e., critical delay $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ or $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$, Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) where the driving effect of delayed, saturating effort growth overcomes the bounding effect of regeneration curvature and installs a large-amplitude cycle as a coexisting attractor alongside the still-locally-stable equilibrium. As established in Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}, an intervening unstable limit cycle of the kind a genuine fold requires has been located and confirmed unstable near $\tau_-$ for both the corrected and ungated cores (shooting-based Floquet multipliers exceeding $1$), although its collision with the stable branch at the fold --- and hence the SNPO classification of the upper-window folds --- remains to be demonstrated, and the corrected core's lower boundary is in fact a tight pair of individually-genuine folds rather than a single SNPO. This geometric account should therefore be read as a plausible mechanism consistent with the numerical evidence, not as a proof of the SNPO classification.

\section{Appendix E: Data Generation Protocols for Unobserved Proxies}
\subsection{Soil Microbiome Function Proxy}
We construct a composite microbial activity index \(M_{\text{mic}}(i,t)\) for each grid cell \(i\) and year \(t\) as:
\[
M_{\text{mic}}(i,t) = \alpha_1 \cdot \text{SOC}(i,t) + \alpha_2 \cdot \text{pH}(i,t) + \alpha_3 \cdot \text{clay}(i,t),
\]
where SOC is soil organic carbon (kg/m\(^2\)) from SoilGrids 2.0, pH and clay fraction are from the same source. The coefficients \(\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\alpha_3\) are estimated from the Earth Microbiome Project (EMP) diversity indices (Shannon entropy) via linear regression on the EMP metadata. The resulting index is normalised to the [0,1] interval and used as a scalar multiplier on the microbial sub-yield \(Y_{\mathcal{R},i,c}\) in the smooth Liebig function.

\subsection{Pollinator Function Index}
The pollinator service \(P(i,t)\) is computed as:
\[
P(i,t) = \text{NDVI}_{\text{phen}}(i,t) \cdot \frac{H(i,t)}{H_{\text{ref}}(i)} \cdot \sum_{c} f_{c}(i) \cdot D_{c}(i,t),
\]
where:

- \(\text{NDVI}_{\text{phen}}\) is the seasonal amplitude of MODIS NDVI (a proxy for flowering intensity),

- \(H(i,t)\) is the national honeybee hive count from FAOstat,

- \(f_c(i)\) is the crop-specific pollination dependency fraction from IPBES \cite{ipbes2019},

- \(D_c(i,t)\) is the cultivated area of pollinator-dependent crop \(c\).

The index is normalised to the global mean of the reference period (2000--2010).

\subsection{Freshwater Lens Integrity}
For coastal aquifers, the freshwater lens volume \(V_{\text{lens}}(i,t)\) is inferred from GRACE terrestrial water storage anomalies \(\Delta\text{TWS}(i,t)\) and WaterGAP recharge estimates \(R_{\text{WG}}(i,t)\) via the simplified water balance:
\[
V_{\text{lens}}(i,t) = V_0(i) + \int_0^t \left[ R_{\text{WG}}(i,t') - E_{\text{ext}}(i,t') - \Delta\text{TWS}(i,t') \right] dt',
\]
where \(E_{\text{ext}}\) is the external (non-groundwater) component of TWS change, estimated from hydrological models. The lens integrity index is \(L(i,t) = V_{\text{lens}}/V_0\).

\subsection{Symbol Reuse and Disambiguation}
\label{sec:symbol-table}
Table~\ref{tab:symbol-reuse} lists every symbol in this manuscript that is deliberately reused with two distinct meanings in two different sections, together with how each occurrence is disambiguated. This table records the disambiguation conventions relied on throughout this manuscript, so that any future revision introducing a new symbol, or a new use of an existing one, can be checked against it before the new usage is added (the conventions governing Sections~\ref{sec:abiotic}, \ref{sec:tech-recovery}, and \ref{sec:natcap-corrected} in particular).
\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\small
\caption{Deliberately reused symbols and their disambiguation.}
\label{tab:symbol-reuse}
\begin{tabular}{lp{5.3cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Symbol} & \textbf{Distinct meanings (disambiguated by context)} \\
\midrule
$\delta$ & (a) Baseline-panic deficit, Sections~\ref{sec:deficit-definitions}, \ref{sec:generalized-effort}, \ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:four-state-core} (units: stock/yr; sets $Z^*=\delta$). (b) Formerly also the CES service-output elasticity exponent (Section~\ref{sec:layer4}); renamed $\delta_S$ there to remove the collision, so $\delta$ alone always refers to (a) throughout this manuscript. \\
$K$ & (a) Carrying capacity of the dominant resource stock (Sections~\ref{sec:frozen-A}, \ref{sec:core-model}, dimensionless/normalised, written as bare $K$). (b) Manufactured capital, always written $K(t)$ with an explicit time argument (Section~\ref{sec:layer4}, monetary units). Never co-occurs in one equation. \\
