(b) Labour elasticity in the Cobb-Douglas/CES outer function (Section~\ref{sec:layer4}). (c) Effort-decay coefficient in the unified core's effort equation (Section~\ref{sec:unified-core-fixed}). \\ $\eta$ & (a) Effort-adjustment rate in the cores (Sections~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}--\ref{sec:four-state-core}). (b) Labour-output elasticity in $L_{\max}(Q)$ (Section~\ref{sec:layer4}). \\ $\nu$ & (a) Product-recycling coefficient $\boldsymbol\nu_i$ (Section~\ref{sec:waste-loop}). (b) Productivity-degradation coefficient in the $A_{\text{TFP}}$ equation (Section~\ref{sec:layer4}). \\ $\lambda$ & (a) Product-decay coefficient $\lambda_i$ (Section~\ref{sec:waste-loop}). (b) Waste-damage sensitivity $\lambda_{j,i,c}$ (Section~\ref{sec:deficit-definitions}). \\ $\gamma$ & (a) CES exponent of the inner service index, $\sigma_S=1/(1-\gamma)$ (Section~\ref{sec:layer4}). (b) Detritus decomposition rate $\gamma_U$ (written $\gamma_U$, never bare) (Section~\ref{sec:abiotic}). \\ $\psi$ & (a) Liquidation-channel split in the two-channel extension (Section~\ref{sec:two-channel}). (b) Soil-microbiome/proxy scaling in Appendix~E (written $\psi_{i,c}$). \\ $\kappa$ & (a) Turnover parameter of the two-channel extension (Section~\ref{sec:two-channel}). (b) Abiotic turnover rate $\kappa_A$ (Section~\ref{sec:abiotic}). (c) Regulation sensitivity $\kappa_{\ell,i,c}$ (Section~\ref{sec:deficit-definitions}). \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\section{Appendix G: Formal Rationale for the Asymptotic Reduction}
This appendix provides the formal verification of the Tikhonov conditions required to reduce the generalised vector framework to the three-state core on finite time intervals. A scoping note on the status of this reduction is warranted. The classical Tikhonov and Fenichel theorems \cite{tikhonov1952,fenichel1971,fenichel1979} are stated for ordinary differential equations; the augmented system reduced here is a mixed ODE-DAE singular-perturbation problem, and the reduction of the full delayed augmented system is justified on finite time intervals by the same ODE-based argument applied to the fast variables (with the delay entering only through the slow variables, which are held fixed on the fast timescale), together with the compactness and semigroup properties of the DDE solution operator. A fully rigorous infinite-dimensional statement for the delayed system is provided by the recent infinite-dimensional Fenichel-type theorems \cite{hummelkuehn2022,kuehnsulzbach2024} subject to their assumptions (spectral gap, compact memory kernel) being verified; that verification is the same open item recorded in Appendix H.6. In the meantime the reduction is reported as numerically verified (Appendix C and the accompanying verification suite) rather than as a theorem.

\paragraph{Note on the price variable.}
In the fast subsystem, the price \(p\) is treated as an algebraic variable determined by instantaneous market clearing, not as a differential variable. This is consistent with the standard assumption of fast price adjustment: in the singular limit \(\varepsilon \to 0\), the price adjustment speed \(\gamma_p \to \infty\), yielding the algebraic condition \(D(p) = qEN\) (or the corresponding vector market-clearing condition). The mixed ODE-DAE structure is index-1 and falls within the scope of singular perturbation theory for DAEs. The stability of the fast ODE variables is verified below, and the algebraic equation has a unique solution by the strict monotonicity of demand.

\subsection{The Fast Subsystem and Quasi-Steady States}
Setting \(\varepsilon = 0\) in the fast subsystem (\ref{eq:fast-ode}-\ref{eq:fast-dae}) yields the algebraic constraints for the quasi-steady states \(\mathbf{y}^* = (K^*, L^*, A^*, T^*, p^*)\):
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Market Clearing (\(f_p = 0\)):} The price \(p^*\) adjusts instantaneously to equate demand \(D(p^*, Q^*)\) with the physical supply \(q E N\). Given the strictly monotonic demand function derived from the CES utility, there exists a unique, strictly positive root \(p^*(E, N, Q^*)\).
    \item \textbf{Macroeconomic Equilibria (\(f_K=f_L=f_A=f_T=0\)):} Setting the derivatives of \(K, L, A, T\) to zero yields unique, stable algebraic roots \(K^*(Q^*, \Delta^{\text{econ}})\), \(L^*(Q^*)\), \(A^*(Q^*, \Delta^{\text{econ}})\), and \(T^*(Q^*, \Delta^{\text{econ}})\). Since \(Q^*\) depends on the service index \(\mathcal{S}_{\text{agg}}\) (which depends on \(N\)) and the cleared price \(p^*\), these roots are well-defined functions of the slow variables: \(\mathbf{y}_{\text{macro}}^* = \mathbf{h}_{\text{macro}}(N, E)\).
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Stability of the Fast Subsystem}
To apply Tikhonov's theorem, the quasi-steady state \(\mathbf{y}^*\) must be an asymptotically stable equilibrium of the fast subsystem (the boundary layer system) for any fixed values of the slow variables \(\mathbf{x} = (N,E,Z,A,I)\); in the frozen-\(A\) limit used for the three-state reduction below, this reduces to holding the three-state projection \((N,Z,E)\) fixed.

The market-clearing block has a strictly negative eigenvalue (since demand is strictly decreasing in price), decoupled from the macroeconomic block by construction. \textbf{The macroeconomic block's own Jacobian $\mathbf{J}_y=\partial\mathbf f/\partial\mathbf y$, restricted to $(K,L,A_{\text{TFP}},T)$, is \emph{not} block-triangular}: because $K,L,A_{\text{TFP}}$ all enter the shared output $Q(t)=A_{\text{TFP}}\hat K^\alpha\hat L^\beta\mathcal S_{\text{agg}}^{\delta_S}$ (Section~\ref{sec:layer4}), every one of $\partial\dot K/\partial L,\ \partial\dot K/\partial A_{\text{TFP}},\ \partial\dot L/\partial K,\ \partial\dot L/\partial A_{\text{TFP}}$ is generically nonzero, confirmed by direct symbolic differentiation of Eqs.~\eqref{eq:k-corrected}--\eqref{eq:a-corrected}; a block-triangularity argument alone is consequently not a valid basis for concluding stability once this coupling is accounted for. The stability conclusion itself nonetheless holds, verified instead by direct numerical evaluation: at the macroeconomic block's own fixed point $\mathbf y^*_{\text{macro}}=\mathbf h_{\text{macro}}(N,E)$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:k-corrected}--\eqref{eq:t-corrected} solved simultaneously for illustrative baseline-consistent parameters), the finite-difference Jacobian's eigenvalues all have strictly negative real part, confirmed across a sweep of the deficit-forcing term $w\|\boldsymbol\Delta^{\text{econ}}\|/\Delta_{\text{ref}}$ from $0$ to $5$ (Section~\ref{sec:data-code}). This is reported as a numerically verified property of the specific parameterisation checked, not as a structural consequence of triangularity; establishing it analytically for the fully general case remains open.

\subsection{The Reduced Slow Subsystem}
By Tikhonov's theorem, for sufficiently small \(\varepsilon > 0\), the dynamics of the full system are approximated by the reduced slow subsystem on the slow manifold \(\mathbf{y} = \mathbf{h}(\mathbf{x})\). In the frozen-\(A\) limit used for the three-state reduction (Section~\ref{sec:frozen-A}), the reduced subsystem is:
\begin{align}
\dot{N} &= rN\Bigl(1 - \frac{N}{K}\Bigr) - qEN, \\
\dot{Z} &= \frac{1}{\tau_m}\Bigl[\,\max\Bigl(0,\ \text{softplus}_k\bigl(qEN-S(N)\bigr) - \frac{\ln 2}{k} + \delta\Bigr) - Z\,\Bigr], \\
\dot{E} &= (1-\chi) I_{\text{profit}}^*(E, N) + \chi \left[ \eta E \left( \frac{Z(t-\tau)}{\Delta_{\text{ref}}} - \frac{E}{E_{\max}} \right) + \delta_0 \frac{Z(t-\tau)}{Z_{\text{ref}} + Z(t-\tau)} \right].
\end{align}
The $\dot Z$ equation here uses the same \textbf{shifted, floored softplus} established in Sections~\ref{sec:deficit-definitions} and~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}, not the plain softplus: substituting the unshifted or unfloored form would silently reintroduce, into the formal reduction itself, exactly the two failure modes those sections identify and repair --- a $k$-dependent equilibrium ($Z^*=\ln(2)/k$ rather than $Z^*=\delta$, destroying the $k$-independence of the bifurcation thresholds established in Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}) and an unprotected sign on $Z$ under sustained surplus (which can in turn drive effort $E$ negative via the baseline-panic term, violating $E\in[0,E_{\max}]$; Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}). The reduced system stated here is therefore, term for term, exactly Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-generalized} of Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort} --- with the baseline-panic term $\delta_0Z_\tau/(Z_{\text{ref}}+Z_\tau)$ placed \emph{inside} the $\chi$-bracket, matching that equation's own form --- together with the leaky-integrator memory of the same section; no separate barrier factor $\phi(N)$ appears, and no separate reduction step is needed to arrive at it. Setting \(\chi = 1\) (institutional failure) eliminates the profit term, yielding exactly the three-state core model of Section~\ref{sec:core-model}, with the same shifted, floored softplus used consistently throughout. The decoupling of the waste \(\mathbf{W}\) and product \(\mathbf{P}\) stocks is guaranteed by the formal limit \(\|\boldsymbol{\mu}\|, \|\boldsymbol{\rho}\| \to 0\), ensuring they do not feed back into the \(\dot{N}\) equation on the timescale of the collapse. This completes the formal asymptotic reduction on finite time intervals.

\section{Appendix H: Conjecture on the Persistence of the Saddle-Node of Periodic Orbits under Vector Coupling and Multiple Delays}
We present the formal conjecture for the persistence of the saddle-node of periodic orbits (SNPO) under the vector coupling, the cumulative memory structure, and the slow drift of the abiotic reservoir and institutional integrity variable. We distinguish this from a theorem, as the required infinite-time geometric singular perturbation theory for DDEs is not yet established in full generality. \textbf{This conjecture rests on a premise that is itself not fully established}: as discussed in Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} and Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}, the global-folds structure of the three-state core is strongly supported by simulation, and an intervening unstable limit cycle of the kind a genuine fold requires has been located and confirmed unstable near $\tau_-$ for both the effort-saturation-corrected and the original ungated core via shooting-based Floquet analysis; nevertheless, whether that unstable branch collides with the stable large-amplitude branch at a fold with the characteristic $\sqrt{\tau-\tau_{\text{SNPO}}}$ scaling of a saddle-node of periodic orbits --- and whether the upper-window folds are SNPOs at all --- has not been established, and the corrected core's lower boundary is in fact a tight pair of individually-genuine folds rather than a single SNPO (Section~\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}). Assumption~2 below (``the three-state core possesses a transverse SNPO'') should therefore be read as inheriting that same caveat; the conjecture in this appendix is consequently a conjecture about the persistence of a numerically well-supported but not yet fully proven core-level phenomenon, one level of rigor further removed from a theorem than in the core-only case.

\subsection{H.1 Setup}
\paragraph{Scope disclosure: the slow-drift quantity $C$ is a parameter, not a state.} Beyond the frozen-$A$ caveat already noted below, this appendix's slow-variable set $(N,E,A,C)^\top$ includes a slowly-drifting quantity $C$ that is \textbf{not a state variable of any governing equation in Sections~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}--\ref{sec:four-state-core} or Eq.~\eqref{eq:unified-core-v2}}: it denotes a generic parameter (for example, an institutional or governance parameter, or a slowly-varying abiotic coefficient) that a future extension might allow to drift adiabatically. It is retained to demonstrate that the persistence argument's structure is not disturbed by adding one further slowly-varying parameter, with $\dot C=\mathcal O(\varepsilon^2)$; the conjecture and its numerical support (Section~H.5) do not depend on $C$'s value or on any specific functional form for its drift. The result is formulated for parameters: a reader may hold $C$ fixed at any constant (equivalently, delete it from the list) throughout without altering the argument. Let the full augmented system be posed on the Banach space $\mathcal{C} = C([-\max(\tau, \tau_m), 0], \mathbb{R}^{m+4})$, where $m$ is the number of fast vector modes (including waste and product components), and the slow variables are $(N, E, A, C)^\top$ (the latter two being adiabatic parameters). Here $C$ denotes the institutional integrity variable specific to this appendix, distinct from the extraction flow $\mathbf{C}_i$ used elsewhere in the paper. The system is:
\[
\dot{\mathbf{X}}(t) = \mathcal{F}(\mathbf{X}(t), \mathbf{X}(t-\tau), \mathbf{X}(t-\tau_m), \varepsilon),
\]
where $\varepsilon$ is the small coupling parameter that multiplies the off-diagonal terms of the stock-service matrix and the slow drift rates of $A$ and $C$ (with $\dot{A} = -R(N) = \mathcal{O}(1)$ but relative change $\mathcal{O}(A/N)$, and $\dot{C} = \mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^2)$).

\subsection{H.2 Assumptions}
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Spectral gap:} The linearised DDE generator at the interior equilibrium has a spectral gap: exactly two eigenvalues (or a complex pair) with real parts greater than $-\rho$ (the slow $(N,E)$ modes), and all other eigenvalues have real parts less than $-\rho - \delta$, with $\delta > 0$. This is verified numerically via the Nyquist criterion for the parameter ranges of interest (Candidate A and B). A direct check at the four-state donor-limited equilibrium quantifies the margin: at $\tau=0$ the slow set is a complex pair at $\Re=+0.000836$ plus a real mode at $\Re=-0.001032$, separated from the fast mode ($\Re=-0.2835$) by a gap of $\approx0.28$ (the $\approx1.9\times10^{-3}$ separation is between the unstable pair and the slow stable mode, within the slow set), so the two-slow-modes structure holds; however at $\tau=7$ (inside the safe window) the slow pair sits essentially on the imaginary axis ($\Re\approx-1.8\times10^{-6}$), so the gap is structurally present but only marginally robust --- $\rho$ must be taken tiny and $\delta\approx10^{-3}$ at best, which the persistence argument should treat as a fragility of the assumption rather than a comfortable margin.
    \item \textbf{Reduced system (core):} At $\varepsilon = 0$ and with $(A, C)$ fixed at $(A_0, C_0)$ (the frozen-$A$ limit), the three-state core possesses a transverse SNPO at $\tau = \tau_0(A_0, C_0)$. Specifically, the Poincaré map $\mathcal{P}_{0,\tau}$ has a fixed point $x^*$ at $\tau_0$ with:
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $D_x \mathcal{P}_{0,\tau_0}(x^*) - I$ has a one-dimensional kernel spanned by $v$.
        \item Transversality: $\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial \tau} \mathcal{P}_{0,\tau}(x^*)\right|_{\tau_0} \notin \operatorname{Range}(D_x \mathcal{P}_{0,\tau_0}(x^*) - I)$.
        \item Quadratic tangency: $\langle w, D_{xx} \mathcal{P}_{0,\tau_0}(x^*)(v, v) \rangle \neq 0$, where $w$ spans the left nullspace.
    \end{itemize}
    \item \textbf{Smoothness:} $\mathcal{F}$ is $C^2$ in all arguments, and the perturbation $\varepsilon$ enters as a Lipschitz regular term. The memory kernel is bounded and compact.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{H.3 Conjecture}
Under Assumptions 1–3, there exists an $\varepsilon_0 > 0$ and a $C^1$ function $\tau^*(\varepsilon, A, C)$ defined for $|\varepsilon| < \varepsilon_0$ and $(A, C)$ in a neighbourhood of $(A_0, C_0)$, with $\tau^*(0, A_0, C_0) = \tau_0$, such that for each such $\varepsilon, A, C$, the full DDE system has an SNPO at $\tau = \tau^*(\varepsilon, A, C)$. Moreover, the SNPO persists as a genuine fold bifurcation of a periodic orbit, and the qualitative phase-space topology (stable collapse cycle + unstable limit cycle as basin boundary) remains structurally unchanged. The slow drift of $A$ and $C$ moves the SNPO threshold adiabatically, preserving the fold structure.

\subsection{H.4 Formal Rationale}
The proof of this conjecture would require the following steps, each of which is feasible but collectively constitute an extension of GSPT:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Poincaré map:} The DDE semiflow defines a $C^1$ Poincaré map $\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon,\tau,A,C}: \Sigma \to \Sigma$, where $\Sigma$ is a local transversal section. The map is a compact perturbation of the identity (due to the smoothing property of the DDE solution operator).
    \item \textbf{Center manifold:} The spectral gap guarantees a finite-dimensional center manifold of dimension 2 \cite{mielke1986} (for the slow $(N,E)$ modes) for the full system. The fast modes $\mathbf{n}$ (including waste, product, and macroeconomic variables) are exponentially attracted to this manifold. The memory kernel and the slow variables $(A, C)$ are treated as parameters on the timescale of the fast subsystem.
    \item \textbf{Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction:} Define $G(x, \tau, \varepsilon, A, C) = \mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon,\tau,A,C}(x) - x$. At $(x^*, \tau_0, 0, A_0, C_0)$, $G = 0$ and $D_x G$ has a one-dimensional kernel. By the Lyapunov-Schmidt decomposition, the equation $G = 0$ is equivalent to a scalar equation $\Phi(u, \tau, \varepsilon, A, C) = 0$, where $u$ is the coordinate along the kernel direction.
    \item \textbf{Fold condition:} The transversality condition ensures $\partial_\tau \Phi \neq 0$. The quadratic tangency ensures $\partial_{uu} \Phi \neq 0$. Since $\Phi$ is $C^1$ in $(\tau, \varepsilon, A, C)$, the Implicit Function Theorem gives a unique solution curve $u^*(\tau, \varepsilon, A, C)$ for the fixed point. The fold occurs when $\partial_u \Phi = 0$. Solving the augmented system $\Phi = 0, \partial_u \Phi = 0$ yields a unique curve $\tau^*(\varepsilon, A, C)$ by the same theorem, as the Jacobian of the augmented system is invertible.
    \item \textbf{Adiabatic slow drift:} For $\varepsilon > 0$ and $\dot{A} = -R(N)$, $\dot{C} = \mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^2)$, the slow drift of $(A, C)$ moves the fold location adiabatically. After the center-manifold reduction of step~2 the slow subsystem is finite-dimensional, so the classical Fenichel theory \cite{fenichel1971,fenichel1979} applies to the reduced system: the slow manifold and its bifurcation persist, the fold curve $\tau^*(\varepsilon, A, C)$ remains transverse, and the system follows it as $(A, C)$ evolve. A direct statement for the unreduced DDE is supplied by the recent infinite-dimensional Fenichel-type theorems for slow-fast evolution equations \cite{hummelkuehn2022,kuehnsulzbach2024}, whose assumptions (spectral gap, compact memory kernel, semigroup on $\mathcal{C}=C([-\max(\tau,\tau_m),0],\mathbb R^{m+4})$) this system must satisfy; that verification is the open item recorded in Section~H.6.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{H.5 Numerical Validation}
\paragraph{Scope caveat on the frozen-$A$ treatment below.} The continuation reported in this subsection treats the abiotic reservoir $A$ as a large, effectively frozen constant. Section~\ref{sec:four-state-core} establishes, from the model's baseline parameters and independently corroborated against primary biogeochemical literature (deep-ocean ventilation ages, the CENTURY soil model), that the active abiotic pool $A^{\text{act}}$ relaxes on a timescale (${\sim}10^3$ years) comparable to or slower than the resource stock itself and \emph{cannot} be formally frozen or eliminated as a fast auxiliary variable at these parameters --- it must instead be retained as a genuine fourth dynamical state. The frozen-$A$ numerical continuation below therefore inherits the same limitation the three-state core is subject to, and the ``$A$ varied over two orders of magnitude'' robustness check reported below should be read as a check of formal sensitivity under the frozen-$A$ assumption specifically, not as independent confirmation that persistence holds once $A^{\text{act}}$ is correctly promoted to a state variable. Repeating this appendix's continuation on the four-state core of Section~\ref{sec:four-state-core} instead of the frozen-$A$ approximation is a further, currently unaddressed item for this conjecture's numerical support, additional to the open items already listed in Section~H.6 below. We performed systematic numerical continuation of the full augmented system (with the abiotic reservoir $A$ treated as a large constant and the institutional integrity $C$ fixed at a nominal value), tracking the periodic orbit itself in $\tau$ rather than restarting from a fixed initial condition at each step, for the reasons discussed in Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}. The continuation reveals the same five-regime topology as the three-state core:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item $\tau < \tau_-$: Equilibrium unstable; large-amplitude collapse cycle is the sole attractor.
    \item $\tau_- < \tau < \tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$: Equilibrium locally stable, but the collapse cycle persists as a coexisting attractor (narrow bistable window).
    \item $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}} < \tau < \tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$: Wide monostable interval; equilibrium is the sole attractor.
    \item $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}} < \tau < \tau_+$: Equilibrium still locally stable, but the collapse cycle has reappeared as a coexisting attractor (narrow bistable window, mirroring regime 2).
    \item $\tau > \tau_+$: Equilibrium destabilises; collapse cycle is the sole attractor.
\end{enumerate}
Each SNPO threshold shifts by less than $5\%$ when $A$ is varied over two orders of magnitude (provided $A \gg N$), confirming the structural robustness predicted by the conjecture. The unstable limit cycle conjectured to be born at each SNPO would, if confirmed, act as the basin boundary separating the two coexisting attractors within that regime's bistable window.

\subsection{H.6 Open Problem and Caveat}
A rigorous proof of this conjecture requires geometric singular perturbation theory (GSPT; Fenichel theory and Melnikov-type arguments) for the infinite-dimensional setting of delay-differential equations with multiple, non-commensurate \emph{fixed} delays (\(\tau\) and \(\tau_m\), as used throughout this manuscript; Section~\ref{sec:variant-registry}). The state of the underlying theory is more favourable than a first reading might suggest, and the local part of the argument does not require new mathematics at all. After the center-manifold reduction of step~H.4.2 --- standard and rigorous for fixed-delay DDEs \cite{hale1993,diekmann1995,faria1995,mielke1986} --- the reduced system is finite-dimensional, and the persistence of the transverse fold is a Lyapunov--Schmidt argument on the Poincar\'e map (steps H.4.1--H.4.4); rigorous analytical instances of saddle-node bifurcations of periodic orbits in DDEs have been established for specific classes \cite{beretka2020}, and the same subcritical-Hopf/SNPO/homoclinic architecture as the present core has been reproduced, including its unstable periodic orbits, by center-manifold reduction of a delayed ENSO model \cite{chekrounliu2024}. A direct infinite-dimensional Fenichel theory has also recently become available: slow manifolds and slow-flow approximation for infinite-dimensional fast-slow evolution equations are established by Hummel and Kuehn \cite{hummelkuehn2022} and extended to fast-reaction systems by Kuehn and Sulzbach \cite{kuehnsulzbach2024}, and the specific class of DDE slow manifolds and canard explosions is developed by Krupa and Touboul \cite{krupatouboul2016}. The genuinely open step for the present system is therefore not the development of new theory but the verification of these theorems' assumptions --- the spectral gap and normal hyperbolicity of Appendix~H, the compactness of the memory kernel, and the semigroup structure on \(\mathcal{C}=C([-\max(\tau,\tau_m),0],\mathbb R^{m+4})\) --- for the vector-coupled, two-fixed-delay augmented system, together with the infinite-time adiabatic tracking of the fold as \((A,C)\) drift (step~H.4.5). Note, finally, that state-dependent delays are the \emph{harder}, not the easier, setting: even center-manifold reduction for state-dependent-delay equations is incomplete at the smoothness needed for bifurcation analysis \cite{hartung2006}, so the fixed-delay setting used here is the developed one, contrary to what is sometimes assumed. We therefore present the SNPO persistence as a \textbf{structurally stable conjecture}, verified numerically and formally plausible, with the local part essentially standard and the remaining gap being the assumption-verification and adiabatic-tracking steps above.

\section{Appendix I: Formalising Survival --- Parametric Attractor Containment via Viability Theory}
\label{sec:viability}
The policy list above (Section~\ref{sec:policy-implications}) is qualitative. This section gives it a precise mathematical form by mapping the framework onto \textbf{viability theory} \cite{aubin1991}, which has an established track record in fisheries and ecosystem-based management \cite{cury2005}: rather than seeking a single optimal trajectory, viability theory asks which states admit \emph{some} governance strategy that keeps the system within prescribed safety constraints indefinitely. This framing fits the vector diagnostics of Section~\ref{sec:depletion-horizon} more naturally than optimal control does, since the Abiotic Depletion Horizons and Depletion Stress Indices are already constraint thresholds, not a single scalar objective to be maximised.

\paragraph{Why real-time control is the wrong abstraction here.} Classical viability theory (and standard bioeconomic optimal control more generally) assumes a controller that steers the state continuously in response to its evolution, typically by treating harvest or effort as a directly-set control input. That abstraction does not fit the reduced core's institutional-failure regime ($\chi=1$, Section~\ref{sec:generalized-effort}), which is what drives the Hopf/SNPO analysis of Sections~\ref{sec:core-model}--\ref{sec:numerical-continuation-corrected}: in this regime, effort $E(t)$ is not a lever an institution sets directly, but an endogenous state variable governed by its own delay-differential equation, driven by the delayed, memory-filtered deficit $Z(t-\tau)$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected}). What an institution can actually choose are the \textbf{structural parameters} that shape this equation, not the trajectory of $E(t)$ itself. We therefore do not treat this as a real-time steering problem; we ask instead which \emph{fixed} institutional architectures give rise to an autonomous dynamics whose long-run behaviour stays inside the safe operating region.

\paragraph{Structural parameters and the safe operating set.} Let the state vector be $\mathbf x=(N,\mathbf A^{\text{act}},\mathbf A^{\text{geo}},Z,E)^\top$ (the reduced core's state, together with the abiotic pools of Section~\ref{sec:abiotic} where the two-compartment closure is in view). The governance levers available under institutional failure are the parameters that already appear explicitly in Eq.~\eqref{eq:effort-core-corrected} and Eq.~\eqref{eq:char-eq-corrected}:
\begin{equation}
\boldsymbol\theta = (\tau, E_{\max}, \delta_0)^\top,
\label{eq:viability-theta}
\end{equation}
the institutional delay, the maximum mobilisable effort capacity, and the baseline panic rate — deliberately restricted to parameters the model's equations already contain, rather than introducing new, uncalibrated control channels. (A hard extraction quota is a natural further lever, mentioned qualitatively in Section~\ref{sec:policy-implications}'s policy list; formalising it would require adding an explicit cap to Eq.~\eqref{eq:stock-core} itself, e.g.\ replacing $qEN$ with $\min(qEN,Q^{\text{cap}})$, which we leave to future work rather than presenting as already part of the model's dynamics.)

Using the vector diagnostics of Section~\ref{sec:depletion-horizon}, define the \textbf{Desirable Set}
\begin{equation}
K = \left\{ \mathbf x \;\middle|\; \mathcal H^{\bullet}_{i,c}(\mathbf x) \ge \mathcal H_{\text{crit}} \ \text{and}\ \Sigma^{\bullet}_{i,c}(\mathbf x) \ge \Sigma_{\text{crit}} \ \ \forall (i,c,\bullet)\in\mathcal D_{\text{active}}(\mathbf x) \right\},
\label{eq:viability-K}
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal D_{\text{active}}(\mathbf x)$ restricts the condition to the indices for which each horizon is actually defined: $\mathcal H^{\text{geo}}_{i,c}$ requires an active mining channel ($C^A_{i,c}>0$) and $\mathcal H^{\text{act}}_{i,c}$ is undefined, not infinite, once the biological stock is functionally extinct ($N_{i,c}\le\epsilon_{i,c}N_{i,c}(0)$, Section~\ref{sec:depletion-horizon}); omitting this restriction would make Eq.~\eqref{eq:viability-K} ill-posed exactly where the diagnostic itself is silent by construction.

\paragraph{Parametric attractor containment.} For a fixed $\boldsymbol\theta$, let $\mathcal A_{\boldsymbol\theta}$ denote the global attractor(s) the autonomous dynamics settle onto (the interior equilibrium, the large-amplitude cycle, or both, depending on where $\tau$ falls relative to $\tau_-,\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}},\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}},\tau_+$; Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}). We define the \textbf{safe parametric regime}
\begin{equation}
\Theta_{\text{safe}} = \left\{ \boldsymbol\theta\in\Theta \;\middle|\; \mathcal A_{\boldsymbol\theta}\subset\operatorname{int}(K) \right\}.
\label{eq:viability-safe}
\end{equation}
This is deliberately \emph{not} the same condition as the wide monostable interval $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}<\tau<\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ identified in Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}: that window establishes only that the quiet equilibrium is the \emph{sole} attractor, i.e.\ that $\mathcal A_{\boldsymbol\theta}$ is a single point rather than a coexisting pair. It says nothing about whether that equilibrium's coordinates $(N^*,Z^*,E^*)$ actually satisfy Eq.~\eqref{eq:viability-K}. The two conditions are independent and must be checked separately:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Monostability} (established in Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified} by the Hopf/SNPO analysis): $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}<\tau<\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$ guarantees $\mathcal A_{\boldsymbol\theta}$ is the single equilibrium point, not that it is safe.
\item \textbf{Geometric viability} (not established by the bifurcation analysis, and specific to each parameterisation): $\mathcal A_{\boldsymbol\theta}\subset\operatorname{int}(K)$, i.e.\ the equilibrium itself clears the depletion-horizon and stress-index thresholds.
\end{enumerate}
Only the conjunction of both conditions places $\boldsymbol\theta$ in $\Theta_{\text{safe}}$. At the Candidate A baseline, the equilibrium is a lightly-exploited state, $N^*/K\approx0.90$ (Section~\ref{sec:parameter-audit}), which is evidently interior to any reasonable $K$; this particular baseline therefore satisfies condition 2 as well as condition 1, but this is a fact about Candidate A's specific parameter values, not a consequence of monostability itself, and would need to be re-checked for any other calibration.

\paragraph{Basin membership, not just attractor safety.} Attractor containment is still insufficient on its own within either narrow bistable window flanking $\tau_{\text{SNPO,L}}$ or $\tau_{\text{SNPO,R}}$, where the quiet equilibrium and the large-amplitude cycle coexist (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}): even if the equilibrium itself lies in $\operatorname{int}(K)$, the system is only actually safe if its current state lies in that equilibrium's basin of attraction, $\mathbf x_0\in\mathcal B(\mathcal A_{\boldsymbol\theta})$, rather than in the basin of the coexisting collapse cycle. This is exactly the basin-dependent capture finding of Section~\ref{sec:basin-capture}: a large stock with correspondingly low effort is the trajectory captured by the boom-bust cycle, not the one that appears intuitively ``healthiest.'' This formalises the productivity illusion (Section~\ref{sec:introduction}) in the present vocabulary: a scalar projection of $\mathbf x_0$ (e.g.\ aggregate biomass) can appear to sit safely inside a scalar approximation of $K$, while the true vector state has already left $\mathcal B(\mathcal A_{\boldsymbol\theta})$ for the safe attractor's competitor. The Abiotic Depletion Horizons and Depletion Stress Indices (Section~\ref{sec:depletion-horizon}) function as leading-order, component-resolved proxies for proximity to $\partial K$; monitoring them is a way of tracking distance to the edge of the desirable set, not a claim that they are themselves the gradient of a known potential.

\paragraph{Scope and status of this formalisation.} We present this as a precise vocabulary for the policy list of Section~\ref{sec:policy-implications}, not as a new quantitative result: $\Theta_{\text{safe}}$ and $\mathcal B(\mathcal A_{\boldsymbol\theta})$ have not been computed for this manuscript's parameter sets, and doing so for the full augmented system (rather than the reduced core alone) would inherit the same open persistence question already flagged for the SNPO folds themselves (Appendix H). The formalisation is offered to make the qualitative claims of Section~\ref{sec:policy-implications} — that institutional design should target the shape of the attractor structure rather than real-time extraction rates, and that basin membership matters independently of attractor safety — precise enough to be checked, extended, or falsified in future work, in the same spirit as the falsifiability programme of Section~\ref{sec:falsifiable-predictions} above.



\section{Appendix J: A Consolidated Extension Scaffold: Stage Structure, Spatial Variance, and Thermodynamic Gating}
\label{sec:scaffold}

\paragraph{Scope.} This appendix analyses a proposed consolidation of four extensions treated separately in the main text --- the two-channel liquidation split (Section~\ref{sec:two-channel}), the donor-limited abiotic draw (Section~\ref{sec:abiotic}), the stage-structured maturation delay (structural-robustness item, Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}), and the thermodynamic tether (Section~\ref{sec:thermodynamic-tether}) --- together with the spatial-variance correction of Section~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity} and the primitive-flux ontology of Section~\ref{sec:unified-ontology}. Two purposes motivate the analysis. First, it supplies a mass-consistent (compartmental rather than lag-based) form of the maturation-delay item flagged as future work in Section~\ref{sec:limitations-further-work}: a bare delay $N(t-g)$ admits adult biomass at time $t$ without tracking the juvenile draw on the abiotic pool during $[t-g,t]$, which the primitive-flux ledger of Section~\ref{sec:unified-ontology} does not permit; an explicit juvenile compartment resolves this. Second, it tests whether the combined architecture can reproduce the delay-amplified instability of the verified cores. The analysis is exploratory: the components are verified individually, but the combined system is not a validated predictive model, and no quantitative claim about any real system is made from it.

\subsection{J.1 The scaffold and its verified components}

The scaffold has nine states: the mass-conserved block $(\bar X_A,\,X_J,\,P,\,U,\,A,\,G)$, the spatial variance $V_N$, the institutional memory $Z$, the industrial exergy/capital stock $K_C$, and the effort $E$. With $\psi$ the liquidation split of Section~\ref{sec:two-channel} --- so that $C_{\mathrm{stock}}=\psi qE\bar X_A$ removes adult biomass while $C_{\mathrm{recruit}}=(1-\psi)qE\bar X_A$ suppresses birth without routing mass --- the governing equations are
\begin{align}
\dot{\bar X}_A &= \tfrac{1}{g}X_J - d_A\bar X_A - \psi qE\bar X_A, \label{eq:scaf-XA}\\
\dot X_J &= g_B(\bar X_A,A,V_N,E) - \bigl(\tfrac{1}{g}+d_J\bigr)X_J, \label{eq:scaf-XJ}\\
\dot P &= (1-\alpha)\psi qE\bar X_A - \lambda_P P, \label{eq:scaf-P}\\
\dot U &= d_A\bar X_A + d_J X_J + \alpha\psi qE\bar X_A + \lambda_P P - \gamma_U U, \label{eq:scaf-U}\\
\dot A &= -g_B(\bar X_A,A,V_N,E) + \gamma_U U + \omega_A\bigl(A^{\mathrm{eq}}-A\bigr), \label{eq:scaf-A}\\
\dot G &= -\omega_A\bigl(A^{\mathrm{eq}}-A\bigr), \label{eq:scaf-G}
\end{align}
with the donor-limited, suppression-gated birth
\begin{equation}
g_B = \max\Bigl(0,\ \bigl[P_0\bar X_A\,e^{-\bar X_A/N_c} + \Phi_{\mathrm{var}}(\bar X_A,V_N)\bigr]\tfrac{A}{A+A_0} - (1-\psi)qE\bar X_A\Bigr),
\label{eq:scaf-gB}
\end{equation}
the Ricker spatial-variance penalty
\begin{equation}
\Phi_{\mathrm{var}}(\bar X_A,V_N) = \frac{1}{2}\frac{P_0}{N_c}\,e^{-\bar X_A/N_c}\Bigl(\frac{\bar X_A}{N_c}-2\Bigr)V_N,
\label{eq:scaf-var}
\end{equation}
and the non-conserved states
\begin{align}
\dot V_N &= 2\Bigl(P_0\,e^{-\bar X_A/N_c}\bigl(1-\bar X_A/N_c\bigr) - d_A - \psi qE\Bigr)V_N - \delta_D V_N - 2q\bar X_A\,\mathrm{Cov}(E,\bar X_A),
\label{eq:scaf-V}\\
\dot Z &= \frac{\ell - Z}{\tau_m},\quad \ell = -\dot{\bar X}_A,
\label{eq:scaf-Z}\\
\dot K_C &= \varsigma Q - K_C(\delta_K + c_E E),
\label{eq:scaf-K}\\
\dot E &= \Bigl(1-\frac{E}{E_{\max}}\Bigr)\bigl(1-e^{-K_C/K_0}\bigr)\Bigl[\eta E\frac{Z(t-\tau)}{\Delta_{\mathrm{ref}}} + \delta_0 - \eta\frac{E^2}{E_{\max}}\Bigr] - \mu_E E.
\label{eq:scaf-E}
\end{align}

The following properties are verified directly. (i) \textbf{Mass closure.} $\frac{d}{dt}\bigl(\bar X_A + X_J + P + U + A + G\bigr) = 0$ identically: $C_{\mathrm{recruit}}$ transfers no mass (it only suppresses birth), so un-laid recruits remain in the abiotic pool, consistent with the mass-conservation argument of Section~\ref{sec:two-channel}. (ii) \textbf{Forward invariance.} The non-negativity of $\bar X_A$, $X_J$, $U$, $A$ and $0\le E\le E_{\max}$ hold at the respective boundaries (extraction, mortality and fecundity all vanish with their donors; the effort boundaries are strictly inward by construction). (iii) \textbf{Ricker moment closure.} The penalty \eqref{eq:scaf-var} is the exact second-order Taylor coefficient $\tfrac12 R''(\bar X_A)$ for $R(X)=P_0Xe^{-X/N_c}$, and is negative for $\bar X_A<2N_c$ --- the native Ricker analogue of the logistic identity $E[S(N)]=S(\bar N)-\frac{r}{K}\mathrm{Var}(N)$ of Section~\ref{sec:spatial-heterogeneity}, which is exact only for the quadratic logistic. (iv) \textbf{Closed-form interior equilibrium of the mass block.} At $Z^*=V_N^*=0$, \begin{equation}
\bar X_A^* = N_c\ln\!\Bigl(\frac{P_0\,A^{\mathrm{eq}}/(A^{\mathrm{eq}}+A_0)}{\Theta(E^*)}\Bigr),\qquad \Theta(E)=d_A(1+gd_J)+qE(1+\psi g d_J),
\label{eq:scaf-XAstar}
\end{equation}
with survival condition $P_0\,A^{\mathrm{eq}}/(A^{\mathrm{eq}}+A_0)>\Theta(E^*)$; the drag term $\Theta$ encodes the ``eat eggs versus kill hens'' distinction: harvesting adults ($\psi=1$) adds the juvenile-mortality drag $qE\,gd_J$ relative to suppressing recruitment ($\psi=0$). (v) \textbf{Thermodynamic gating of the feedback.} Linearising the effort law \eqref{eq:scaf-E} at the interior equilibrium gives the delay-coupling and exergy-damping coefficients
\begin{equation}
C_Z = h_0\,g_0\,\frac{\eta E^*}{\Delta_{\mathrm{ref}}},\qquad
C_K = \frac{\mu_E E^*}{K_0}\frac{1-g_0}{g_0},\qquad
h_0 = 1-\frac{E^*}{E_{\max}},\quad g_0 = 1-e^{-K_C^*/K_0},
\label{eq:scaf-C}
\end{equation}
so the delayed autocatalysis is throttled linearly by the exergy gate $g_0$, while the instantaneous exergy-damping channel $C_K$ diverges as $g_0\to0$; the modulus condition of the resulting characteristic equation consequently fails at sufficiently low $g_0$, the precise threshold depending on the full parameter set.

\subsection{J.2 Numerical analysis}

Direct evaluation of the full characteristic quasi-polynomial (modulus and phase conditions, with the coupled equilibrium solved at each parameter point) yields the following. (i) \textbf{Existence.} Delay-induced Hopf bifurcations exist for a subset of parameter space, verified to machine precision at the crossing, with oscillation periods of order $10^3$--$10^4$ time units. (ii) \textbf{Structure.} All verified crossings examined are \emph{stabilizing} ($d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\lambda/d\tau<0$ at the crossing); no clean two-crossing $\tau_-/\tau_+$ bistable-window structure was found, in contrast to the verified cores (Section~\ref{sec:hopf-verified}). (iii) \textbf{Anchoring.} Anchored to life-history classes (anchovy $g\approx1$, sprat $g\approx2$, cod $g\approx5$; productivity set to satisfy the survival condition), the scaffold is delay-independently stable at the manuscript's default economic parameter ranges; a genuine cod-class Hopf appears only under elevated economic forcing ($\eta=5$, $\varsigma=0.8$, $K_0=0.03$, $q=0.01$), with $\tau^*\approx43$ and period $\approx263$ time units.

\subsection{J.3 Status and relation to the manuscript}

The scaffold is presented as an exploratory extension, not as a replacement for the verified cores and not as a unification of the registered variants. In particular, the expectation that the cores emerge as asymptotic limits of the scaffold is \emph{not} established: the verified crossings are stabilizing-only, unlike the cores' $\tau_-$ plus $\tau_+$ pair, and the effort laws differ (a gated law with an explicit juvenile cascade and an institutional-entropy term, versus the shifted-softplus form) in ways that do not obviously limit out. Its value is threefold: (a) it supplies the mass-consistent juvenile-compartment form required by the maturation-delay future-work item; (b) it demonstrates that the combination of stage structure, spatial variance, abiotic pool, and thermodynamic gating can reproduce the delay-amplified phenomenon --- but only under elevated forcing, reinforcing the manuscript's finding that the instability is marginal in realistic parameter regions; and (c) its century-scale periods are consistent with the record-length argument of the empirical hunt (Section~\ref{sec:empirical-hunt}). Open items: the dependence of the critical gate factor on the full parameter set (it is not a universal constant), the classification of crossing direction across parameter space, and calibrated anchoring with uncertainty propagation.
